Code copies at 0x804A1000-0x804A1298 (bigspace +0x3EB00). Generator `ffny_tools/gxfast.py IN OUT [--revert]`. Test build `gxfast test build/start.dol` (md5 09c84e70758de6e1582314b2d96551c9) = rollback test build + gxfast. Lab: Babylon 39.8 s -> 29.3 s per 1800 frames (Dolphin 2609 single core, Null video); fighter select pixel-identical. Not yet tested by Robert.

  - Data in `ffny_tools/`: `ffny_allmoves.ord/.orl/.mnl/.json` (move library), `ffny_vnames.json` / `ffny_vnames_base.json` (Vendetta clip names from its Start.dol), `ffny_movenames.json` (Robert's names for "NO NAME" moves), `ffny_animnotes.json` (translated camera.xml notes + Robert's viewer names), `ffny_retail.json`, `refpack.py`, `xa.c`.
  - **Slot numbers in every tool are those printed by `charstats wwaz` (1-129).**
  - Separate from the tools: the FFNY animation viewer (`ffny_anim_viewer.html`), the Vendetta clip viewer and the Snowman 2 pose editor (artifacts), script zips for the Vendetta ports (`vendetta_port_scripts.zip`, `dmx_port_scripts.zip`, `dmx_cinematic_port_scripts_v3.zip`; see the Vendetta sections).
- **Building the disc image (important):** Robert extracts the ISO with GCRebuilder into `modded_root`, edits files, and rebuilds `modded.iso`. **GCRebuilder stores every file's length when the root folder is OPENED and writes the file table from those lengths at Rebuild** (it copies each file to its real end), so a file that grew after opening is cut off for the game and its extra bytes overwrite the next file. Rule: after changing files, close and re-open the root (or restart GCRebuilder) before Rebuild; check with `python ffny.py isocheck modded.iso --compare modded_root` (must report 0 different). Every "screens.viv crash" of 5-6 October was this.
- **Archives:** use `ffny viv` (`put` appends data and re-points entries, works with or without free directory room; `add` rebuilds BIGF archives like retail). BigGUI once dropped the `L231` tag, renamed entries and reordered data in music0.viv; another time (wres_all.viv) it worked. All game archives are found by a linear case-insensitive name search (`FUN_80245e6c` stricmp, loop 0x802461E8), so entry order does not matter. Archive types (`FUN_80245f08`): 0xC0FB, "BIGF", "BIG" + digit ("BIG4" = BIGF layout with 4-byte fields).
- **DOL:** main text file offset = address − 0x80003000 (0x800034A0 → 0x4A0); data sections from the header (file offsets at 0x00, addresses at 0x48, sizes at 0x90). The extra code area ("cave") = text section 2, see "Patches".
- **Writing patch code in C (16 October 2026):** `python -m pip install ziglang`, then `python -m ziglang cc -target powerpc-freestanding-eabihf -mcpu=750 -Os -fno-builtin -ffreestanding -fno-jump-tables -c x.c`, link with `python -m ziglang ld.lld -T script.ld` (text at a fixed bigspace address, bss NOLOAD in bigspace) and `objcopy -O binary -j .text`. Game functions are called through absolute function pointers; no r2 / r13 small data is generated; cr2 / cr3 are saved when used. (`eabi` without `hf` is soft-float and needs __addsf3 etc.) Emulating game code with unicorn needs the Gekko quantized load / store `psq_l / psq_st` (opcodes 56 / 57 / 60 / 61) done by hand in the code hook: GQR2 = u8, GQR3 = u16, GQR4 = s8, GQR5 = s16, scale 0 (set at 0x80112144); `ffny_tools/movename/emu.py` does it. **Ghidra 11.1.2 headless** works here (Java 21 present; download from GitHub; DOL → ELF by `dol2elf.py`, language `PowerPC:BE:32:default`, r2 / r13 set in a pre-script; full analysis of the retail DOL takes ~10 minutes, decompiling a function ~10 s) — much faster than reading disassembly.
- **Verification here:** keystone (assembler) + unicorn (emulator; MSR 0x2000 for floating point) with the DOL sections loaded. Keystone encodes `b` / `bl` to absolute far targets wrongly: encode them by hand (0x48000000 | ((target − pc) & 0x03FFFFFC), +1 for bl). Run long jobs detached (`setsid nohup`). Never print an unfiltered string / pointer scan of the DOL.

## djv2.sod (SODB, big-endian) – menu and front-end data

- **Header:** "SODB", version, +0x0C SKMA schema, +0x10 DATA directory, +0x14 STRT (strings, sorted by hash). String fields hold an index into STRT. **Hash:** djb2 over the string including the NUL. **Schema key** = djb2(category) XOR djb2(field) → [word index, type (1 string, 2 integer)].
- **Category layout:** at A (from DATA): [hash][pointer to index node][pointer][record count]; index node [1][1][hash][pointer to B]; B: [hash][count][1][name hashes, sorted][record offsets]; then the records. **Record number = position in the sorted name-hash index**; a reference = category number << 24 | record number (`FUN_800ce514`, which returns nothing for a number past the count). Category numbers: Song 0, Character 7, rewards 8, Venue 23. The Character category has two more indexes: by word 13 (field hash 0x0AD29D29) and by **FE_Id** (0xFCDBA74E; offsets in FE_Id order, no key array).
- **Character records** (26 u32 words, 0x68 bytes): 0 internal name (key), 2 Charisma, 5 shown name, 6 "DefaultEntity", 8 **FE_Id**, 9 **menu style list bit mask**, 10 **fighter number = fighter record index + 1**, 12 Health, 13 main-entry flag (no effect found), 14 **model name** (`V2IP_002A`), 16 Power_Lower, 17 Power_Upper, 18 **menu main style bit**, 21 **taunt voice id**, 22 Speed, 24 **roster category** (1 / 2 / 4 / 8: decides the select-screen block), 25 Toughness. Menu style bits: KB 1, SF 2, MA 4, WR 8, SB 16. **These stats and styles are only shown on the select screen (tested);** in-fight values come from WPAR (menu ≈ fight × 5). 12 retail fighters show other styles than they fight with.
  - Category 1 = Crow, NORE, Omar, Sticky (+2), Ludacris, Doc, D-Mob (+2, 3), Xzibit, Blaze (+2, 3), Crack, Ice-T, Magic; 2 = the other music artists / celebrities; 4 = Bo, Cruz, Masa, Meca, Nyne, Rome, Solo, Teck, Chiang, Snowman, Baby Chris, Pockets, Bless, Cindy, Dan G, House, Manny, Baxter, Skull, Trick, Santos, Shaniqua; 8 = Starks, Shadowy Figure, Jervis, Stingray, Rollins, Jacob, Lauren, E3 Hero.
- **The game identifies the chosen fighter by FE_Id** (first match in the FE_Id index; tested with two records sharing one). Model = word 14, fight data = word 10.
- **Blazin' category** (hash 0x47D305FD): [name, id, ?, ?, value, animation "blazin_NN"]; value = the WPAR Blazin' byte.
- **Song category** (hash 0x0E1BB13C): see "Music".
- **Rewards** (category 0x51060F0D, 175 records `<venue>_<fighter>_Bounty`, `Song_<name>`, `Ship_*`, `PlayerDefault`, **`GlobalItems`** = default unlocks awarded at boot): [name, number, pointer, number, item count, pointers..., per item 12 bytes: 0, reference, 0x1F]. 11 characters are in no reward (bought in the shop; not traced).

## main_all.dat (little-endian chunks: 4-char tag, u32 size, payload)

Chunk order: WAZA, FZUI, NAME (table 1); WAZA, FZUI, NAME (table 2); then 141 pairs WPAR + WWAZ. Fighter record index 0-140: **0-85 roster, 86-140 created-fighter style templates** (`GameCoreFightStyleToID`). Animation bank = `wres%03d` with index + 1. `FUN_801f9b50` builds the pointer table 0x803F6020 + index × 16 (byte-swaps WWAZ); fighter +0x2C = record index.

### WPAR (64 bytes, bits MSB first; decoded by `FUN_801faea8` into fighter +0xAE4)

| Bits | Content |
|---|---|
| 3 / 3 / 3 | styles main / hybrid 1 / hybrid 2 (0 SF, 1 KB, 2 SB, 3 WR, 4 MA, 7 none) |
| 9-13 | **stance idle** (+0xAF0): base00 A101 + n (17 = A119) |
| 14-18 | **walk set** (+0xAF4): base00 A301 + 5n .. +2 (middle = backward walk); roster = stance number except 15 → 13, 16 → 14 |
| 19-20 | **run type** (+0xAF8): B101/B201/B301 + n; 0 everywhere in retail; 1 = the unused jog B102/B202/B302 (**tested**), 2 = the women's set |
| 21-22 | **grapple type** (+0xAFC): 0 SF/KB, 1 SB/MA, 2 WR (hard-grab hold D201 / D211 / D221, grab attempt D101 + 2 × type) |
| 23-31 | unknown (3-bit groups) |
| 5 × 4 at bit 32 / 52 | limb attack / limb defence (head, body, arm, leg, 5th) → +0xB0C / +0xB20; all 5 in retail |
| from bit 72: 7 × 4 | **in-fight stats (tested):** Upper, Lower, Speed, Toughness, Health, (unused), Charisma; 0-15 → +0xB34..+0xB4C |
| 47 × 6 from bit 100 | **parry table** (identity = Parry neutral) |
| 8 × 3, 3 × 7, 3 × 7 | unknown |
| bytes 56-59 | **Blazin' per right-stick direction** right, left, up, down (tested); move = table-2 0x8E + value (0-81) |
| bytes 60-61 | **grapple class** → +0xBC8: 0x08 main SF/KB, 0x10 SB/MA, 0x20 WR (no exception in the roster); 0x01 men, **0x02 women** (always A119 / A378 set) |
| bytes 62-63 (big-endian) | **style mask** → +0xBCA: main 1 << s, hybrid 1 << (s + 5), second hybrid 1 << (s + 10). Uses: any MA → second environment-grapple list; main SF/KB → other parry/reversal pair; main SF/KB/MA at grab start / hold; any WR blocks the hold shove with +0x91A & 0x80; main SB/WR allows the hard-grab turn-around |

- Stances 3 (A104, arms far forward) and 10 (A111; walk set A351-A353 is a T-pose) are unused. `grappleclass stance` / `run` set them.
- **Parry table sets:** 0, 1, 5, 6, 7, 8 → 35-40 = **Parry+** (Doc, Crazy Legs, Crow, Flava Flav, Havoc, Carmen, Bo, Pockets, Chiang); → 41-46 = **Parry−** (Sean Paul, WC, Joe Budden, Bless, Lil Kim, Solo, Meca, E3 Hero); others neutral (tested).
- **Created fighters** get template stats through `FUN_80013d0c` (floor(A/5) × 1.275 + B/2.2222 + 0.5 from the save profile), overriding WPAR.

### WWAZ (130 slots × 6 bytes: u16 move ID (bits 0-11; **bit 15 = can KO at Danger, tested**), u16 local animation index, u16 global motion ID)

Looked up by `FUN_8021d4a4`. Slot map (tested unless "?"):

| Slots | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 1-5 / 6-10 | light grapple P / K: neutral, right, left, up, down (direction bits 0x2→1, 0x8→2, 0x1→3, 0x4→4) |
| 11-15 / 16-20 | hard grapple P / K, same direction order (Robert) |
| 11-20 | also **every clinch**, from light and hard grabs |
| 21-25 / 26-30 | finishers / SF grapple strikes |
| 31-34, 38-41 | Blazin' slots (WPAR bytes 56-59) |
| 35 / 36 / 37 | rear light grapple / rear hard grapple P / rear hard grapple K (tested) |
| 42-45 | whip grapples (random one of four; each uses `__K`, `__A`, `__B`) |
| 46 / 47 | running grapple front without / **with L1** (tested); 48 / 49 back (?) |
| 50-51 / 52-53 | turnbuckle grapples / Wrestling rope hot spot (which of 52 / 53 depends on grapple strength, Robert) |
| 54-56, 71-73 | light P / light K neutral, forward, back |
| 57-70, 74-87 | **light chain hits 2-4, see "Light-strike chains" below** (not style-exclusive) |
| 88-90 / 91-93 | hard P / hard K neutral, forward, back |
| 94-95 | running P / K |
| 98 / 99 | whip strikes (hard / light button) |
| 100-101 / 103-104 | ground strike P / K (standing); running ground strike P / K (Robert) |
| 106-109 | wake-up P / K reversed, P / K toward the opponent |
| 110-115 | turnbuckle dives (113-115 use five animations AA1-AA5) |
| 116 / 117 | Subway: dive from the platform onto the tracks, P / K (Robert) |
| 118 / 119 | rope rebound / wall rebound (MA only) |
| 120-124 | taunts (`_AP` animations, Robert) |

Style-exclusive: 52-53 WR, 119 MA. (The old note "63-70 SF, 80-87 KB only" is wrong: e.g. Havoc (MA), Crow, Bless, Slick Rick (SB), Omar (SF) have 80-87 filled; the chain code has no style check.)

**Light-strike chains (15 October, read from code `FUN_8022e828` / `FUN_8022eb40`; identical to combs.xml's list):** chain start 54 / 55 / 56 = light P neutral / forward / back, 71 / 72 / 73 = light K (`0x9E0 & 0x800` = P). Up to 4 hits (+0x40C). Same button all the way → table 0x802E48E0 (hits 2, 3, 4): P neutral 57, 59, 63; P forward 74, 78, 86; P back 57, 76, 84; K neutral 75, 79, 87; K forward 58, 60, 64; K back 75, 62, 66. Any mix of P and K → slot = 55 (P start) or 72 (K start) + pattern, pattern = first hit as 1 then one bit per later press (0 P, 1 K): P start: PK 58; PPK 60, PKP 61, PKK 62; PPPK 64, PPKP 65, PPKK 66, PKPP 67, PKPK 68, PKKP 69, PKKK 70. K start: KP 74; KPP 76, KPK 77, KKP 78; KPPP 80, KPPK 81, KPKP 82, KPKK 83, KKPP 84, KKPK 85, KKKP 86. An empty slot ends the chain. **Reaction and cancels per chain hit (15 October, read from code):** a light hit (attacker state 0x45) takes its class from combo table 0x802E4C0C level = attacker +0x40C − 1 (hit 1-4 → levels 0-3; rows = victim health band, columns = stun band): levels 0-2 = flinch (1) except short stagger (2) in the highest stun band (levels 1-2 also the 2nd highest at health < 50 %); **level 3 (= every 4th hit) [2,2,2,2,2,2,2,4] [2,2,2,2,2,2,4,4] [2,2,2,2,2,4,4,4] [2,2,2,4,4,4,4,4] = short stagger, knockdown at high stun**. A move with WAZA `2c` 0x800 (ender) uses level 3 whatever its position (0x80231E48), plus the retail 'every chain hit connected → knockdown' rule that `enderstagger` removes. So the ender flag is not the hard-strike flag (0x2000, only needed for a long stagger, which the hard table 0x802E4A8C has at middle stun), and a non-ender 4th hit already staggers like an ender. **Cancels:** `FUN_8022e828` buffers presses and acts at the animation's `f17` (fighter +0x32C): next chain hit, hard strike (slot 88 / 91 + direction, input 0x40), grab, block; it returns at once when +0x40C > 3, so **after the 4th hit nothing can be chained or cancelled** (no 5th hit, no hard strike / grab / block cancel); hits 1-3 can cancel into those. Havoc (and DMX, who has Havoc's record): 59-70 empty → P neutral chains and every P-start chain with a K stop after 2 hits; P forward (74-78-86) and P back (57-76-84) and all K-start chains reach 4. Snowman: 80-87 empty → K-start mixed chains stop at 3; his pure K forward / back chains go through 58-60-64 / 75-62-66 to 4. `charstats wwaz` and the GUI label every slot this way. Animation-order bug in `FUN_802023dc` (assumed indices rise with slots) → `motionlookup` patch (tested).

### WAZA (move data; header [0x20, count, n, n words], 32-byte records, MSB first; decoded by `FUN_801fa804` into fighter +0xF74; field names = byte offsets in the decoded record)

Table 1 = 826 moves, table 2 = 256 (Blazin's, environment, parry counters 95 + category, shared moves).

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `00` (8) | **damage (tested)** |
| `02` (6) / `03` (6) | **meter gain** / **victim momentum gain, lower = more** (tested) |
| `04` (7) | **health-tank damage (tested)** |
| `06` (3) / `07` (3) | **attacker limb used** / **victim limb targeted**: 0 head, 1 body, 2 arm, 3 leg, 4 fifth. `06` scales damage by the attacker's limb health; 0-2 use Upper power, 3-4 Lower |
| `08` / `09` / `0a` (5) | **attack parts = hitbox bones** (debug text "atk-parts"): 9/8/7 left hand/forearm/upper arm, 13/12/11 right arm, 15/16/14 left leg, 18/19/17 right leg, 5 head; 0 on grapples; `08` also gives the hit direction |
| `0c` (3) | **hit reaction (tested):** 4 / 6 → victim straight into state 0x10 (6 = sweeps / axe kicks = always knockdown; 4 = low blows, doubled over); 3 → a long stagger becomes short; 5 → normal tables |
| `10`/`14`/`18`/`1c`/`20` (6, /10) | limb damage to head (shown on the submission meter), body (hidden), arm, leg, 5th (tested) |
| `25` | dive / rebound self-damage on a miss; with `blockstaggerflag` ≠ 0 = block stagger |
| `29` (3) | **ground-submission lead-in** (byte +0xF9D, read at 0x802279D4 / 0x8022A284): base00 D251 + 2 × `29` for the attacker, + 1 for the victim, played before the submission's opening. 0 none, 1 **D253** flip left onto the back (HOME WRECKER, CHOKE CHAIN, JOYRIDE, POWER OUTAGE, SNAPPIN' NECKS), 2 **D255** flip right onto the back (UNJUST CONVICTION, GET BENT, SPOON FED, MILITIA MANGLE), 3 **D257** flip right onto the stomach, victim's head toward the attacker (CUBAN NECKTIE), 4 **D259** kick behind the leg, victim drops onto the stomach, head away (GHOST RIDAH) |
| `2a` (6) | **parry category (tested)** |
| `2c` (32) | flags: 0x00800000 light strike, 0x2000 / 0x800 hard strike (0x800 = combo ender), 0x10000 ground strike, 0x08000000 = no tank damage, 0x00080000 = average Upper / Lower power; class-3 stagger needs 0x2000 |
| `36` (10) | **points ÷ 100 (tested)** |
| `38` | 0 = grapple states 0x1F / 0x20 after the move lookup |
| `3a` (12) | global motion ID (on submission tick records: base of 8 FZUI records, see "Submissions") |
| `3c` | linked tick record on submissions (799-825), else 0 |
| unknown | `05`, `0b`, `0d`, `24`-`28`, `30`, `34`, `3e` |

**Parry categories (tested on Snowman):** 1-4 arm pushed away / shove → stagger; 5 / 6 leg caught and thrown → knockdown; 7 / 8 leg pushed → stagger; 9 jump over → knockdown; 10 dodge → short stun; 11 leg over head → knockdown; 13 head pushed → stagger; 14 / 15 airborne pushed down → knockdown; 16 / 19 knee pushed in the air → stagger; 20 / 21 / 24 pushed to the floor, 22 / 23 leg pushed to the floor → knockdown; 25 Capoeira flip → knockdown; 26 / 30 leg held, kick to the face → knockdown; 27 / 28 knee pushed → stagger; 31 back turned → stagger; 0 generic (still parryable); 12, 17, 18, 29 unused. Counter damage: stagger 0, knockdown 15, Parry+ / Parry− 25.

**Free move records** for private copies: 744, 749-753, 761, 767, 774, 775, 778-782, 789, 791 (NOT 799-825 = submission tick records).

### FZUI (frame data per global motion; header [39, count], MSB first; decoded by `FUN_801faba0` into +0x2BC, loaded in `FUN_802021c0`)

Table 1 = 2088 records (fighter motions), table 2 = 873 (motion − 10000 = base00; Blazin' from 709).

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `hit_start` / `hit_end` (11, 2047 = none) | **active frames (tested)**; on reaction animations = wall-stick window; on grab attempts = catch window |
| `win_start` / `win_end` | **invincibility (tested)** |
| `f5` | grapple damage frame; on wake-ups "counts as floored until this frame" |
| `f10` | **release frame (tested)**: victim handed to the floor state; from it the fighter counts as floored (+0x91E bit 0x20), also on attack animations (LETHAL DOSE `f10=none` **tested**) |
| `b` (7) | 1-2 attacker animations, ≥ 51 = landing posture on victim animations |
| `f17` | **cancel point (tested)** |
| `h` / `i` / `j` (8, ×100) | **speed: whole attack / recovery on hit / on block (tested)**; `h` also slows shared animations |
| `w1`-`w4` | 4 × (start, end, type 7 bits) event windows (type 26 = extra damage frame) |
| `e` (5) | **added to the parry window** of a parry against this animation (fighter +0x328; 0 in every record), see "Parry window and `catch random rate`" |
| unknown | `a`, `c`, `d`, `f6`-`f9`, `f14`, `g`, `k` |

A grapple's victim animation = attacker motion + 1 and local index + 1.

### Shared-data fixes done (tested)

- Wall get-off invincibility: table-2 FZUI 258 and 299 win 0-2047.
- Chain stuns: release = victim animation length − 2 for 90 grapple victim animations; falls 281/283/286/288, wall falls 255/260/301, 32 Z environment animations. Pairing rule: victim record = attacker gid + 1 with posture ≥ 51 (skip if it is a move's own animation).
- Block stagger C301 (table-2 #106) `hit_start=0 hit_end=2047` (for `blockwallstick`).
- Crowd weapon hit (state 0x74, Z605 #550 / Z606 #551, damage table-2 move 31): suggested `f10` 154 / 118 against the chain stun; not tested.
- Modder's "MA+SF+SM haymaker fix": template #133 slots 88-90 carry move 251 (right-hand hitbox) with N1172AA (left-hand haymaker); move 223 matches. Only such mismatch outside records 76-81.

## Animation banks and the animation format

- **`.ord` + `.orl`** concatenated = one ELF (MIPS id, little-endian container, big-endian data, pointers = internal relocations); `.ord` = ELF header + `.data`, `.orl` = the rest, sections 16-byte aligned. Symbol `__AnimationBank:::a`: +4 count, +0x10 object table, +0x14 name table (both sorted by name). **Each animation = 5 objects `NAME_d`, `_f`, `_h`, `_s`, `_t`** stored as one block plus a few shared small blocks (`00080000` word, channel lists). **`.mnl`** = u32 count, u32, '.'-separated names in bank order (WWAZ local index points here). Runtime motion ID = bank slot × 1000 + local index; `FUN_80097008` loads each player's `wres` + `blazin.viv` + `base00.viv` (bank loader `FUN_800af048`).
- **Animations with the same name are identical in every bank** (1,288 names, 16,356 copies checked). **Move library** (`moves`): one bank with all 1,288 animations + catalog of the 736 WWAZ move IDs; `moves add` copies a move's animation group (grapple 2, whip grapple 3 `__K`/`__A`/`__B`, turnbuckle dive 5, **submission 10** = 5 attacker/victim pairs, whip submission `__K` + 10) and sets the slot (needs `motionlookup`). Tested: CHITOWN STEAMROLLA, DOWNTOWN THRILLA, DROPPIN' HEAT, BEG 4 MERCY on Snowman.
- **base00** = 709 shared animations (index = FZUI table-2 index; motion = 10000 + index). Families: A idle / walk (A101-A119 stances, A301-A380 walks), B run (B101 start, B201 loop, B301 stop, B601 out of breath), C block (C301 = block stagger), D grapple attempts / holds (D101-D134 attempts, D201-D240 hold loops by grapple type, D251-D260 submission set-ups, D261-D266 / D321-D322 hold interrupted by an outside hit, D271-D276 / D331 hold breaks, D301-D310 rear hard WR hold), E grapple turns, F rebounds (F101/F110/F111 rope, F201-F204 crowd push, F302 run over a floored fighter), G / I wall sticks, H falls (281/283/286/288 knockdown falls), Q staggers / stun loop, V floor idle / hit on the floor (**never change their release**), Y weapon versions, Z environment / wall hot spots / crowd, AA parries and reversals (AA701 short roll). Robert named 560 of 709 in the viewer (`ffny_animnotes.json`). 431 are named directly by the DOL / main_all.dat; most others follow a named first ID.
- **Objects** are type 0x0F compounds: +2 skeleton id (0x3F23 body, 0xC241 face, 0xC51C hands, 0x1400 camera), +4 pointer to a shared info block (holds the frame rate byte, 0x1E = 30 for scenes), +8 u16 child count, +0xA u16 frames, +0xC child pointers. **Children are decoded last-to-first into one output buffer, so where two children write the same words the FIRST child wins.** Output: 0x30 bytes per bone, quaternion at +0x10 (word 4), position at +0x20 (word 8); camera: word 0 = vertical FOV in degrees, words 8-10 = position.
  - `_s` = body rotations (0x13 + 0x12, 0x12 or 0x0D), `_t` = root position (0x14 / 0x15), `_d` = smoothed facing (not part of the pose), `_f` / `_h` = face / hand players' banks (0x19 pose-number tracks).
  - **0x12 / 0x13:** +4 u16 keys, +6 u8 bones, +8 key frame list, +0xC bone number bytes, +0x10 block shift n; 0x12: per bone 16-byte header (4 u16 offsets, 4 u16 scales), blocks of 2^n keys (full key 6 bytes/bone = four 12-bit values; in-between keys 3 bytes/bone).
  - **0x0D** (quaternions; decode 0x8010565C): +4 data block [u16 N channels, u8 bits (4/8/16), u8 0, N × (offset, scale, initial)], K−1 rows of quantised deltas; +0xE N/4 u16 output word indices (bone × 12 + 4). **0x0C** = same with plain floats (decode 0x80104FC4). **0x14** (positions; decode 0x800FA8DC): +4 output word list, +0xE groups, +0x10 block shift (0 = every key full; works), per group 0x24 bytes (min, range, u16 offset / scale), keys 3 × u16 per group. 0x14 / 0x15 also answer the root-motion queries, so positions must be 0x14, not 0x0C. **0x19:** +4 u16 count, +6 u16, +8 pose number per key (bytes), +0xC u16 key frames. Shared by 0x0C/0x0D/0x14: +8 key frame list, +0xC u16 key count; linear between keys, held outside.
  - Decoder objects: `FUN_800f6354(type)`; vtables of 8-byte pairs, setup at +0x9C, decode(obj, out, 0, f1 = frame) at +0x3C; compound vtable 0x803173D8. **Emulation recipe:** DOL sections, bank `.data` with relocated pointers rewritten big-endian, r13 = 0x804477E0, r2 = 0x804577E0, MSR 0x2000, fake allocator object at *0x802F40B8 (vtable + 0x34 = alloc). Unload handler of "AnimationBank" = `FUN_800f7224` (crashes on unrelocated pointers).
  - Every animation starts at its own origin facing +Z (+X = fighter's left, +Y up); the game places the victim of a pair.
- **Embedded node objects need a reserved header in front of the node (12 October 2026):** nodes of type **0x19** (face / hand pose-number tracks) have **0x14 bytes** before them, types **0x16 / 0x17 0x18 bytes**; in retail banks these are zero except the BE word at header + 8 = the node type (572 / 572 good in wres031, 5152 in the library). The load handler `0x800f7224` runs the factory's init (singleton 0x802F40B8, vtable 0x80317148: +0x40/+0x44 allocate 0x800f6904, +0x4c push to freelist 0x800f6ad0, +0x54 factory by type 0x800f6354, +0x5c create-from-node 0x800f670c, +0x64 free-object 0x800f6b0c, +0x6c init / release node 0x800f67c4) over all roots; for 0x19 `0x80107568` builds the header's vtable (0x80318148, constructor 0x8010dba4) at header + 0. Playing creates an object from the node (`0x800f670c`, reads the type at header + 8); **freeing it (`0x800f6b0c`) zeroes the vtable word at header + 0**, and the next play of the same animation goes through that vtable → **"Invalid read from 0x00000098, PC = 0x800f6798"**. The 8 0x19 nodes of the ported REPRESENTIN' JERSEY (V0196__A/B `_f` / `_h`, built from Vendetta data) had no header (the node followed the previous array directly), so the bytes in front of them were taken as the header: first play fine, second play of the Blazin' crashed. Reproduced exactly in the emulator (init → play → free → play: crash at 0x800f6798) and fixed: repaired bank survives 3 play / free rounds on all 8 nodes. **Tools:** `ffny moves checkbank BANK...` counts nodes without their header; `ffny moves fixbank IN OUT` repairs (copies each bad node behind a fresh header at the end of the data, moves every pointer, blanks the old copy; all 725 / 6450 animations compared node by node before / after: identical); every bank written by `moves` (add, blazinown, build) is repaired automatically, and `copy_anim` / `extract` now copy a node's header with it when the node is a separate block. The library `ffny_allmoves` is repaired (12 October). Not checked: blazin.viv banks written by `moves blazin` before this fix (same nodes; there the bank may be reloaded per match, so it may not have crashed).
- **Face / hand players:** model +0x200 body, +0x230 face, +0x260 hand are decoded in that order into ONE pose buffer; the face / hand players take `%s_t`, `%s_s` and `%s_p` (0x19 palette track) from their banks, all optional (so `_f.o` / `_h.o` may hold ordinary 0x0D / 0x14 nodes; **tested**). `_F.o` in v2ip.viv = `__PosePaletteBank` (face poses).

## Fighter code (Start.dol)

- Fighters at 0x803F1144 + slot × 0x12F8. Opponent = +0x0; **+0x834 / +0x838 = distance / angle to the opponent** (from pair tables 0x803F9D10 / 0x803F9D50). State +0x30, sub-state +0x34, state time +0x38, frame +0x9C. State table 0x802E185C (118 entries); set state `FUN_8021dd88(f, state)`.
- **Inputs:** +0x9E0 held, **+0x9F0 pressed this frame**, +0x9F8 = presses of the last 5 frames. Low 4 bits = stick direction (0 neutral); 0x400 RUN, 0x800 P, 0x200 K (0xA00 either), 0x100 grapple, 0x20 pick-up, 0x40 (hold turn-around). Strike inputs are buffered near the end of an animation (`FUN_80203150` → +0x348 / +0x34C).
- **States:**

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 1 / 2 / 3 | idle / guard / block stun |
| 6 | **run** (sub-states 0 start, 1 start-of-run animation, 2 loop, 3 stop, 4 B601 out of breath) |
| 7 / 8 | short roll AA701 (also weapon pick-up) / rope rebound |
| 0x0E / 0x10 / 0x11 / 0x12 / 0x13 | flinch / direct knockdown (WAZA `0c` 4/6) / short stagger / long stagger / knockdown fall |
| 0x14 / 0x1B / 0x37 | wall stick variants (0x37 = after a stagger / shove) |
| 0x15 / 4 / 0x47 | floor / getting up / wake-up attack |
| 0x16 | standing dizzy (base00 10348) |
| 0x1D | end of a run over a floored fighter |
| 0x1E / 0x1F / 0x20 | grab attempt / hold: holder / held |
| 0x2E / 0x2F | clinch states |
| 0x30 / 0x31 | grab reversal: reverser / reversed |
| 0x34 / 0x35 | hold shove |
| 0x41 / 0x42 | submission attacker / victim |
| 0x45 / 0x46 / 0x48 / 0x49 | **light strike / hard strike / running strike / whip strike** |
| 0x4A / 0x4B / 0x4C | ground strike (handler `FUN_8022f738`, slots 100 / 101) / not identified (from `FUN_80207fec`) |
| 0x4E / 0x4F / 0x50 | parry stance / parry counter / being parried |
| 0x56-0x5B | turnbuckle dives, rope / wall rebounds |
| 0x5F | pushed back by the crowd |
| 0x60 / 0x61-0x62 / 0x74 | held by the crowd / crowd double-team / crowd weapon hit |
| 0x6C-0x70 | weapon attacks (0x6E running) |
| 0x73 | block stagger after a blocked dive / rebound |
| 0x36 | not identified (running flag, motion depends on the opponent's move) |

- **Running:** `FUN_80207b04` starts a run when RUN is pressed (+0x9F0 & 0x400); direction from the stick, neutral → toward the opponent (+0x838); sets state 6 (or 0x1C / out of breath). Run sub-state 1 calls `FUN_80223a30(f, 0)` then `FUN_802230c4` (environment) at 0x802225B0; sub-state 2 calls `FUN_802228c0` = running input: environment moves (slots 116 / 117 → state 0x56 with an object in +0xAC0; slot 118 = rope rebound → 0x57), `FUN_80236328`, `FUN_80207fec` (0x4D), weapon pick-up (0x20 → state 7), `FUN_80222fe0`, then **held P/K → 0x48 (0x6E with a weapon, +0x8D4 bit 0); grapple → slot 0x2E filled (`FUN_8021e2e0`) ? state 0x1E with +0x91A = 0x108 : 0x48**. +0x91E bit 0x100 ("running") is set by states 6, 8, 0x1D, 0x36, 0x5F. Leg health slows movement (× (0.75 + 0.25 × health / 50)).
- **Light strike state 0x45:** +0x40C = chain hits so far, **+0x420 = hit history (bit 0 = the current chain hit connected; blocked hits go to +0x421)**, shifted on chain advance. Handler: movement `FUN_8022e700` at 0x8022E5F0, chain input `FUN_8022e828`, epilogue 0x8022E6EC.
- **Strike input from idle** (`FUN` 0x80207E0C, P/K pressed): (1) **ground strike → 0x4A** (0x6F weapon) when the target is on the floor (+0x91E (0x20 or 0x40) and not invincible, or 0x80), same floor level (|+0x3A4 difference| < 1.0) and within 19.5 + body sizes; (2) **whip strike → 0x49** (slot 98 / 99) when the target is in state 6-8, 0x36, 0x48 or 0x5F and runs at the striker within 45° of head-on (angle > 135), no distance check; (3) `FUN_80207fec(f, 10.0)` → 0x4C; else light / hard strike. The grapple input before it: floored target within 17.0 → pick-up grapple (state 0x1E, +0x91A = 0x40, D127). **Whip grapples** (`FUN_80226ab8`): target +0x91E bit 0x100, same floor level, facings differ by > 100 → slot 42 + random 0-3. So a running strike can be whip-struck but not whip-grappled (Robert's experience matches).
- **+0x91E bits:** 1 = invincible (FZUI win window, recomputed each frame in `FUN_801fa538`; also on a state change), 0x20 = frame ≥ `f10` (floored), 0x40 set later in the floor state, 0x80 = getting up before `f5`, 0x100 running.
- **Momentum** +0x364: floor 20.0, caps 98 / 100 (99 = Blazin' ready). **Health:** +0x350 current, +0x354 max, +0x358 tank ceiling, **+0x35C = current / max**; Danger = +0x35C < 0.1 (+0x926 bit 0x1000). **Stun** +0x374 / max +0x378; reaching max sets +0x926 bit 0x400 and stops recovery; idle with the flag → state 0x16 (which empties stun). Points +0x480 (`FUN_80204c18`: WAZA `36` × 100, min 250).
- **Damage** `FUN_801f91b4` (DamageCalc; f1 damage scale, f2 limb scale, f3 stun scale): sum of A = `00` × attacker limb health[`06`] / 50, B = (WPAR limb attack − defence) × `00` × 0.05 (0 in retail), C = (attacker − defender momentum) × 0.1; × (1 + 0.05 × [(power − 5) × Upper/Lower − (toughness − 5)]), floor 0.1 (toughness 5 = no reduction, tested). Limb damage: victim limb −= WAZA field × scale (`FUN_801fc7dc`). **Tank damage** (0x801F9628): `04` × (0.3 + 0.7 × ceiling / max) × scales, ≥ 1.0, ceiling not below max × gamecore value.
- **Limb health** (+0x820, five floats, 50 = full, floor 0): damage part A; leg → movement speed; submission victim's limb `07` at 0 ends the hold; `FUN_801f842c` (submission related); CPU threshold check; HUD. No effect on max health or toughness.
- **Hit reaction** (`FUN_80231128`): victim state switch at 0x80231244 (guard → block stun 3; parry stance 0x4E → parry); WAZA `0c` 4 / 6 → state 0x10 directly; victim states **6, 8, 0x11, 0x12, 0x17, 0x36 force class 4** (check 0x80231B54; `nokd*` / `runhit*` patches); otherwise **class = table[health band][stun band]**: health band `FUN_801f98d8` (+0x35C ≥ 0.75 / 0.5 / 0.25 / below; thresholds 0x80300DC4), stun band `FUN_801f992c` (stun / max ≤ 0.2, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8, 0.9, above; 0x80300DD4). Table by the victim's +0x914 hit flag (= the attacker's STATE when the hit lands: 0x45 → 1, 0x46 / 0x49 → 2, 0x48 → 4, 0x47 → 8, 0x4A/0x4B → 0x10, 0x6C → 0x40, 0x6D → 0x80, 0x6E → 0x100, 0x6F/0x70 → 0x200 / 0x10):
  - 0x2 / 0x8 / 0x80 → 0x802E4A8C: [2,2,2,2,2,3,3,4] [2,2,2,2,3,3,3,4] [2,2,3,3,3,3,4,4] [2,3,3,3,3,3,4,4]
  - **0x4 / 0x100 (running strikes) → 0x802E4B0C (file 0x2E1B0C): [3,3,3,3,3,4,4,4] [3,3,3,4,4,4,4,4] [3,3,4,4,4,4,4,4] [4 × 8]** (patch `runstrikenokd` copies row 0 into rows 1-3)
  - 0x40 → 0x802E4B8C: [2 × 8] [2 × 7, 4] [2 × 6, 4, 4] [2,2,2,4,4,4,4,4]
  - 0x400 → class 1; 0x1 (light) → combo tables 0x802E4C0C (4 levels × 4 × 8; level = +0x40C; a light hit with `2c` 0x800 = ender uses level 4 0x802E4D8C, and class 4 if victim +0x918 bit 0x1000 = **every chain hit connected** (0x802315B4; `enderstagger` removes it)).
  - Classes (tested): 1 flinch 0x0E, 2 short stagger 0x11, 3 long stagger 0x12 (only with `2c` 0x2000 and not `0c` 3, else short), 4 knockdown 0x13. If `FUN_80238508(f, 10.0)` finds something (object?) and the flag is in 0x1CE → state 0x13 with +0xAC0 set (not traced).
- **Hit from behind** (`FUN_80231f94`, hit direction = attacker root → his bone `08`): within 90° of the victim's facing → +0x918 bit 8 (no block, no parry; `rearparry` allows the parry).
- **Parry:** input `FUN_80208b28` from idle (+0x9F8 direction + block 0x20); stance 0x4E turns to +0x838 at 20°/frame; window = (attacker +0x328 + table 0x80304848[style]) × (1 − random × 0.2) (details: "Parry window and `catch random rate`" below); `FUN_8021f144`: attacker state 0x45, 0x46, 0x48 → 0x4F / 0x50; 0x6C-0x6E → 0x53 / 0x54; wake-ups (0x47) excluded (`wakeupparry`). Counter = category `2a` through the defender's WPAR table → base00 10572 + 2x, table-2 move 95 + x. `FUN_8021f1d8` = the same for dives / rebounds (→ 0x51).
- **Grabs:** attempt state 0x1E (`FUN_80226848`); attempt motion `FUN_80226ab8`: +0x91A 0x40 → D127 (pick-up), 0x10 → D126 (wake-up grapple), **0x8 → D125 (running grab)**, whip grapples as above; otherwise D101 + 2 × grapple type. **Catch window** = attempt animation's `hit_start..hit_end` (D125 = table-2 #114: 22-25). **Reach** (`FUN_80226c54`): opponent distance < max(root → hand) + 3.0 (0x802E3E8C) and facing difference ≤ 25 (0x802E3E90) (`rungrab`, `grabreach=` / `grabangle=`; tested). **Reversal** (`FUN_8022705c` at 0x80227158): the caught fighter is in strike state 0x45 / 0x46 with state time ≤ gamecore "grapple catch frame" (13) and frame ≤ his `hit_end`, not grabbed from behind (+0x91A bit 0x200, `rearreversal`) → states 0x30 / 0x31; the reverser turns 10°/frame (0x802E1CE4); +0x930 = 1 for a punch, 3 otherwise; reversal motion 0x29AA / 0x29AC by style, 0x29AE with +0x930 bit 2, 0x29B0 with +0x920 bit 1. From behind = `FUN_8021e16c(..) & 8`.
- **Hold** (`FUN_80227778`, sub-state +0x34 0-7; input `FUN_80228924`: +0x438 direction, +0x43C buttons; flags +0x91A, +0x920, +0x922):
  - shove = RUN / GRAPPLE (0x500) → states 0x34 / 0x35 (not for wrestlers with +0x91A & 0x80); turn-around (E103 / E105) = main SB / WR, hard grab, input 0x40; move slot: from behind 35-37, +0x91A & 0x80 → 52 / 53, else 1 / 11 + 5 for K + direction; `FUN_8021d1d4` picks the state after the lookup.
  - After a grapple move: WAZA `29` ≠ 0 or class bit 0x10 + move flag 0x1000 → D251 + 2 × `29` and keep holding, else release. Kind 8 (D261 / D321) when +0x922 bit 0x10 (hold interrupted by a third fighter), kind 9 (D271 / D331) when the victim breaks free.
  - **Grapple-strike table** 0x802E40D0 (`FUN_8022bb10`; 4 signed bytes per style record 86-141: light P, light K, hard P, hard K; 1 = KB clinch, 2 = SF grapple strike, 0 = normal grapple; record 141 = Ludacris) → nonzero = **clinch states 0x2E / 0x2F**; clinch limit +0x44C = int((1 − defender health) × 8 + 2); finisher when +0x448 ≥ limit and limit > 5; no button mashing. `grapplestrike=` value patch, `slotclinch`, `noclinchshove` (tested).
  - Clinches = slots 11-20 with N02xx animations (punch clinch fighters: Ludacris, Santos, Meca, template 97); clinch moves 327-336.
  - Hard grab with class bit 0x30 and SF/KB main style waits for D201 first (~2 s; `sfkbholdnowait`). The class change was found unnecessary for Santos' submissions (Xzibit and Santos at class 0x0009 do PAIN KILLER like Masa, tested).
- **Submissions:** opening part uses the slot's move; **ticks use the record in `3c`** (799-825; loaded by state 0x41 via `FUN_8021e238`); tick rule (victim state 0x42, 0x802348C0): constant health damage, **limb damage × (1 + 0.5 × tick number)** (`subtick`); `FUN_801f8e68` spreads damage over `f5` + type-26 windows (one = 1.0; several = 0.7 first, 0.3 shared). **Hold-part frame data:** motion lookup special case: animation more than 1 past the slot's while in state 0x41 → FZUI record = **tick record `3a`** + (offset − 2): +0/+1 hold loop attacker / victim, +2..+7 the later pairs. One tick per loop (loop victim record has no `f5` → last frame); tick interval ≈ loop length / (`h` / 100) (**`h` on 384 / 385 slows CHOKE CHAIN's hold, tested**). Links 431→799, 432→800, 441→801, 442→802, 443→803, 445→804, 558→805, 456→806, 457→807, 466→808, 487→809, 506→810, 545→811, 546→812, 549→813, 556→814, 488-498→815-825; `3a` bases 799→0, 800→8, 801→16, 802→32, 803→40, 804→112, 805→136, 806→152, 807→160, 808→200, 809→224, 810→232, 811→312, 812→320, 813→336, 814→344, 815→376, 816→384, 817→392, 818→400, 819→424, 820→496, 821→568, 822→584, 823→616, 824→624, 825→680. Loop lengths (frames, `h` 110 unless given): CHOKE CHAIN 55 (fastest), HOME WRECKER 61, RUN FOR SHELTER 61 (100), POWER OUTAGE 64, CUBAN NECKTIE 66, BLOODLINE ANTHEM 66, PAIN KILLER / NICKLE N DIME 71 (100), SNAPPIN' NECKS / UNJUST CONVICTION 71, BEG 4 MERCY / JOYRIDE / GET BENT 73, 441 / ACT OF MERCY 81, SPOON FED 84, CLOZE YA DOORZ 96 (100), GHOST RIDAH 97, MILITIA MANGLE 109; THE OMEN 34, PARENTAL ADVISORY ~36 (120).
- **Joe Budden's choke crash (retail):** BLOODLINE ANTHEM (546, N08341A-N08345A) is the only submission without camera.xml entries; in a session's first match the grapple camera (`FUN_800b944c`) then uses an all-zero shot (bone ID 0 → crash; with `boneidguard` a black screen). Fix: camera.xml entries N08341A-N08344A modelled on BEG 4 MERCY (built; **not tested in-game**).
- **Dives / rebounds** (`FUN_80235718`, states 0x56-0x5B): victim in guard → **block stagger 0x73** (`FUN_8022c4c4`), else hit; a missed dive costs WAZA `25` + table-2 move 56. `blockwallstick` / `blockstaggerflag` (tested).
- **Wall sticks** (`FUN_802371f8(f, type)`, called each frame by staggers 0x0E/0x11/0x12, shoves, and 0x73 with `blockwallstick`): only inside the animation's `hit_start..hit_end`, once per state, nearby wall with flag 8, roll by health band: short stagger 20 / 30 / 50 / 70 %, long 35 / 55 / 75 / 95 % (table 0x802E55F4); walls with 0x42240000 always stick; then state 0x37. Wall-stuck hits: fall-off chance 0.075 × hits on the wall (0x802D6DD0; `wallchance=`, gamecorex); get-off: stun ≥ 50 % → standing (253 / 258 / 299) else roll Z505 (invincible, 68 frames). State animations: 0x1B G101 / G104 / G203 / G106 / Z505 / G205 (impact table-2 44); 0x37 G401 / G404 / G503 / G406 / Z505 / G505 (impact 46, 58 with wall flag 0x02000000), H801 / H806 knocked off, G701-G901 other cases; 0x14 I401-I406. `charstats wall` lists them. Falls G205 / G505 / I406 invincible until `f10` 46 / 46 / 53; get-offs G106 and Z505 invincible throughout, G406 / I403 not in retail (Robert's mod makes them whole).
- **Robert's wall / collision types (to be matched to states):** always-stick wall; bounce-off-and-fall wall; stagger-back wall (probably state 0x36); edge teeter; crowd / rope pushback (0x5F, 8); the same back-turned after a shove; turnbuckle corner stick; lying on the ropes; crowd hold / weapon (0x60-0x62, 0x74).
- **Environment grapples:** the environment code (0x80224C64) starts **table-2 move 73 + (Z index − 500) / 2** (Z101..Z411 → moves 73-94, shared by all fighters; edit with `setwaza ... --table2`): 73 bottle smash, 74 subway train, 75 tire / oil drum, 76 footlight DDT, 77 pillar (tested), 78 speakers, 79-83 random wall, 84-88 hot spots (88 MA only), 89-94 car (Gun Hill Garage). Any-MA fighters use the second list of each pair (0x80309050 on). Z5xx-Z7xx (crowd) use other records.
- **Crowd:** state 0x60 Z501 / Z503 / Z507 / Z508 / Z505; crowd weapon hit 0x74 (`FUN_80221e28`): Z605 / Z606, damage table-2 move 31 at `f5`.
- **Base motion chooser `FUN_8021ca98(f, kind)`:** 0 idle (A101 + stance; women A119), 1 walk (A301 + 3 × +0xAF4), 2 block (C108 / C109 with a weapon), 3 start-of-run B101 + run type (women B103), 4 run B201 + type, 5 stop B301 + type, 6 grab attempt D101 + 2 × type, 7 hold D201 + 10 × type / D231 light, 8 D261 + 2 × type / D261 / D321, 9 break D271 + 2 × type / D331, 10 hold walking. Weapon in hand (+0x8D4 bit 1) → Y-family motions.
- **Statistics counters** `FUN_802045ec(f, n)` (0x803F6C48 + player × 0x130; 0x29 runs, 0x2A runs started > 60° off the opponent).

## Rounds, match end, gamecore.xml

- **Match setup** 0x803F0D94: +4 flags (0x40 = time over decided by points), +6 bit 1 = 1v1, bit 2 = teams, **+8 time-limit index** (table 0x80303F20, file 0x300EE0: 1 5:00, 2 10:00, 3 15:00, 4 30:00, 5 60:00, 6 4:00, 7 1:00, 8 2:00, 9 0:10), +0xC match type. Clock `FUN_802037b8`; HUD countdown `FUN_800c4a1c` shows 300 − elapsed (0x800C4A9C) for match rules 1.
- **Time-over winner** `FUN_80203928` (points +0x480, team averages, tie = coin flip); summary screen decides separately in `FUN_8006e1f4` (HUD packet 0x8035EA28). Health regen `FUN_801fbf6c` stops with fight state +0x12C bit 4. `timewinhealth`, `crowdtime=` (tested).
- **Match end** `FUN_80203c04` per frame: +0x932 "lost" bits → bit 0x200 = out; `FUN_80204000` decides the end (1v1, whole team out, ≤ 1 left); else `FUN_802040d0` records an elimination. Fight loop `FUN_801f9fc4`, fight state 0x803F0FF8.
- **gamecore.xml** is read at the start of every match (`FUN_8020cca8`); unknown top-level tags fall through to 0x8020E440 and are ignored (so `gamecorex` adds tags there); `<? ... ?>` comments are read as tags (keep under 0x80 chars). Values go to globals 0x80304600-0x80304800 (per-style tables 0x80304820 / 0x80304834 / 0x8030485C, parry window 0x80304848, grapple catch frame 0x80304724 / 0x80304738).

## Announcer speech (speech1.viv)

- `audio/speech1.viv`: `speech/Announcer/<ANN|CRD>_<event>_<voice>.dat/.hdr` (221 banks) + `eventdat/Events.csi` / `Events.evt` (29 events 0x20xx, record = [voice mask, bank id]). Voices E1, E2, F1, F2, J (announcers), CST, DRG, GRG, JNK, PWR (crowd). Stage audio table 0x802E9CF8 (28 × 0x2C; +4 voice mask; stages 1, 4, 7, 8, 13, 17, 18, 20, 23 have no voice); banks loaded by `FUN_80032750`.
- A line is requested by one generated function per event (0x80024618-0x80024B00) → `FUN_801d29f0(1, 0, id)` + the function at 0x8043FB40; callers check 0x80440B40 == 0, the announcer flag 0x80440C74 and that the announcer channel is silent (`FUN_80033520(-1)`). Speech channels: announcer (-1), fighter (1), crowd (0); a new line stops the current one.
- **Called by retail:** Venue_Intro, Fight_Intro_GN, Story_Intro, Lull_In_Action, Climb_Object, Mom_Boost_Big, Special_Happening / Expires / Able / Meter, Weapon_Pre / Post, Enviro_Object_Post, Venue_Extro, Story_Extro. **Never called (≈1,600 recorded lines):** Mom_Level_High, Hold_Init (grapple comments), Hold_Escape, Stunned, Enviro_Crwd_Post (crowd joins in), Enviro_Object_Pre (wall stick comments), Special_Run (running away), Eliminate_Hand, Eliminate_Survival, Story_Intro_Spec (partly missing banks). 0x202D / 0x2039 point to banks not on the disc.
- Patches (all need cave; `anncore` = `ann(event, params)` at 0x80002300): `annholdinit`, `annholdescape`, `annstunned` (`stunnedtime=`, 15), `annmomhigh` (`momhigh=`, 80), `annenviropre` (wall states 0x37 / 0x1B), `anncrowdpost`, `annrunaway` (`runawaytime=` 40, `runawayangle=` 110), `anneliminate` (cuts the current line; `elimdelay=`), all **tested**; `annlisten` = test tool. Old versions `old*` exist only for `--revert`.

## Music and songs

- **A song = folder `music/<name>/`** (`tune.mpf`, `main.mus`, `loading0.mus`, `overlay0.mus`) in any of `audio/music0-4.viv`. Both consoles use **EA-XA ADPCM**; GameCube 22050 Hz streams / 16000 Hz banks, PS2 36000 / 24000, byte order and the map's bit-field order differ (bit fields from the low bit on PS2, from the high bit on GC). `music convert` (`ffny_music.py`) reproduces retail GC files from retail PS2 (`tune.mpf` byte-identical).
- **Map (`tune.mpf` v4):** nodes (sample index 1-based, track, section, part labels), branches [min, max, destination] chosen by a 0-127 controller, events by ID with actions (type 4 go to node / stop, 6 volume, 2 wait, 10 save position, 16 / 11 filter, 8 / 9 initial values). **Node numbers −16..−31 = "the node saved in slot (−n) & 15"; type 10 (−33, slot) saves the current position.** Retail events: 2 → save slot 1 + Blazin' mode music (part 22), 6 → save slot 2 + Blazin' move (part 24), 3 / 7 → part 28 then back to slot 1 / 2, 5 → part 16 (chorus after a Blazin'), **8 → back to slot 1** (fight loop), 9 → part 18 (fight end). Parts: 2-12 fight loop (variants by controller 0-29 calm / 29-120 fight / 120-127 with lyrics), 16 chorus, 18 end, 20 whole song (menu), 22 / 24 Blazin', 26 scratches, 28 Blazin' end, 30 loading screen.
- **Prototype PS2 songs** (`where`, `hypno`, `takeload`) need `fix_map` (event 8 → slot 1, marker-17 branch, event 26) and `--retail-volumes` (both default in `portsong`). **Tested:** where, hypno, takeload play correctly incl. after a Blazin'.
- **Song list = djv2.sod Song category** (32 records × 6 words: 0 name / key, 1 Album, 2 Artist, 3 Entity = folder, 4 reference to a substitute song (followed until a song refers to itself), 5 Title). Venue word 14 (and 15, 20) = reference to the venue's song. The GameCube uses only 20 of the 32: 12 folder names are skipped (list at 0x80297D54).
- **The options save holds the song list:** profile + 0x1444 = 36 song references, +0x1420 = 36 state bytes (low 2 bits 0 locked, 1 unlocked, 2 disabled, 3 enabled; upper 6 bits playlist position), +0x14D4 counter; manager at profile + 0x30 (vtable 0x803152C8). **Limit 36 usable songs = 20 retail + 16 added.** New songs need the `songlist` patch (rebuilds the list from djv2.sod after loading a save, unlocks songs 32+; tested).
- **`portsong`** = convert + add to an archive + add to djv2.sod in one step (`--venue` sets a venue's song). `addsong` gives the new record an internal name hashing just above the current top so it sorts last (existing numbers never change). Battle song list `FUN_80014b90`.

## Characters, outfits and the select screen

- **An added character / outfit needs:** a Character record (`addchar`; own FE_Id, model name, roster category word 24, word 21 voice), the model set in `assets/v2ip.viv` (`modelcopy` or a port; `viv put`), an `entity` entry in `assets/entity.xml` (`<entity><name>M</name><model><name>M</name><texture><name>M</name></texture></model></entity>`; missing = invisible fighter + invalid read at 0x8007B9B4), a `taunt` entry in IntroExtro.xml (`<n>MODEL</n><intro>CI_..</intro><extro>CE_..</extro>`, in `nis.viv`; missing = hang at the intro), patch **`charlist`** (puts records 75+ on the unlocked list; list capacity 80 → 5 added characters guaranteed), menu pictures for the FE_Id, and for own fight data `fighterslot own` + `fightersound`.
- **Unlocks:** reward items by category (`FUN_8000b280`): Character → sorted reference list at profile + 0x1C (capacity 80, saved at profile + 0x12E0, flag bit 0x800000 = "new"); Venue → profile + 8; Song → byte manager. Six 2-bit item managers at container + 0x14E8 (not traced).
- **Select-screen list** (`FUN_8003ea78`): entries (+0 reference, +4 name, +0x18 category, +0x1C FE_Id) sorted by name (stricmp, 0x80040EE8), then **four loops, one per category 1 / 2 / 4 / 8**, each keeping unlocked fighters; the retail groups (Blaze 56 / 81 / 82, D-Mob 59 / 83 / 84, Sticky 57 / 80) are always kept and dropped as a whole if none is unlocked. `Game.GetNextFighter()` (`FUN_8003f5dc`) returns `CLOTH=n&FE_ID=..&LOCK=..&U=..` per entry; **an entry with CLOTH = n takes the next n − 1 entries as its outfits** (n hardcoded by FE_Id at 0x8003F6D0). Patch **`outfits` + `outfits=71:86`** makes any group (tested: Snowman + Snowman 2 in one square, locked outfit greyed).
- **Fighter numbers / records:** free numbers with placeholder data 3, 76-80, 82, 83, 85, 86 (records #2, #75-79, #81, #82, #84, #85). `fighterslot own djv2.sod main_all.dat wres_all.viv OUT FE NUMBER --like FE` gives an entry its own WPAR / WWAZ record (copy) and bank `wres%03d` (written into a new wres_all.viv); `styles` sets menu + fight styles; `check` reports placeholders. **Tested** (Snowman 2 = number 76, record #75, wres076; DMX = 77, #76 = Havoc's, wres077).
- **Sound table per FIGHTER NUMBER** at 0x802EA1C8 (file 0x2E7188), 12 bytes: +0 voice id (−1 none; < 500 `Def_Jam_Characters/%03d`, ≥ 500 `Def_Jam_IP_Characters/%03d`), +4 hit-sound size (1-3), +5 sex, +6 class 1-9, +8 −1. Copied per slot by `FUN_8002f1c0` into 0x804372B4 (+0x14 voice). An empty entry silences the fighter AND loses hit sounds, whooshes and crowd. **`fightersound=NEW:FROM[:VOICE]` (tested).** Taunts use Character word 21 instead.
- **Speech** (`audio/speech2.viv`): `speech/Character|Taunts|Enviro/Def_Jam_(IP_)Characters/<voice>.dat/.hdr` (banks), `speech/streams/Intro|Extro/<voice>.asf` (+ `i1..5` / `e1..5` variants for created-fighter voices 532-538). Missing banks are tolerated (voice 523 Masa, 539 DMX). Voices used by no entry: 501, 504, 505, 509, 511, 532-534, 537, 538.
- **.asf (EA SCxl, GameCube):** `SCHl` PT header (22050 Hz, codec 0x04 = **EA MicroTalk 10:1 revision 3**), `SCCl`, `SCDl` blocks of 4 / 3 frames (1728 / 1296 samples), `SCEl`. Decoder from utkencode (`utk.h`); encoder `utkencode_rev3.c` + `make_asf.py` (built DMX's 539; works in-game).
- **Intro / win speech is started by `audio/animtags.dat`**, not by the scene: the audio code looks up the playing animation's name (upper case, first 8 characters as a 64-bit key, binary search `FUN_800339a0`). Format: "SGAT", count, offsets; entries sorted: 8-byte name, u32 event count, events (u16 frame at 30 fps, s16 n, u16 command, n × u16). **CI_ entries: command 59 (0x3B) param 0 at the first and last frame = intro speech; CE_ entries: 60 (0x3C) param 0 = extro speech, 60 param 1 in the middle = a front-end cue**; 68 = sound effects. `animtags copy ... SRC DST --frames N` (**tested** for CI_133A / CE_133A).

### Menu pictures (screens/screens.viv)

- `screens.viv` ("BIG4", 8,504 entries with full paths, data 32-byte aligned after the directory). Each picture `<name>.big` = RefPack BIGF with `.apt`, `.const`, `.o` (quads), `.gsh` (SHPG textures). **Numbering:** by FE_Id: `thumbs/thumbN(off)`, `portrait/l_CF/l_CF_N_2p/3p/4p`, `match/3_fighters/l_ML_N_..`, `portrait/l_MS_N_..` (name graphics), `t_CO_N`, `t_CT_N`, `t_MS_N_..` (face tiles); by **FE_Id + 1**: `portrait/N_2p/3p` (loading screen, logo painted in), `p_CF/p_cf_N_..`, `p_CT/p_CT_N`, `match/3_fighters/p_ML_N_..` (portraits). `fepics copy 71 86` makes a new FE_Id's 25-file set; `export` / `import` PNGs (tested).
- **Select-grid squares** come from the sprite `thumbAll` inside each of `screens/battle/chooseF2/3/4.big` and `thumbs/thumball.big` (300 frames: **frame FE_Id + 1 = highlighted, FE_Id + 150 = normal**). `fepics thumb SRC NEW` appends frames showing SRC's pictures (tested; appending items to an .apt works). **Why `fepics square` crashed (found 10 October 2026):** when a picture `.o` is loaded, `FUN_8019f674` (called by `FUN_8019f950` with a 512-entry table) enumerates its "Model" symbols and files each model under **atoi(the string at model header +0xB4)**, the model's internal name, which equals its number in every retail file. The copied square models kept the template's name ("120" / "269" in chooseF2), so they replaced Snowman's squares in the table (his square showed the copy, "red"), and the new numbers stayed empty (null model drawn by `FUN_8010F978`, read +0xC8). Fixed: the copy gets its own name string. Checked by resolving every thumbAll frame the game's way (`sqsim.py`: char → model by internal name → TAR SHAPENAME → .gsh texture): only FE 87 (and the unused 88-90 that hold its frame) change, Snowman, D-Mob 3 and FE 86 unchanged. `square ... --replace` overrides frames already set by `thumb`. **Second cause (Robert's test, 10 October 2026: DMX's own square showed Snowman's face):** textures are bound to models by name too: `FUN_8019f950` (per texture N, 512-entry model table) looks in every model for its state named "Matrix::N::TextureMatrix" and the texture-name string "N" (both pointed to from the model's block; the string sits just before the model header). The copies still carried "119" / "Matrix::119::TextureMatrix", so Snowman's texture was bound to them. `square` now renames every such string in the copy (model name, texture name, matrix name; new strings appended, pointers moved); `squarefix screens.viv out.viv 87` repairs squares made by the earlier versions (only the models shown for that FE_Id, and only when a model's Matrix name names another texture; finds nothing in retail). Robert's file after `squarefix`: texture 429 binds only to model 430 etc. **Not yet tested in-game.**
- **Loading-screen portraits (`screens/battle/oldMLoad.big`, `match/3_fighters/p_ML_<FE+1>[R]_2p/3p/4p.big`):** picture x = 0 is the slot's centre line; the game's portraits put the middle of the face there (checked on FE 7, 20, 47, 72 and Robert's FFA / team screenshots). Right-hand slots use the separate pre-made file `p_ML_<FE+1>R_Np.big` (same quad geometry, art mirrored about x = 0), but only for characters listed in the loader's Apt variable **REVERSE_CHARS** (const index 0xAC): FE_Ids 77,75,72,64,82,81,56,55,54,51,47,46,44,36,33,32,30,24,23,20,19,17,16,7 (24 entries; R files exist for exactly these). Others are shown unmirrored on the right, which is why DMX faced the wrong way. The list is built at apt offset 15947 (`a2 ac` push name, `b5 xx` push byte × n, `b5 18` count, `42` initArray, `1d` setVariable); FEID (const 0xAD) is checked against it near 14660. Patch for DMX: `b5 57` (87) inserted and count 0x19; the 2 extra bytes come from turning the unused `DEBUG_P2` definition (`a2 fc b5 52 3c`, only read for a debug sMatchInfo string) into `b5 00 17` (push 0, pop), so the file keeps its length. Other Apt opcodes seen: `a2` push constant (u8 index), `ae` push variable's value, `3c` defineLocal, `17` pop. No other screen has such a list.
- Menu `.gsh`: directory names padded with spaces; a texture's own name is in its last block (type 0x70). Apt format notes: `.const` → movie offset; characters 1 shape, 5 sprite, 7 image; frame items 1 action, 2 label, 3 place object (64 bytes), 4 remove; pointers are file offsets.
- **Pose editors (artifacts):** "Snowman 2 Pose Editor" and "DMX Pose Editor" (10 October 2026; DMX's current model V2IP_133A with brow bones and fixed pivots, the same three reference views and Robert's pose as start, plus face controls: both / each eye, eyelids (Blink x), Squint (x), both / each brow (x = lower / raise, z = tilt, y = sideways; right brow's z mirrored), Sneer (x = lift, y, z), Jaw (x = open, y = sideways)). Face values are the bones' local Euler rotations (R = Rz Ry Rx after the rest rotation), the same format `r3d.skin` / `run_dmx_pics.py` read. Built by `dmxposer/gen.py` + `build.py` from the Snowman page. Version 2 of the DMX page: "Whole body" also moves the body (pose key `move` = [x, y, z] added to the root's world position), a T-pose button (empty pose = rest pose), and "Choose a picture…" adds the viewer's own picture as a view (front camera at (0, 11, 60), zoom / pan sliders; kept only in the open page). Version 3: the pose text box is editable and "Load pose from the text" restores a saved pose (accepts `{"pose": {...}}` or the bare object; unknown bone names are skipped and listed); the text also stores the own-picture zoom / pan as `picture`; Undo (100 steps) also takes back Reset / T-pose / Load.
- Snowman 2 (FE 86) and DMX (FE 87) pictures were rendered from the models in Robert's pose (`poseA.json` / `poseB.json`, pose editor artifact) and placed by matching the originals (scales / offsets per picture are in `mkpics.py`). DMX's name graphics come from Vendetta `Menu/DEFJAM_LOGOS.tpl` image 4. **DMX pictures v2 (10 October 2026):** re-rendered with Robert's pose from the DMX Pose Editor (arms crossed, Vendetta-portrait look; `pose_robert.json`, `run_dmx_pics2.py` = run_dmx_pics with `move` support, 4-player close-up placed by `fit4p.py` inside that picture's visible quads (scale 0.86, 80 px left), squares zoom 1.05 / 11 px down). **DMX loading pictures v3 + square (10 October 2026, `DMX_loading_v3.zip`, not yet tested in-game):** square = the thumb framing Robert picked (zoom 1.15, 6 px down, `SQMAP=35/33,-2.12,-1.12` maps thumb box (0,1)-(35,36) onto the square box (2,2)-(35,35)), sepia by the 119 → 268 tone ramp; `p_ML_88_*` re-rendered by `pics/lp.py` (template geometry of FE 72's `p_ML_73_*`, which covers the whole panel; face on x = 0; 1v1 shifted 12 px further left (`DX={"2p":-12}`) so the head is whole) and new `p_ML_88R_*` = mirror about x = 0, built by `pics/mkbig.py`. **v4 (same day, Robert's choice, `DMX_loading_v4.zip`):** directions swapped so DMX faces right like most FFNY fighters: `p_ML_88_*` = mirrored render, `p_ML_88R_*` = unmirrored (lp.py mirror flags swapped); square face area (2,2)-(35,35) mirrored too. **v5 (`DMX_pictures_v5.zip`):** every other DMX picture mirrored too (p_cf_88_*, p_CT_88, t_CO / t_CT / t_MS tiles, thumb87(off), 88_2p/3p) by `pics/run_dmx_pics3.py` (= run_dmx_pics2 + `MIRROR=1`: r3d.render wrapped to render with offset w-1+ox-2·u_axis and flip, i.e. a mirror about a vertical axis; default axis = face point, `AXIS={stem: x}` gives it in picture x; used: head-silhouette middles p_cf 2p 91.5 / 3p 72.5 / 4p 47, p_CT 74.5, loading 72_2p 91 / 72_3p 65); `FULLBODY=1` rescales p_cf_88_4p about the head top so the feet end at Snowman's (y 21-299 of 304; retail 4p select pictures all show the shoes): factor 0.853. Logos by `pics/make_logos2.py` with `LOGOX={"88_2p":152,"88_3p":110}`. thumb87 = select-square framing (1.15 / 6) flipped in box (0,1)-(35,36). Name graphics (l_*) not mirrored.

- **FFNY Portrait Studio (19 October 2026, `FFNY Portrait Studio.html`, sources `ffny_tools/portrait_studio/` = page.html + fmt.js + render.js + app.js, `build.sh` concatenates them):** one local HTML page (not an artifact: artifacts cannot save files). Opens v2ip.viv and screens.viv in the browser (nothing uploaded). **Any of the 80 models** (JS port of ffmodel / skelcmp: ELF relocations, `EAGLAnimationBuffer` batches, GX strips 0x98 / triangles 0x90 / fans 0xA0, weights; sheet = largest texture of the .gsh; names / FE_Ids from Robert's djv2.sod embedded); pose controls of the DMX Pose Editor (start pose = Robert's arms-crossed pose); software renderer with up to 6 directional lights (colour, strength, around / height, "edges only" = Fresnel-weighted rim light; optional "turn with the camera"), base light, shine, colour (brightness / contrast / saturation / warmth). **The DMX pictures of 10 October had lighting too** (r3d.render: key (-0.5, 0.6, 0.65) 1.0, fill (0.7, 0.1, 0.5) 0.25, ambient 0.42, no back light). Per picture type of the chosen FE_Id: own orbit camera (drag / Shift-drag / wheel), mirror, background (transparent / black / white / original), "draw only inside a box", "keep the original inside a box", optional own pose / own lights; loading + name pictures (`N+1_2p/3p`) can paint the FE's own `l_ML_N_kp` name graphic in. Types found by file name: p_cf_N+1_2p/3p/4p, p_CT_N+1, p_ML_N+1_kp (+ `p_ML_N+1R_kp` written as the mirror about picture x = 0), N+1_2p/3p, t_CO_N / t_CT_N (texture 3 = toned copy of texture 1: quads at the same place -> "alternate"), t_MS_N_kp, thumbN (+ thumbNoff toned), select squares (chooseF2/3/4 + thumball: frame FE+1 / FE+150 -> texture; the square quad is 37 x 37 at texel (0,0)-(37,37) of a 64 x 64 texture). Toned versions: per channel toned = a * luminance(colour) + b fitted on the original pair. Writing: every texture filled from the 1x composite (4x4 supersampled render) through texel = (composite + origin) * scale + trans, own palette per texture (exact when <= 256 RGB5A3 codes, else median cut), inner BIGF patched in place (.gsh keeps its size), RefPack-compressed (JS port of refpack.compress), archive written like ffny_viv.put (**byte-identical to the Python put in a test**); saved through the browser's save dialog (Chrome / Edge) or as a download. Tested headless on a 49-file sample of Robert's screens.viv (`extract_sample.py` -> `screens_sample/`): FE 87 all 16 types (20 files) and FE 72 p_ML with R files; Python check: only .gsh members changed, fepics / sqsim read the result; squares of FE 87 also show for the unused FE_Ids 88-90 (the page lists such sharing). Not yet tested in-game.
- **Freeze of Robert's PC (19 October, ~16:48):** during a staging of ~140 MB (screens.viv 121 MB + v2ip.viv + others) through the desktop app; event log: Kernel-Power 41 with bugcheck 0 (manual reset, no blue screen, no WHEA error), last log 16:48:06. Avoid staging screens.viv whole; extract small samples on his PC instead.

## Character models and textures (assets/v2ip.viv)

- `v2ip.viv` (BIGF, RefPack entries, not name-sorted): per character `V2IP_nnnX.o` (model), `.gsh` (textures), `_F.o` (face pose palette). Snowman = V2IP_002A, Sticky 114A, Blaze 113A, D-Mob 014A, Ice-T 132A, Crow 126A, Ludacris 123A, Havoc 118A.
- **`.o` model:** ELF (little-endian container, big-endian data, little-endian pointer words fixed by relocations). `__Skeleton:::root` (+0 u16 0x3F23, +8 bone count 94, records from +0x10, **0x70 bytes: scale, parent (s32), quaternion, translation, mirrored bone index, 4 × 4 inverse rest matrix stored TRANSPOSED**; PonyTail2, Pendant2, placellip, placerlip are inconsistent in retail). Bone order: root, tophip, L leg, hip, waist, chest, L arm + fingers, R arm + fingers, neck, head, hair / face (59-80), backbone_eff3, pendants, R leg. Batches (≤ 10 blended matrices, weight table 16 bytes per matrix, positions, normals s16 × 16384, UVs, GX strip display list), 7 material groups listed in a "TTAR" table (0 skin Skin2, 1 mouth, 2 eye Skin1, 3 hair, 4 cloth, 5 unskinned "Direction", 6 eyelid alpha), group bounds, model header (+0x9C group count, +0xA0 names, +0xCC TTAR). **`owrite.py` writes complete models (tested in-game).**
- **`.gsh` (SHPG):** header, count, id, per texture name + offset; block chain (0x19 image CI8 8 × 4 tiles, pixels at +0x10; 0x32 palette 256 × RGB5A3; 0x69, 0x7C position, 0x70 name, 0x6F text). Every character: body sheet 256 × 256 + `br01` / `br02` (128 × 128) = the sheet's top-right quarter with bruises / blood. `gsh export / import` (tested).
- **Damage faces:** the game copies br01 / br02 as palette INDICES over the whole top-right quarter of the sheet (level 1 below ~40 % health, level 2 near Danger), so **the three textures must share ONE palette** (`gsh import` keeps a common palette automatically). **Dolphin's texture cache at "Fast" hashes only every 64th 8-byte word**: if a bruise picture changes none of those words, Dolphin keeps showing the clean face (23 retail fighters, e.g. Ludacris, never look hurt at Fast; real hardware is fine). `gsh import` applies `dolphin_nudge` (one invisible sampled pixel changed). Tested: Snowman 2's blood shows.
- `modelcopy` copies a set under a new letter (renames `__Model:::` / `__BBOX:::`, texture name, SHAPENAME, .gsh names); `rename_model.py` (dmx scripts) allows any same-length name.

## Def Jam Vendetta ports (Snowman 2 = V2IP_002B, DMX = V2IP_133A)

- **Vendetta files:** `Data/sNNNx/` per character (Snowman `s114a`, DMX `s305a`): Nintendo character-pipeline formats, parts `_m0` body, `_m1` face, `_m2` / `_m3` hands, `_m5` glass quad: `.act` (bone tree; bone records 0x1C, control 0x34 = type 0x0D, scale, quaternion, translation; names after "ACTU"), `.gpl` (geometry, s16 positions with 10 fraction bits), `.skn` (one-bone / two-bone / accumulation weight lists), `.tpl` (512 × 512 CI8 sheet + I4 transparency mask). **Vendetta's body skeleton = FFNY's** (same names, parents, rest pose); hands and face differ.
- **Model port** (`vendetta_port_scripts.zip`: `vport.py` mapping + material groups by texture region, `owrite.py` writer, `vtex.py` sheet 512 → 256 + br01 / br02 from the FFNY bruise differences shifted onto the Vendetta face; `model/rebuild.py` = rebuild with the CURRENT model as donor, byte-identical regression). Bone mapping: body 1:1; hand_bone → hand, index → pointer, pinkey → pinky, thumb_root / thumb1 → Thumb1; face: head_bone → head, jaw → JAW, squint, sneer, L/R_lip, mabuta_blink → Blink, L/R_eyeball → L/R_Eye, **miken_brow → L_Brow / R_Brow** (split by side, since 10 October). owrite needs a triangle in every skinned group (DMX: one hidden triangle in group 6).
- **Face pivots:** both models' L_Eye, R_Eye, Blink, JAW, Squint were moved to Vendetta's bone positions (`model/face_pivots.py`: local translations from the unchanged parent, BotLip keeps its world position, inverse rest matrices recomputed; rest pose unchanged). **Tested:** eyes stay in the sockets (DMX; Snowman 2 since 10 October). R_Lip / L_Lip / Sneer pivots were NOT moved (the scenes carry translations for them instead).
- **ANXB (`*.anx`) animation format** (`anxb.py`): header +0x20 clip count, +0x38 bit 0 = LZSS (flag byte LSB first, 1 = literal; match u16 LE, offset = absolute 4096-byte ring position, length + 2); clip entry 44 bytes (s1 per-bone headers, s2 keys (u16 flags, u16 time), s3 s16 values, s4 8-byte events, bone count, key count, length in frames, flags). Body clips: delta-coded Euler (2π / 32767; order 0x27 → q = Y·X·Z, 0x1B → X·Y·Z, multiplied on the left), absolute local rotations; translation only on root. 60 fps. **Per-bone layout (flag bit 0)** for facial.anx (clip = character × 3 + {face, left hand, right hand}), lip.anx (clip = character), eye.anx (clip = the motion's FZUI field a6c − 1). DMX character id 42. All 821 base / 2,003 skill clips decode exactly.
- **Section-4 events** (u32 frame at 60 fps, u32 bits): bits 20-24 face pose, 15-19 left hand, 10-14 right hand, 5-9 mouth (lip clip frame n + 18; face = slerp(face, identity, 0.6) × lip on SQUINT, R_lip, L_lip, jaw). Poses between events are blended.
- **Vendetta intros:** WWAZ slot 95 / 96 chosen 50 / 50 (DMX: skill #1801 / #1802); camera = the motion's FZUI camera field into `data/game_cam.pak` (369 paths: frame, eye, target, roll, FOV), with four cameras per intro block (one per ring corner; P1 = corner 0, P2 = corner 1). DMX win = skill #1908, camera 344.
- **DMX's scenes (`CI_133A.viv` / `CE_133A.viv`, `build_cine3.py`, v3d):** body = Vendetta clip at 30 fps (every second frame), Vendetta orientation, intro held to 109 frames; cameras: **intro p1 = Vendetta camera 208, p2 = 209** (FFNY picks `%s1_EYE.o` for side 0, `%s2_EYE.o` otherwise; extro `%s_EYE.o`), win = 344; FOV in a 0x14 node at word 0 (never leave a template 0x15 path in a compound: the first child wins); face (`vface.face_exact`: rotation + translation per face bone, incl. brows), eyes (eye.anx), fingers in `_f.o` / `_h.o` as `_s` / `_t` objects; animtags entries for the speech. **Relocation rule: every rebuilt `.o` must have the retail relocation types (1, 2)** (a writer bug once produced (0, 1) and crashed in the unload handler). **Tested: looks "very similar to Vendetta if not the same", speech, fingers, mouth, snarl, brows, eyes.**
- Snowman 2 uses FFNY Snowman's own intro (CI_002A) and Robert's chosen outro (BK_15A131); FFNY's face poses act on his Vendetta face bones.
- **Venue KO / outro scenes:** `assets/v2ta.viv` (CI_/CE_ 69 each, TI_/TE_, VI_ fly-ins, BK_ Blazin' KO scenes for venues 04A, 05A, 07A, 15A). Scene `.viv` = BIGF ("L231" tag) with `<name>.o` (body), `<name>[1|2]_EYE.o` / `_TARGET.o` (cameras), `_f.o`, `_h.o`; RefPack-compressed inside v2ta.viv. BK_15A12 / BK_15A13 are one-fighter outros; `BK_15A121` (sad) / `BK_15A131` (angry) were rebuilt as normal extros (tested). FFNY FOV is vertical (16:9 letterboxes).

## Def Jam Vendetta: PS2 vs GameCube (11 October 2026)

- **DATA.BIN file table is in SLUS_206.39** at file offset 0x157F40 (vaddr 0x256F40): 293 × (u32 start sector, u32 sector count), no names (`data/data.bin` is the only path; loader object at gp − 0x5FA0, read call `0x1b7530(obj, dest, sector, count)`). By index: 0-4 small 0x80-header textures; 5-124 TIM2 24-bit 512 × 768 pictures; **125 game_cam.pak, 126 eye.pak, 127 main_all.dat, 128 base.pak, 129 skill.pak** (GC formats: game_cam / main_all identical apart from byte order; base / skill / eye in the PS2 clip format below); 130-197 0x80-header GS textures and venue models (pairs 140-195: model with an (offset, id) list + textures); **198-255 the 58 wrestler packages ("WRS ")**; 256-280 "AUD " / "PAK " (sound); 281-285 other; 286-291 "PRM "; 292 texture. `ps2vendetta list / extract --elf SLUS_206.39` uses the table and names the packages `wrs_<id>_<NAME>.wrs`; without `--elf` it falls back to a content scan.
- **Roster** (SLUS_206.39 vaddr 0x27E100 = file 0x17F100, 32 bytes per character id 0-58): +0 name, +4 short name, +8 height, +0xC weight (string pointers), +0x10-0x18 three ids, **+0x1C DATA.BIN file index** (loader `0x165838` reads *(0x27E11C + id × 32)). Same ids as GC Vendetta (DMX = 42 → file 239; Ludacris 40 and 41 share file 238). Packages are loaded to 0x8C4500 + slot × 0x96000; `0x1b14e0` "attach wrestler", `0x1b15f0` prints the package (debug strings "model data ;", "template data ; LIP / EYE", "motion data ;", "texture data ;").
- **WRS package:** "WRS ", u32 size, f32 scale, u32, then u32 offsets relative to their own position (−1 = none): +0x10 shadow, +0x14 body, +0x18 body 2, +0x1C face, +0x20 / +0x24 L / R hand, +0x28 / +0x2C alpha models; +0x30 body skeleton (42 bones), +0x34 face (11), +0x38 lip (11), +0x3C eye (11), +0x40 / +0x44 hand skeletons (13); **+0x48 face poses, +0x4C mouth shapes, +0x50 / +0x54 hand poses** (u16 length, then one track per skeleton bone: u8 key count, keys u16 frame + 3 × s16, 1/4096 rad, x then y then z); +0x58 textures. These are the PS2 copies of GC `facial.anx` (clip 3·id + 0 / 1 / 2) and `lip.anx` (clip id): face and hand keys at the same frames; **mouth: PS2 key k = GC lip frame k + 19** (GC lip clips are 30 frames: identity at 0, shapes at 19-30; PS2 keeps only the 12 shapes).
- **The mouth difference (found 11 October 2026):** the event bits 5-9 (mouth value n, 0 = none) are blended between the previous and the next event on both consoles, but **GC Vendetta samples lip frame n + 18** (0x80019CF0: `addi r3,r3,0x12`, lip player at +0x1024) while **PS2 samples key n** (0x1b2c24: `sra 5; andi 0x1f` → sampler 0x1b79e8 with frame n, no offset) = GC frame n + 19. **So GC Vendetta shows every mouth value one shape too early** (mouth n = PS2's shape n − 1); the data is the same. DMX's win (mouth 1, 1 at 7, 6 at 15, 2 at 24, 6 at 32, then 5, 6, 5, 6, 2, 6, 10 ...): on PS2 value 6 = jaw open 8°, lips ±8°, value 2 = lip corners pulled 20°; on GC value 6 = shape 5 (lip pout, jaw 1.7°) and value 2 = shape 1 (all zero), so the jaw hardly moves in the first half second, which is what Robert saw (PS2 tested on PCSX2). The FFNY DMX scenes copied the GC rule (n + 18); rebuilding them with n + 19 gives the PS2 mouth. DMX's shapes (PS2 key: jaw / R lip x, degrees; "jaw" = lip-skeleton bone 4 = GC lip bone 8, y): 0 −0.3 / 0.7, 1 0 / 0, 2 0.7 / −21.1, 3 −2.0 / 8.0, 4 −2.4 / −1.1, 5 −1.7 / −9.0 (y −16), 6 −8.2 / −8.0, 7 −16.6 / 0.7, 8 −11.1 / 9.7, 9 −2.3 / 21.0, 10 −1.1 / 12.6, 11 1.6 / 7.4.
- Both consoles then blend the face pose toward identity by 0.6 (PS2 constant at gp − 0x71F8, **gp = 0x29C570**) and multiply in the lip rotations (PS2 0x1b7020 = slerp, 0x19f8f0 = quaternion multiply; event search 0x1b77e0(…, mask) = GC `FUN_800199bc`). PS2 sampler 0x1b79e8: per track the last key with frame < t and the next one, slerp by the fraction. Face poses and hand poses use value n directly on both consoles (no offset).
- **Face / hand / mouth data, PS2 vs GC, all 58 packages** (`ps2vendetta compare`): face poses and mouth shapes the same (< 0.05°) for 49 characters including DMX; different: Cruz (face 43°, mouth 3.7°), Steel (15° / 11°), Redman (5° / 3.4°), Ludacris (mouth 7°; PS2 has one package for both costumes, GC two different sets), WC (face 1.2°), the women 54-58 Deja, T'ai Monique, Opal, Penny, Carla (face 25-28°). Hand poses: one thumb track differs by about 20° in the hand-pose set most characters share (Masa, Pockets, Keith Murray and the women within 0.15°); not checked whether that is real or a GC Euler-order detail.
- **PAK** (both consoles; GC game_cam.pak is one too): "PAK ", u32 size, u32 count, u32 per entry; **entry i starts at 12 + 4·i + value** (relative to its own table slot). GC game_cam.pak entries are big-endian inside, PS2 little-endian.
- **PS2 body clip:** u16 length (frames, 60 per second; = GC float word 9), u8 event count, events 6 bytes unaligned (u16 frame, u32 bits = GC section 4), then **25 tracks** of [u8 key count, keys u16 frame + 3 × s16], then padding to 16. **Angles in 1/4096 radian, X applied first, then Y, then Z (q = Rz·Ry·Rx)**; root position units / 128 (GC / 2^A, A = 11 → PS2 = GC / 16). Track → GC track: 0 → (none, see below), 1 root (position), 2 tophip, 3 hip, 4 waist, 5 chest, 6 neck, 7 head, 8-11 → 10-13 (L shoulder, arm1, arm2, hand), 12-15 → 16-19 (right), 16-19 → 22, 25, 26, 29 (L hipbone, leg1, leg2, foot), 20-23 → 32, 35, 36, 39 (right), 24 → GC track 41. GC tracks 8, 9, 14, 15, 20, 21, 23, 24, 27, 28, 30, 31, 33, 34, 37, 38, 40 (roots / effectors) are static and absent on PS2. Key frames are the same frames as GC's normalised times (t / 32767 × length). Base clip 820 is cut short on PS2 (track 24 missing).
- **PS2 track 0** has rotations in 2,254 clips where GC track 0 is always identity (DMX intro 1801: about (0, −4.6°, −1.7°); win 1908: a constant (140°, −120°, 115°)). Not a body rotation as far as the data shows (DMX's win would be turned sideways); purpose unknown (facing / look direction?); to be traced in SLUS_206.39 from the body sampler.
- **PS2 eye clip:** u16 length, u8 events (0), then 2 tracks (L_Eye, R_Eye) in the same key format (GC eye bones 9 / 10).
- **GC per-bone layout (eye / lip / facial, clip flag 1)** read directly: clip word 0 → per bone 12 bytes (u32 value address, u32 key address, u32 key count); keys u16 flags + u16 time (/32767 × length); values 3 × s16 (2π/32767). GC eye bone 1 = a constant (1171, 0, 8191).
- **Vendetta FZUI** (unpack `FUN_8005ce84`, bit reader `FUN_8005d5dc`, MSB first): header u32 38 (record bytes), u32 count (table 1 = 2003 skill clips, table 2 = 821 base clips); fields in bits: 10 × 11 (+0x30-0x54; +0x30 / +0x34 / +0x48 2047 = none), 3, 7, 11, 4, **10 = camera** (game_cam.pak path; +0x68), **9 = eye clip + 1** (+0x6C), 8 (÷ constant → float +0x70), 8 (+0x74), then 4 × (11, 11, 7) windows. DMX: 1801 cam 204 / eye 38, 1802 cam 208 / eye 39, 1908 cam 344 / eye 100.

**Comparison results (body: decoded GC clips from the DMX clip viewer vs PS2 keys, sampled every half frame, 24 bones + root position):**

| Data | Result |
|---|---|
| clip lengths, face / hand / **mouth** events | **identical in all 2,824 clips** |
| cameras (game_cam.pak) | identical (369) |
| frame data (FZUI) | identical except base #690 / #692 (fields +0x50, +0x60, window 0) |
| body animation | 2,774 within 0.5° (quantisation) / 0.03 position; 36 within 1° (long grapples, rounding); **14 really different**: skill #969 (L arm1 up to 7°, root 0.29 at frame 0), #970 (L hand 1.9°), **#1049 (re-keyed around frames 118-134: L leg1 15°, arms 11-14°)**, cinematic #1794 (root position up to 5.3 units), #1906 (root 0.25), base cinematics #753, 755-758, 761-763, 779 (root position 0.14-1.6) |
| GC track 41 / PS2 track 24 | same keys; only 180° flips interpolate differently |
| eye clips | 137 of 192 the same; 39 re-keyed / changed by 1-10° (intros / outros skill #1774-1929 and base #774-783, #820), 16 where PS2 has plain straight-ahead eyes and GC animated ones (base #765-767 (GC data there is garbage), #787-819) |

- **DMX (intros 1801 / 1802, win 1908): same data on both consoles** — body within 0.13°, same mouth / face / hand events, same cameras, same frame data, eyes 38 / 39 identical, eye 100 within 0.6°, same face poses and mouth shapes. Mouth cues: intro first change at frame 6 (0.1 s), win: value 1 held from frame 0 to 15 (0.25 s), then 6, 2, 6, 5 ... **The visible difference is the GC engine's mouth off-by-one (see above), not the data.**
- **DMX scenes v4 (11 October 2026, `DMX_scenes_ps2mouth.zip`, not yet tested in-game): CI_133A / CE_133A rebuilt with the PS2 mouth rule.** `vface.LIP_OFFSET` (new, default 19 = PS2; 18 = GameCube) in `dmx_cinematic_port_scripts_v4.zip`; `port/relip.py OFFSET OUTDIR` rebuilds only the body `.o` (it carries JAW too) and `_f.o` from the existing scene files (frame count from the old body, ground offset recovered from the old root track) and repacks the `.viv` with the other entries unchanged; `port/build_cine3_lite.py` = build_cine3 without the model import. Check: with 18 the `_f.o` files come out byte-identical to the scenes Robert had, the bodies identical in rotation and within 0.00002 in root position. With 19 only the mouth changes: L_Lip / R_Lip up to 30° (intro) / 22° (win), JAW up to 9°; other face bones < 1° (shared key reduction). In the win the jaw now opens 4-6.5° within the first 20 frames (30 fps). Paths the scripts expect: `/home/claude/in/big/data/` = Vendetta GC `data`, `/home/claude/work/vdata.json` = the clip viewer's VDATA, `/home/claude/work/scenes/CI_002A|CE_002A/` = the extracted FFNY Snowman scenes, `/home/claude/port/CI_133A_old/` etc. The `.viv` sizes changed (CI 35,792, CE 28,608 bytes): re-open the root in GCRebuilder before Rebuild.
- **FFNY's own scenes are not affected:** the n + 18 rule is in GameCube Vendetta's code; FFNY's characters use FFNY's own face data and players. Whether FFNY PS2 and FFNY GameCube differ in their face animation has not been checked.
- Scripts used: `ffny_tools/ffny_ps2vendetta.py` (extract + events / cameras / FZUI / eyes); the body comparison (`compare.py` / `runcmp.py`, reads the clip viewer's decoded data) is in `ps2_vendetta_compare_scripts.zip`. Per-clip results: `gc_vs_ps2_body_clips.csv` (max bone difference, worst bone / frame, root difference, eye clip and camera of cinematic clips).

## Vendetta move list and the moves viewer (11 October 2026)

- **Vendetta main_all.dat (GC, big-endian chunk sizes; PS2 the same with little-endian sizes):** WAZA (table 1: 999 moves, table 2: 256), FZUI (2003 = skill clips, 821 = base clips), NAME (999 / 132 names), then 59 × WPAR + WWAZ (one per character id). **WAZA:** header u32 record size 25, u32 count, then a header block; table-1 records start at chunk offset 228 (the u32 before them is 199 = bits per record), table 2 at 16; record bit fields MSB first (`FUN_8005d0f4`): 8 damage, 6, 6, 7, 4, 3, 3, 5 × 4 (attack parts like FFNY `08`-`0b`), 4, 3 × 6, 4, 5, 4, 5, 4, 5, 32 flags, 8, 10 points, 7, **12 motion = skill.anx clip number** (table 2 → base clips), 12, 12.
