First version `oldshoveropeface` (crowd state 0x5F, bigspace +0x6480; wrong case, tested) is replaced automatically | emulated |

- **`runcancel`:** hook 0x8022E5F0 (`bl 0x8022e700` → `bl 0x80002E00`): after the movement function, if +0x420 bit 0 and +0x9E0 & 0x400, call `FUN_80207b04` with the stick bits cleared and RUN marked pressed (inputs restored after); on success the fighter goes into the 4-entry table 0x80002DA0 and the frame returns into the handler's epilogue 0x8022E6EC. The run starter's first instruction (file 0x204B04) branches to 0x80002DB0, which removes the fighter from the table when RUN is pressed this frame (any new run), then runs the original `stwu` and returns to 0x80207B08.
- **`runstartattack`:** hook 0x802225B0 (`bl 0x802230c4` → `bl 0x80002F00`): keeps the environment call; if it did nothing and the fighter is in the table, applies the loop's strike / grapple rule to +0x9F0 and returns 1 when it set a state. Old first versions (`oldruncancel`, `oldrunstartattack`) are replaced automatically when applying the new ones.
- **Cave layout** (keep code in 0x80001A00-0x80002FE0: Dolphin writes "STUBHAXX" at 0x80001804, HLE-hooks 0x80001800, 0x800018A8 (Gecko handler; its first runs flush the JIT = stutters) and 0x80002FF0): 0x80001A00 sfkbholdnowait, 0x80001A80 limbtank, 0x80001B00 slotclinch, 0x80001C80 boneidguard, 0x80001CC0-0x80001CCC values (limbtank K / 50.0, nolimbweak mask, subtick growth), 0x80001CD0 nolimbweak, 0x80001D00 rungrab (values 0x80001D40 / 44), 0x80001D80 annholdinit, 0x80001E40 anneliminate per-frame part, (0x80001EB0 / 0x80001F10-0x80002000 = oldgrapplenames v1, free once v2 is applied), 0x80001F00 cavetest, 0x80002000 gamecorex, 0x80002300 anncore, 0x800023C4 anneliminate stub, 0x80002400-0x80002580 announcer wrappers, 0x80002580 annrunaway, 0x80002600 annmomhigh, 0x80002660-0x8000267C values, 0x80002680 annstunned, 0x80002740 anncrowdpost, 0x80002780 anneliminate, 0x800027F0 elimination delay, 0x80002800 songlist, 0x800029C0 charlist, 0x80002A80 crashtrace, 0x80002B00-0x80002D98 outfits (table 0x80002B40, counters 0x80002C60), 0x80002DA0-0x80002F00 runcancel, 0x80002F00-0x80002FC0 runstartattack. **Free:** 0x80001AE4-0x80001B00, 0x80001EAC-0x80001EB0, 0x80001EE4-0x80001F00, 0x800022C8-0x80002300, 0x80002700-0x80002740, 0x80002FC0-0x80002FE0. Also free in the main text: 0x8020251C-0x80202524 (after motionlookup / wallnodizzy / runhit).

## More space: `bigspace` (12 October 2026)

- The cave (text section 2, 0x80001800-0x80002FE0) is nearly full (~200 bytes in small gaps) and cannot grow (exception vectors below, the game from 0x80003000).
- **Memory layout (retail DOL):** text 0x80003100-0x80296D60, data 0x80296D60-0x80319A00, bss 0x80319A00-0x80441128 (sdata / sdata2 sections 0x8043F7E0-0x804424C0 inside it), **main thread stack 0x804424C0-0x804624C0** (stack info stored by OS code near 0x80271Fxx: 0x804624C0 / 0x804424C0, stack end magic 0xDEADBABE). **OSInit arena start:** `OSSetArenaLo` = 0x80272E3C (variable −0x7A08(r13)), `OSGetArenaLo` 0x80272E2C, `OSGetArenaHi` 0x80272E24, `OSSetArenaHi` 0x80272E34. At 0x80271C08 boot info +0x30 (arena lo) = 0 → __ArenaLo **0x804624E0** (0x80271C14); then if boot info +0x30 = 0 and the BI2 debug flag (pointer −0x6F14(r13)) < 2 → stack end rounded to 32 = **0x804624C0** (0x80271C4C). Arena end: boot info +0x34 or 0x81780000. The game's main heap (`FUN_8000ebd4`, OSInitAlloc 0x80272D48, OSCreateHeap 0x80272DB8) takes the whole arena, so it is what shrinks.
- **Patch `bigspace` (needs the full cave):** appends DOL **text section 3: file 0x31A200, 0x40000 bytes (256 KB) at 0x80462500-0x804A2500**; both arena constants → 0x804A2500 and the boot-info test (0x80271C10) skipped, so the area can never be in the heap. Emulated: arena start 0x804A2500 in all three boot-info / debug-flag cases (retail: 0x804624C0 / 0x804624E0 / boot info value). `--revert bigspace` restores the file exactly (refuses while the area holds anything). `cave` revert refuses while bigspace is on.
- **Patch `bigtest`:** like `cavetest` (same hook 0x801FBF6C at the start of health regeneration, so not together with cavetest): jump to 0x80462500, from there to 0x804A24F8 (stwu + jump back to 0x801FBF70). Emulated path 0x801FBF6C → 0x80462500 → 0x804A24F8 → 0x804A24FC → back. In-game: fights only run if the whole area is loaded and not overwritten by the heap. **Tested in-game 12 October on Robert's full DOL** (checked: all his patches + cave + bigspace + bigtest; cavetest shows PARTIAL/MIXED in --status only because bigtest uses its hook): boot, fights (grapples / strikes, as DMX), music / sound / graphics fine, character select, a cutscene, a story fight, the created fighter's crib, Snowman's clothing store with a shirt preview — all fine with the heap 256 KB smaller. Still to try: 4-player free-for-all, long sessions, memory card save / load.
- Robert's Dolphin OSReport at boot confirms: `Arena : 0x804a2500 - 0x817ff480` (arena end = boot info value). 4-player free-for-all not tried (needs 4 created fighters); treated as fine unless a report comes.
- **`grapplenames` v2 (12 October, needs bigspace):** hook 0x80202FB0 → `b 0x80462600`; code 0x80462600-0x80462634 (same as v1 with 2048 slots and table address); **names 0x80462700-0x80468700 = 2048 slots × 12 bytes → records 2088-4135**. Applying it on a DOL with v1 (now `oldgrapplenames`, 20 slots in the cave) removes v1 (cave 0x80001EB0-0x80001EE4 and 0x80001F10-0x80002000 free again) and carries its names over. Emulated on Robert's DOL: 24 = N05012_A, 2087 = N5401_B, 2088 / 2089 = V0196__A / V0196__B, 3000 / 4135 after setting them = their names, 2090 / 4136 / 0x7FFF = (none); only the hook, the old cave bytes and the new area changed; `--revert grapplenames` works; `blazinown --dol` works with it. **Tested in-game 12 October:** RJ on Budden still has its camera, strike sounds, end voice line, lined-up animation, no crash on repeats — and **face (probably also hand) animations now play**, which were missing when RJ replaced shared value 52 via blazin.viv. Likely cause: the same missing node headers (the face / hand 0x19 nodes are exactly the ones that had none, so the face / hand players could not build them); not proven. A blazin.viv built now (`moves blazin`, library repaired, `extract` / standalone copies headers) should have them too.
- **bigspace area map:** +0x000 bigtest jump (4 bytes), +0x100 grapplenames code, +0x200-+0x6200 grapplenames names, +0x6200-+0x6284 hitstamina (code 0x80468700, values 0x80468778: 0.0, 5.0, K), +0x6300-+0x6388 runstartstamina (code 0x80468800, values 0x80468880: X, 0.0), +0x6400 nowhipbreath strike part (28 bytes), +0x6440 nowhipbreath grab part (36), +0x6480 oldshoveropeface (32, free again once replaced), +0x8000-+0x96AE movename (0x8046A500: settings "MVNM", movenamedy, combody, unnamed mode, enabled; then the C code), +0x9B00-+0x19500 movename work memory (0x8046C000-0x8047BA00: 1536 name entries, 24 KB name pool, 4 popup objects, anchors, pending names; zero in the file), +0x19B00 rbprobe (0x8047C000 settings "RBPN", rbextra float, fightonly; code; 0x8047F000 counter; 0x80480000-0x80497700 heap table), free +0x6500-+0x8000, +0x19500-+0x19B00 and +0x35200-+0x35B00, +0x35B00 rbprof table (0x80498000-0x80499490), +0x39B00 rbprof trampolines (0x8049C000-0x804A0CE0), free to 0x3FFF8, last 8 bytes bigtest.
- Plans for the space: Blazin' value tables (82+), the select list / moveset tables for a bigger roster.

## Stamina and momentum (12 October 2026, read from code)

- **Stamina = fighter +0x844**, scale 0-500 = **5 × the momentum bar** (momentum +0x364, 0-100). **Ceiling = momentum × 5**: the per-frame function `FUN_80223f18` (called from the fight loop 0x801FA2B8 for every fighter, after Blazin' timer `FUN_801fc61c`, momentum decay `FUN_801fc544`, stun recovery, health regen) refills and then always clamps stamina to momentum × 5. HUD packet + 0x14 = stamina / (momentum × 5) (0x801FB368).
  - **Refill per frame** (skipped while +0x939 bit 0x20 = run loop flag): + (Health stat +0xB44 × 0.2 × gamecore `health stamina recovery rate` (0x80304818) + 10) × `stamina replenishment rate` (0x80304700) / 60 × frame step (0x803F0FFC). In Blazin' mode (+0x93E bit 2) stamina = 500.
  - **Spend** `FUN_80223fdc`, called ONLY from the run loop (state 6 sub-state 2, 0x80222734), every frame: − table[Speed stat +0xB3C] × 1.5 × gamecore `stamina spend rate` (0x803046FC); table 0x802E3810 (16 floats) 0.45, 0.5, 0.55, 0.6, 0.65, 0.7, 0.75, 0.8, 0.85, 0.9, 0.92, 0.93, 0.95, 0.97, 0.98, 1.0; no spend in Blazin' (+0x93E & 3). Below 0 → 0 and the loop goes to sub-state 3 (B301 stop).
  - **The start-of-run animation (sub-state 1, B101) spends nothing, and stamina even keeps refilling during it** (+0x939 bit 0x10 is set there, the refill only checks 0x20). Bits 0x30 of +0x939 are cleared on every state change (0x8021BE28).
  - **Run start** `FUN_80207b04` (0x80207C2C): **stamina < 20.0 (0x802DEDB4, i.e. 4 % of a full bar) → state 6 sub-state 4 = out of breath B601 (motion 0x275B)** instead of a run.
  - Round start (0x802066CC) and match setup (0x8020B18C): stamina = momentum × 5 (full).
- **Momentum add `FUN_801fc26c(f, amount)`** (nothing in Blazin' mode, +0x93E & 3): a gain is first × (Charisma stat +0xB4C × 0.1 × gamecore `charisma` 0x8030481C + 0.5) × difficulty scale (player / AI table) × a match-type table (match type 2). **Then a gain refills stamina first**: if stamina is more than 0.1 below momentum × 5, stamina += gain × 5; only the part above the ceiling goes on to momentum (debug texts "MpAdd(P%d) dash stamina ..."). Then momentum += rest (≥ 99 → Blazin' mode, 98 if the fighter is out), floor 20; finally, if stamina was full at the OLD momentum (within 0.1), stamina = new momentum × 5 (a full meter stays full). Losses never touch stamina directly; stamina only drops through the per-frame clamp when momentum falls below it.
- **Hit momentum** `FUN_801fc908(attacker, victim, partner, move, 1, scale)` (from DamageCalc 0x801F9794): attacker / partner gain from the move's meter field via `FUN_801fc0d8`; victim gets move block +0x27 (signed) × scale × repetition factor (`FUN_80204cd8`), −50 instead when the attacker has +0x926 bit 1. Other momentum callers: 0x8021E578, 0x8021F950, 0x802270F8 / 0x80227108, 0x80227968, 0x802312C4 / 0x802312DC (victim state 0x55: −10 victim, +15 attacker), dives 0x80235B10-0x802370C0.
- **Health damage** goes only through `FUN_801fc808(f, flags, f1 = change)` called at **0x801F9580** in DamageCalc (all strikes, grapples, dives, crowd etc. go through DamageCalc); the other two callers (0x801FBFE0, 0x801FC0C0) are regeneration. Flag bit 2 (move `2c` 0x08000000) skips the health change. **Max health** (`FUN_801fbe48`) = 176 + Health stat × 16 × gamecore `health base amount` (0x80304810).
- **Momentum decay** `FUN_801fc544`: every 30 frames (timer +0x368) momentum −= 0.5625 × `momentum expiry rate`; continuous dt / 24 × rate while +0x924 & 0xC000; none in Blazin' mode or with +0x926 bit 0x20.
- **Patches built 12 October (emulated, not yet tested in-game):** `hitstamina` (+ `hitstaminarate=K`), `runstartstamina` (+ `runstartrate=X`), `momentumdirect` — see the patch table. Emulated on retail and on a DOL with Robert's patch set (cave, bigspace, grapplenames, ownblazin, runcancel, runstartattack, annrunaway, annmomhigh, limbtank, nolimbweak, rungrab, gamecorex, songlist, charlist, outfits, unblockflag, unparryflag): no overlap; `--revert` of the three restores the file byte for byte. Results: momentum add retail (mom 50, stamina 125, +10) → (50, 175), with momentumdirect → (60, 125); (50, 125, +40) → retail (65, 325) / patched (90, 125); a loss from full stamina is clamped to the new level by the next per-frame call (250 → 200 at momentum 40). hitstamina K 0.8: 20 damage → stamina 400 → 320; a no-damage move (flag 2) → unchanged; overkill → 0. runstartstamina X 1.5, Speed 7, spend rate 2.25: start-of-run frame 300 → 295.95 (loop cost 2.7 × 1.5); stamina 2 → 0 and sub-state 3, handler epilogue; Blazin' mode → no cost; refill during the start-of-run 0.229 / frame retail → 0 patched (standing unchanged).
- gamecore globals confirmed: 0x803046E4 momentum expiry rate, 0x803046FC stamina spend rate, 0x80304700 stamina replenishment rate, 0x80304810 health base amount, 0x80304814 health recovery rate, 0x80304818 health stamina recovery rate, 0x8030481C charisma.

## Out of breath, hold shove into the ropes (12 October 2026, read from code)

- **Out of breath = run state 6 sub-state 4 (B601 = motion 0x275B) then 5.** The run handler sets +0x91E bit 0x100 ("running") in EVERY sub-state, and the whip strike check accepts opponent state 6 whatever the sub-state, so B601 counted as running for whip strikes (state 0x49) and whip grapples (FUN_80226ab8: whip attempt = +0x91A bit 4; its catch, 0x80226CA8, again needs the target's bit 0x100, distance < 11.0 (0x802E3E78), angle < 30 (0x802E3E7C)). Patch `nowhipbreath`. A normal strike on a fighter in B601 still hits a "state 6" victim, so the hit reaction's forced knockdown for running victims applies (unless `runhit*` / `nokd06`). Sub-state 3 (B301 stop) still allows whip attacks.
- **Hold shove chain:** shover state 0x34, victim 0x35 (plays the shover's motion + 1, e.g. Wrestling pushes F423 / F425 / F427 → F424 / F426 / F428), then **state 0x36 = shove stagger** (handler 0x8022CD3C; motion `FUN_8022d438` = table 0x802E44E8 [shover +0x458][victim +0x45C]: row 0 F444, F444, F444, F441; rows 1-2 F444, F441, F441, F441). +0x91E bit 0x100 is set here too (so a shove-staggered fighter can be whip-struck). Sub-state 2 per frame: edge check `FUN_8022d520` → `FUN_802209a0(f, 2)` (only while +0x46C bit 8 is clear and frame > 1), wall check `FUN_8022d4a8` (state 0x1B), wall-stick roll `FUN_802371f8(f, +0x934)`.
- **Animations (root motion from the viewer data):** F441 (10245, 69 frames) = forward stumble, +40 units forward (Robert's "back to the thrower" shove stagger); F444 (10248, 61 frames) = backward stagger, −33 units, facing forward ("facing attacker"); F445 (34 frames) −18; F204 (50 frames, "long back lean into pushed forwards") hardly moves, no turn; F201 / F202 / F203 barely move.
- **Edge / crowd / rope push-back `FUN_802209a0(f, mode)`** (mode 0 standing, 1 running, 2 shove stagger, 3 / 4 others): edges in +0x898 (points, 12 bytes each), +0x8B0 (normal angles, pointing into the ring), +0x890 (edge ids, table *0x803666F4, 0x34 bytes each, +0x18 type: 8 → crowd weapon state 0x74 under some angles); `FUN_80221378` sets +0x8B8 = direction edge → opponent when within 60° of the normal (returns 1 → +0x920 bit 8), else the normal. Result = **state 0x5F** with +0x920 bits: 1 two edges (corner), 2 from a shove (mode 2), 4 running (mode 1), 8 opponent-facing. Angle helper `FUN_802012f0(a, b)` = atan2(dx, dz) of b − a in degrees (0 = +Z, 90 = +X).
- **State 0x5F** (`0x802215F0`): sub-state 0 picks F204 (0x27EA) for a shove (sets +0x46C bit 4), F203 (0x27E9) when running (clears bit 4), else F201 / F202 by |facing − normal| > / ≤ 90 (bit 4 clear / set); `FUN_802334a8` case 0x5F turns the fighter over 6 frames to face +0x8B8 (bit 4 set) or +0x8B8 + 180 and places him 8.0 from the edge point (`FUN_802343d8` applies +0x870 per frame for +0x388 frames). Sub-state 1: with +0x920 bit 8, after frame 34, turns toward the opponent (bit 4) or away at 2°/frame. At the end (0x80221774) **+0x46C bit 4 is toggled: set → F444, clear → F441**, then state 0x36 sub-state 1 (+0x934 = 4, +0x46C bit 8 = no second push-back). So after a SHOVE into the ropes the victim always gets F441, the forward stumble (toward the ring / shover according to the turn above; Robert sees the victim come off the ropes with his back to the shover when he hit them facing the ropes, which the code reading does not explain). Patch `shoveropeface`: shove case → F444 and facing = +0x838 (angle to the opponent) at that moment.
- Patches built 12 October (emulated, not yet tested in-game): `nowhipbreath` (sub-states 2 / 3 → whip strike 0x49, 4 / 5 → light strike 0x45; whip grapple flag only for sub-states < 4), `shoveropeface` (shove: F441 → F444 and facing set to the opponent angle; crowd / rope push-backs without a shove unchanged). Both revert byte for byte, no overlap with Robert's patch set.
- **In-game result (12 October):** `nowhipbreath` works (no whip attacks on the out-of-breath animation). The first `shoveropeface` (now `oldshoveropeface`) only changed the CROWD push-back (state 0x5F), which was already fine in retail; the crowd shove then gave a flinch-like stagger and idle. **The ring ropes are a different path:**
- **Ropes = rope rebound state 8** (handler 0x80223228), entered from the wall-stick check `FUN_802371f8(f, type)` (0x802375A4) when the stagger meets a rope object (object flags 0x80000) head-on (|dot| > 0.707, 0x802E562C); it sets +0x920 bit 1 ("pushed") and +0x46C bit 4 = 1 when the type has bit 4 (0x802375C0 `andi. r0,r11,4`) or the hit side f31 ≥ 0 (back to the ropes), else 0 (facing the ropes). Types: 1 short stagger (0x80230298), 2 long stagger (0x80230524), 16 holds / clinch (0x8021ED9C, 0x80227CC8, 0x8022A194), shove stagger 0x36 = +0x934 = 4, or 8 when +0x46C bit 1 (0x8022CDE8). State 8 sub-state 0: pushed → **F111 (0x27E6, bit 4: comes off facing the inside of the ring) or F110 (0x27E5: turned round, back to the inside)**, not pushed (running into the ropes) → F101 (0x27E4) and back to the run; alignment `FUN_802334a8` case 8 → `FUN_802340d4(f, 1, rope object +0xAC0, angle offset 0 (bit 4) / 180, 5.0)`. End: +0x46C bit 4 toggled → F444 (set) / F441 (clear), state 0x36, +0x934 = 4, +0x46C |= 0x10. So F110 → F444 = backward stagger with the back to the shover (Robert's case: shove type 8 facing the ropes), F111 → F441 = forward stumble facing the ring. **Patch `shoveropeface` (2nd version): 0x802375C0 mask 4 → 0xC**, so shove types 4 and 8 always take F111 → F441. Emulated: types 4 / 8 → F111 both sides; types 1 / 2 unchanged (facing the ropes still F110 → F444, the same back-turned result for strike staggers into the ropes).

- **Robert:** HARLEM HEIST with D259 and PERPETRATIN' ARMBAR with D257 look perfect (PERPETRATIN' no longer snaps into place). HARLEM HEIST still crashed; the victim gets up much earlier than DMX when the hold ends; a camera rumble at the end of the let-go animation.

Fixed in `main_all_v2.dat` (= main_all_leadins.dat + the library's `waza` / `tick_waza` for 827 / 828 / 831 / 832; 9 bytes). Lesson: after a library fix, run `vport ... --update` (or apply the fields) on the installed main_all.dat.
- **Submission end parts (read from code, attacker state 0x41 `0x8023445C`, hold flags +0x922):** part 3 (tick record `3a` + 2 / + 3) = **the attacker lets go** (+0x922 bit 1: grapple button 0x100 pressed, or `FUN_8021E034`); part 4 (+4 / +5) = **submission win** (bit 8; sets victim +0x932 bit 2); part 5 (+6 / +7) = **the victim breaks free** (bit 0x10). Retail part-5 attacker animations start with a Punch_Kick (the victim's elbow / kick).
- **Lengths attacker / victim (frames):** HARLEM HEIST part 3 136 / 76 (victim free 60 frames = 1 s earlier; retail let-go gaps 0-40: SPOON FED 121 / 81, CUBAN NECKTIE 78 / 60, GET BENT 42 / 32), part 5 76 / 66 (like retail, e.g. GET BENT 42 / 32, GHOST RIDAH 89 / 56). Same clips in Vendetta. Open: Robert to say which ending he meant; options: speed DMX's let-go up (FZUI `h` of V05283A), slow the victim's (V05283B `h`), or let DMX act earlier.
- **Camera:** HARLEM HEIST's let-go V05283A had `135,0,0,after,0,0` (from the port's retiming; GET BENT's N33033A has no entry), while every other let-go ends with `back` (SPOON FED N33143A 100 of 121, GHOST RIDAH 64 of 66, PAIN KILLER 40 of 48, PERPETRATIN' 39 of 48, FOR MY DOGS 31 of 38). Changed to `112,0,0,back,0,0` in `camera_v2.xml` and in the library (likely the rumble; not yet tested). Other shake sources in HARLEM HEIST: part 4 `19 shake L` (submission win, as retail), the hold loop `62 shake S` (every loop, as retail), and part 5's Punch_Kick + command 62 at frames 0-1 (Vendetta's hit at frame 1).

## Hard-grapple turn-around (14 October 2026)

- **Check** (`FUN_80228B10`, returns 3 at 0x80228B44): +0x920 bit 8 clear, +0x91A bit 0x1000 clear, input 0x40 in +0x43C, hard grab (+0x91A bit 2) and **style mask +0xBCA & 0x0C** (bit 2 / 3 = main Submissions / Wrestling; the same bit order as in the WPAR notes: bit 3 = Wrestling, bits 4 / 9 / 14 = Martial Arts). So in retail only fighters whose MAIN style is Submissions or Wrestling can turn the opponent around; hybrids do not count.
- **Turn animation** (state code 0x80227A68): +0x920 bit 0x10 (set by the Blazin' start when a rear Blazin' is chosen from a front hold, 0x80228E6C) → E103 (front → back) / E105 (back → front) for everybody; otherwise grapple class +0xBC8 & 0x10 (SB / MA) → E107 / E109, else E111 / E113. **Checked with the viewer joints:** E103 starts from D201 (SF / KB hold, 0.06), E107 from D211 (SB / MA, 0.04), E111 from D221 (WR, 0.12); all three front → back turns end in the rear hold D301 (0.1); back → front: E105 → D201, E109 → D211 (2.0), E113 → D221. Robert's viewer note "E105 / E106 Street Fighting turn, probably not possible in retail" fits: the SF / KB turn is only used before rear Blazin's.
- **`hardturnall` (built, not tested):** mask 0x0C → 0x1F at 0x80228B38 (any main style), and the turn animation = 0x27D8 / 0x27DA + 4 × grapple type (+0xAFC: 0 SF / KB → E103 / E105, 1 SB / MA → E107 / E109, 2 WR → E111 / E113) at 0x80227AA4 / 0x80227ABC, so SB / WR fighters get exactly the retail animations. File for Robert: `start_hardturn.dol` (his modded_root start.dol + this patch, 40 bytes). The CPU may also start using the turn with the new fighters (not checked).

## Rear hard hold for everyone; front + rear Blazin's (14 October 2026, evening)

- **Robert's test of `hardturnall`:** DMX can turn the victim around, but then does not wait in the rear hold: he plays his rear hard grapple move (GET AT ME) at once.
- **Cause (`FUN_80228290`):** at a hard grab from behind (+0x91A bits 0x200 and 2) the style mask +0xBCA & 0x13 (main KB / SF / MA) is tested at 0x80228318: set → move lookup 0x23 / 0x24 straight away; clear (main SB / WR) → rear hold loop D301 (0x27C8). A second identical test at 0x802288E8 forces input 0xB00 (= play the move) while in the rear hold. A light grab from behind always plays the move at once. **`rearholdall` (built, not tested):** both masks → 0 (`andi. r0,r0,0`), so every fighter holds from behind like SB / WR fighters. File: `start_rearhold.dol` = `start_hardturn.dol` + rearholdall (2 bytes).
- **Front + rear Blazin's need no new code:** with `ownblazin` v2 (already in Robert's DOL) the front hold reads own-bank WWAZ slots 31-34 and the rear hold slots 38-41 (one per right-stick direction), and an empty slot (0xFFFF, 0, 0, as in every retail record) falls back to the fighter's WPAR Blazin' value. So up to 4 front + 4 rear Blazin's per fighter are possible with main_all.dat edits only (a rear Blazin' in a front slot is reached through the turn-around, a front one in a rear slot through the back → front turn). DMX had DISHRAG in all eight slots (the 12 October test filled 31-34 and 38-41), so it was his only Blazin'. `main_all_v3.dat` = main_all_v2 with DMX's (record #76) slots 31-34 cleared: front hold → his WPAR Blazin' (Havoc's value), rear hold → DISHRAG. `blazinown --dirs` chooses the directions; which set (front / rear) it fills still has to be made selectable (it fills both).

- **Tested in-game (14 October, evening):** with `rearholdall` + `hardturnall` DMX keeps the rear hold and does GET AT ME with P and PUMMEL HORSE with K (rear hard grapple slots 36 / 37; slot 35 = rear light grapple; all fighters have these three).
- **Tools (14 October):** `moves blazinown ... --holds front|rear|front,rear` (default both, as before) chooses which hold's slots get the Blazin'; new **`moves blazinslot djv2.sod main_all.dat out.dat --fighter X --holds front|rear --dirs ... (--use "NAME" | --clear)`** puts a Blazin' already installed for that fighter into other slots, or empties slots (→ WPAR Blazin' value). `charstats` now also accepts a unique start of an added fighter's internal name (`"DMX"` for "DMX alt fmzeyryx"). `charstats wwaz` (15 October) shows a filled Blazin' slot like any move (e.g. `826* REPRESENTIN' JERSEY anim 143 V0196__A g2088`) and an empty one as the shared Blazin' it falls back to (`(empty) -> 59 Joe Budden's Blazin' table2 #201, rear`; shared Blazin's have no move name in main_all.dat, so djv2.sod's owner name is used); labels say front / rear hold and stick direction. Animation names come from `--mnl`, else from Start.dol (`--dol FILE`, default a Start.dol / start.dol next to main_all.dat, `--nodol` off), which also names grapplenames records 2088+.

## FFNY's sound engine in the emulator; FFNY samples in the timeline (14 October 2026)

- **Files:** `audio/sfx0.viv` = `sfx/DefJam2_eventsys.csi` ("MOIR": named events, 33 + 76 + 125 records), `DefJam2_eventsys.a1c` ("A1CL": the game events → `A1cp...` / `A1cb...` / `A1ce...` sub-events), and **ABKC banks** (`WCommon1/2`, `Fighter_{Small,Med,Big}_Hits_{Ring,Out}`, `Grunts_*` (9 voices), `Spec_*`, `Chops`, venue banks `BsmntSFX` .. `SbwaySFX`, crowd `cmed` / `csmall` / `crlarge`, `cctrl`, `cmomets`, `randomr`, `SumPts`, `NIS_KO_*`); sfx1 = venue ambience streams (`.ast` + small `.abk`), sfx2 = more venue streams, sfx3 = front end. **ABKC banks contain compiled PowerPC code for their sound modules** (copied to memory and called by the engine) plus a `BNKb` v5 sample bank (same PT header format as Vendetta: 0xA0 codec 0x12 = DSP ADPCM, mostly 16000 / 18900 / 22050 Hz, 1087 samples), so the module graphs cannot be read like Vendetta's; the engine has to run.
- **Engine calls (FFNY Start.dol):** audio init `0x800100E0(0, cfg)` (cfg from `0x80010054`, game values 0x40000, 0x32000, 2, 0x10000, 0x20, 8, 0x7D00), event-system files `0x801F2E88(csi)` / `0x801E0FA4(a1c)` (normally via `0x800104C0(path_csi, path_a1c)`), bank `0x801DE218(bank, 0, 0, alloc_cb)` (returns the bank id; `0x800106A0` does it from a path), event handles `0x801F2A58(handle, desc)` with the game's descriptor structs (pairs taken from the init list at 0x80026C44: handle 0x8036259C + 8n, descriptor 0x8030D038 + 8n), event `0x801F327C(handle, &values[0], &out)`, the animtags dispatcher `FUN_80035394(ev)` (ev: s16 command, s16 values at +2.., s16 fighter at +0x18; values are clamped and passed in order), the audio thread loop `0x801E4088` (one pass per frame when flag 0x802FF768 bit 0 is set), sound-object state machine `0x801DD3CC` (play handlers table 0x802FE7E4: type 0 = bank sample `0x801DCFF8` → **`0x801DFCB4(bank handle, sample index, params)`**, types 1 / 2 = streams `0x801DE79C`), sample table *(0x803DE878) (12 bytes per bank handle → BNKb). Params: +0x0C u16 = playback rate × 4096 (random pitch per play), +0x08 s16 = pan (whoosh pairs ±4000).
- **Emulator rig (`ffny_sound_engine_scripts.zip`: frig.py, frig2.py, rig3.py, sweep.py, ffdata.py):** unicorn with the retail DOL; stubs: OSReport-like 0x80283B20, ARAM check 0x8025656C, AI clock 0x802560A4, **AXInit 0x802581E4**, OSResumeThread 0x802772F0, ARAM upload 0x801E6000 / poll 0x801E6054 (instant), `sc` → nop, game allocators 0x8001F174 / 0x8001F498 / 0x8001F4E0 → bump allocator; MMIO 0xCC000000 mapped (0xCC005016 = 1). Hook on 0x801DFCB4 records every sample start (bank, index, rate). Events played: 0.04 s each, every sound starts on the first engine frame.
- **Results:** Punch_Kick value 2 = size → `Fighter_Small/Med/Big_Hits_*` (1 / 2 / 3), value 1 = punch / kick, two layered samples per hit; Feet / Falls / Hand_Slap / Omni_Event / Environmental_Smash come from the venue bank; Whooshes = WCommon1 (stereo pair), Land / Bone_Crunch / Body_Contacts / Face_Hits / Foley / Muscle = WCommon2; Grunts / Grunts_cycle = the fighter's voice bank. Sweep: 257 value sets (all used in retail + Robert's animtags.dat at least twice, plus the timeline defaults and the Vendetta-conversion values) × 22 venue banks (ring hits) + outside hits + 9 voices, 8 trials each (the engine picks samples at random) → `ffsnd.json`.
- **Timeline update:** both viewers' FFNY lanes now preview FFNY's own samples (random variant per play, with FFNY's pitch), with selectors for the venue bank, ring / outside hits and the voice; values not in the table use the closest known values of the same event (the hint says so); events FFNY plays nothing for in that venue stay silent; Vendetta's sample of the same kind is the fallback when FFNY's is unknown ("FFNY samples" switch).

## Parry window and `catch random rate` (15 October 2026, read from code)

- **gamecore tags → globals** (parser `FUN_8020cca8`): `block start frame` 0x80304820, `block end frame` 0x80304834, **`block counter frame` 0x80304848 (= the parry window table)**, `block invalid frame` 0x8030485C (5 floats each, order SF, KB, SB, WR, MA = defender's main style +0xAE4), **`catch random rate` 0x803048EC** (tag string 0x802E0050, store 0x8020DE3C), `fighting combo link time` 0x803048E4, `scorring combo link time` 0x803048E8, `pressure k1 rate` 0x803048F0, `pressure parameter` 0x803048F4 (6 floats).
- **`catch random rate` is read in exactly two places, both parry checks** (no other code reads 0x803048EC): strikes in `FUN_80231128` at 0x802312F8-0x80231368 (victim state 0x4E parry stance, not hit from behind (+0x918 bit 8), not attack flag 0x400; then `FUN_8021f144` checks the attacker's state), and dives / rebounds in `FUN_8021f1d8` at 0x8021F234-0x8021F2A4 (→ state 0x51).
- **Formula:** W = (attacker FZUI `e` + `block counter frame`[defender main style]) × (1 − R × `catch random rate` / 100), R = `FUN_80201518` (game random, uniform 0 ≤ R < 1; 100.0 at 0x802E1D30 / 0x802E4E20). **The parry succeeds only if the defender's time in the parry stance (+0x878: set to 0 when the stance starts at 0x8021F364, + frame step every frame at 0x8021F380) is ≤ W**; otherwise the hit lands normally. So W = how early before the hit the parry may be entered; the rate randomly shortens it by up to rate % on each hit.
- **Attacker +0x328 = FZUI field `e`** of his current animation (5 bits; FZUI decoded into +0x2BC by `FUN_801faba0`, `e` at +0x6C). `e` is 0 in all 2125 table-1 and 873 table-2 records of Robert's main_all.dat, so in practice W = block counter frame × (1 − R × rate / 100). (`e` could be used to give single moves a longer / shorter parry window.)
- **Effect of the value:** 0 → fixed window (no randomness); 20 (Robert's file; retail value not checked) → W between 0.8 × and 1 × the window; 100 → anywhere from 0 to the full window; **above 100 the factor goes negative for R > 100 / rate, then no parry is possible at all** (e.g. 500: 80 % of hits unparriable, the rest with a tiny window) — matches Robert's memory of high values disabling the parry. **Negative values make the window longer** (−100 → 1-2 × window), so very low values should not disable parries unless something else is involved. Chance that a parry entered t frames before the hit works: min(1, (1 − t / window) × 100 / rate) for t ≤ window.
- Robert's current values: block counter frame 11 / 12 / 11 / 11 / 13 (SF / KB / SB / WR / MA), catch random rate 20 → e.g. SF window between 8.8 and 11 frames.

## Ported moves in the editor's "any move in the game" list (15 October 2026)

- The library JSON `moves` catalog (built from retail banks) has no Vendetta ports, so after DMX's bank was pruned UNLEASHED could not be given to anyone from the GUI. **`Project.full_catalog()`** now adds, per opened main_all.dat: (a) every move a fighter's slot uses whose animations are in the library but not in the catalog (with follow parts from `ffny_prune.keep_set`; shown as "added move (fighter)"); (b) every entry of the JSON `vendetta` section that is installed: a NAME-table move with that name and a frame-data record 2088+ that Start.dol (grapplenames) names after its first animation, even when no slot uses it (slots for the group filter = the template move's slots; shown as "added move (Vendetta port, like TEMPLATE)"). Blazin' ports stay out (`moves blazinown`). `add_library_move` passes this catalog to `moves add`. Camera / sounds follow automatically (keyed by the record names). Tested: with no slot using move 834, Snowman slot 95 ← UNLEASHED (V0445__A copied, global 2122, lookup OK); FOR MY DOGS / HARLEM HEIST / PERPETRATIN' ARMBAR listed for hard-grapple slots.

## UNLEASHED vs FALLIN' ROUNDHOUSE (15 October 2026)

- Robert noticed FFNY's FALLIN' ROUNDHOUSE (move 204, N1137AA, global 965; slots 66 / 68 / 70 / 92 / 93, e.g. Fam Lay, Rollins) looks like Vendetta's UNLEASHED. In Vendetta they are **two separate moves**: UNLEASHED (move 445, skill clip 1496, 80 frames) and FALLIN' ROUNDHOUSE (skill clip 1412, 111 frames; = FFNY N1137AA, moves-viewer distance 3.35). The moves viewer put N1137AA as UNLEASHED's nearest FFNY animation at 16.79 (its fixed 32-sample comparison is thrown off by the different lengths).
- **Time-warped joint comparison (Vendetta clips decoded from Vendetta_Moves.html, forward kinematics in Python; `cmp/vload.py`):** same move shape: run-up, jumping spin roundhouse with the right leg (peak foot height 21.1 vs 22.2, body turned ~254° vs ~262° at the kick), left leg follows, landing on the floor (hip ~2), ~27 / 25 units forward, 4.5 / 8 sideways. UNLEASHED's frames 0-80 match FALLIN' ROUNDHOUSE frames 0-79 (mean joint distance 4.3; TWO FOOTED FURY for comparison 7.3); UNLEASHED kicks ~10 frames earlier (right foot peak 26 vs 36). FALLIN' ROUNDHOUSE's frames 80-111 are the get-up (total turn 350° vs 275°); UNLEASHED ends lying down.
- FFNY data: FALLIN' ROUNDHOUSE FZUI 965: active 36-58, invincible 30-80, **f10 none** (the fighter never enters the floor state; the get-up is in the animation), h / i / j 100 / 110 / 110; WAZA 00 18, 02 6, 03 62, 04 7, 2a 11, 2c 0x30002800. UNLEASHED port (move 834, FZUI 2122): active 26-34, invincible from 23, **f10 50** → floor state + shared get-up (the long recovery Robert saw), h / i / j 110 / 120 / 100; 2a 14, 2c 0x30002000.

## What the three fighting styles do in a fight (15 October 2026, read from code)

- Decoded styles: fighter +0xAE4 main, +0xAE8 second, +0xAEC third (WPAR 7 = none, read as −1). **Fight code reads the second / third style only in `FUN_8022bb10`** (clinch / grapple-strike type): `FUN_801fbd60(main, h1, h2)` = byte table 0x80300DF8[main × 36 + (h1 + 1) × 6 + 1 + h2] = style-combination record 86-140 (`GameCoreFightStyleToID`), then grapple-strike table 0x802E40D0[record − 86] (fighter record 3 = Ludacris → 141). Other callers of `FUN_801fbd60` (0x80014374, 0x80052F90, 0x80086C84) are front end / created fighters.
- **Every gamecore per-style table is indexed by the MAIN style only** (+0xAE4 readers: parry window `block counter frame`, block frames, `style damage rate` tables, e.g. grapple-move rate at 0x80229E20).
- Style-mask (+0xBCA) tests in fight code: 0x4210 = Martial Arts in any slot (0x80213F54, environment lists 0x80224974 / 0x80224B8C, 0x8023634C = P / K input, wall rebound); 0x2108 = Wrestling in any slot (0x80228A78, rope hot spot grapples); 3 = main SF / KB (reversal animation 0x8021E9CC); 0x1F / 0 = main style checks changed by `hardturnall` / `rearholdall`. **No code tests Submissions (or Street Fighting / Kickboxing) as a second or third style.**
- So DMX MA + WR → MA + WR + SB: combination record 140 → 139, grapple-strike entries [0, 0, 0, 0] for both → **no difference in a fight** (only the select screen). Not checked: AI scripts (ai.viv).
- **GUI (15 October): Fighter tab "Fighting styles"** (main / second / third): writes WPAR bits 0-8 + mask bytes 62-63; optional: the djv2.sod Character words 9 (menu style mask KB 1, SF 2, MA 4, WR 8, SB 16) and 18 (main style bit) of every name using the record (Save then also writes `assets/djv2.sod`); optional when the main style changes: grapple type (WPAR bits 21-22: SF / KB 0, SB / MA 1, WR 2) and grapple class byte 61 (0x08 / 0x10 / 0x20, gender bits kept). Tested here (xvfb): DMX → MA + WR + SB (bytes 0x8D.., mask 0x1110, menu 28 / 4), Bo → WR + KB (mask 0x0048, type 2, class 0x21), undo, save.

## HUD, combo popups and move names (16 October 2026, read from code; `movename` emulated)

- **The fight HUD is native code, not Apt:** textures and layout from `assets/misc.viv` `hud.gsh` (`hudF.gsh` French; RefPack, SHPG "G359", 128 shapes, BE; an entry's full name is in its type-0x70 block). Layout shapes are 8 × 8 / 22 × 15 placeholders whose position word holds x / y: **`pos_pN_<item>`** (bars, Blazin' flames, danger, right stick) and **`text_pN_<item>`** (text anchors). Positions (x, y; text anchors get + 15 = shape height, used as the baseline): p1 name (19, 16), totalscore (17, 67), **score (109, 68)**, **combo_name (17, 100)**, danger (58, 31), tapname (16, 124), taphealth (16, 83); p2 the same on the right (x ≈ 620 = right edge, right-aligned); p3 / p4 at the bottom (score y 356, combo_name y 332, i.e. above the score); time (277, 48); 640 × 448 screen. Fonts are TrueType in `fonts/fonts.viv` (TradeGothic, Ferro, ...), so any text can be drawn.
- **Per-player HUD object** (`FUN_800c3b2c` creates it: 0x1204 bytes "PLAYERHUD", pointer table 0x8043A1CC[player]): +0xC90 **7 text items of 0x6C** (text at +0, x +0x40, y +0x44, colour fader +0x48, font index +0x64, scale +0x68), from the item table 0x803BEA44 (16 bytes each: name, default text, RGBA, font; filled by `FUN_800c5094`): name, danger, score, totalscore, **combo_name (+0xE40, font 6)**, tapname, taphealth. +0x1044 = score-points popup, **+0x1070 = combo popup object** (0x174 bytes), +0x11E4 flags, +0x11EC player number (1-4), +0x11FC = HUD hidden (Blazin' scenes etc.). Update `FUN_800bf584` (only when +0x11FC = 0), draw `FUN_800bf264`, create-time init `FUN_800be79c`, free `FUN_800c4d98`.
- **HUD packet** 0x8035EA28 (built each frame by `FUN_801fb22c`), per fighter 200 bytes from +0x18: +0x94 combo flags (from fighter +0x47C), +0xA4 combo hit count (+0x48C), **+0xA8 combo name pointer (+0x490 = combs.xml record + 0x6C)**, +0xAC combo points (+0x494), +0xB0 meter bonus (+0x498).
- **Combo check** `FUN_8021f72c(attacker, victim, waza struct)` (called by DamageCalc for the attacker and a double-team partner, only for hits with param_8 bit 2, and by the taunt code): combs.xml (`assets/combs.xml`, loaded by `FUN_8021fb74` into 0x803F9D90, 200 basic entries × 0x8C + 2 per player) **"skill no" = WWAZ slot numbers**; on progress fighter +0x47C |= 2 (| 4 for chain slots 54-87), on completion |= 8 (+0x10 for basic entries), name / points / meter stored, "COMB SUCCESS" OSReport. The HUD then starts the combo popup: flag 2 without 4 → `FUN_800c0880(popup, name or "HUD_MOVE", hit count, points > 0)`; flag 0x40 → name or "Fighting Combo", flag 1. combs.xml names are loc keys first (`HUD_2HIT` = "2 Hit Combo" in misc.viv `cmusen.loc`), else shown as written (BIG PIMPIN etc.).
- **Popup object** (`FUN_800c0880` start (name, count, flag), `FUN_800c0b04` update, `FUN_800c0ed0` draw, `FUN_800c0acc(popup, anchor text item, player)` init): three text objects (+0x14 count "%d", +0x80 ordinal suffix st / nd / rd / th by `FUN_800c0794`, +0xEC name; each with a colour fader at +0x48 = `FUN_800bd5c0`, scale 1.0 at +0x68), position from the anchor item's x / y / font (+0x40 / +0x44 / +0x64); flag 0 → count + suffix + name (name x + 14), flag ≠ 0 → name only, raised by 12 for players 1-2; right-aligned for players 2 / 4. States 0 (fade in, 5 frames, 0x802EEBF0), 1 (5 frames), 2 (hold, 100 frames, 0x802EEBF8), 3 done (+0x164 visible flag cleared).
- **Waza struct** (the `param_7` of DamageCalc and of the combo check) = fighter +0xF50 (filled by `FUN_8021d4a4(fighter, owner, slot)`): +0 **slot** (≥ 10000 = table-2 move slot − 10000), +4 table-1 move number (−1 for table-2 / Blazin'), +0xC same, +0x24.. the decoded WAZA record (+0xF74 = decoded record start: +0 damage, +4 tank damage, +0x10 limb floats, +0x2C flags `2c`), +0x64 owner fighter, +0x68 fighter whose WWAZ was used. Some callers pass another fighter's struct (parry counter: victim +0xF50 with slot 10055; clinch: +0xFC0).
- **Current motion of a fighter:** +0x80 requested motion id, +0x84 = 1 fighter motion / 0 base00 (table 2), +0x88 local index, **+0x8C FZUI record**, +0x9C frame; `FUN_80202fa0(+0x84, +0x8C)` = its name (N1137AA ..., grapplenames records 2088+ through its hook).
- **main_all.dat NAME tables in RAM** (`FUN_801f9b50`): table 1 count 0x803F5FD8, u16 LE offsets 0x803F5FDC, strings 0x803F5FE0; table 2 0x803F6010 / 14 / 18. (Others: WAZA-1 base 0x803F5FC0, record size 0x803F5FB0; FZUI-1 0x803F5FD0 / size 0x803F5FC8; WAZA-2 0x803F5FF8 / 0x803F5FE8; FZUI-2 0x803F6008 / 0x803F6000.)
- **Game XML / file helpers:** `FUN_80245c10(path, 0x100)` loads a whole file (RefPack aware) into the heap and returns it (0 if missing, no error screen: open `FUN_802486a4` fails cleanly), `FUN_8001f388(ptr)` = heap block size, `FUN_8001f578(ptr)` frees; CTextParser: `FUN_80020b04(parser, path)` open, `FUN_80021258` next tag, `FUN_80021278` value, `FUN_80020be8` close (the combs.xml / gamecore.xml loaders; no end-of-file check, so a missing closing tag loops forever). `FUN_8001fff0()` = "assets".
- **Patch `movename`** (C source `ffny_tools/movename/movename.c`): DamageCalc's combo-check call for the attacker (0x801F97FC) → `mn_hit`: combo check, then the name: movenames.xml entry for the attacker's current animation name, else for the move number (table 1 = waza +4; table 2 = slot − 10000, Blazin' slots 31-34 / 38-41 = 0x8E + WPAR value at owner +0xBB8 + 4 × direction), else main_all.dat's NAME entry ("NO NAME" / empty → nothing, or per `<unnamed moves>` the animation name / "MOVE n"). Same name while still shown, same attacker state and frame not lower → not restarted (multi-hit moves, submission ticks); a new animation of the same move restarts it. The name waits per fighter slot (+0x28) until that player's HUD update: `mn_update` (hook 0x800BF5B8) starts a **second popup object per player** (bigspace) anchored on a copy of the combo_name item moved by `movenamedy`, with flag 1 (name only), and updates it; `mn_draw` (0x800BF4F4) draws it after the combo popup; `mn_init` (0x800BE7DC) sets it up like the HUD sets up its own (zeroed, faders, scales). The four combo-popup starts (0x800BFAB4 / AE0 / B2C / B48) → `mn_combo`: start, then + combody to its y (name y +0x130, anchor y +0x15C; minus for players 3-4). Fight init's gamecore load (0x801F99A0) → `mn_matchinit`: gamecore, then `assets/movenames.xml` parsed (tags = keys, values trimmed; `<? ?>`, `<!-- -->` skipped; stops at `</move names>`; up to 1536 entries, 47 characters). Result for players 1-2: move name at y 103 (where named combos were), named combos at 123, count popups ("1st ...") at 135. **Emulated** with the real popup code (text measuring / loc lookup / text drawing stubbed): parse incl. the generated file, name resolution (NAME table loaded by the game's own loader from Robert's main_all.dat, override by animation / number / table 2 / Blazin', empty entry hides, unnamed modes), popup start / 110-frame run / draw for players 1-3, combo shift, same-move suppression. Not yet seen in-game.
- **Version 2 (16 October, after Robert's in-game test of v1: "looks very good"):** Robert saw table-2 placeholder names pop up: a hold shove = BLOCK, being thrown into a wall = REVERSAL, grapple reversal and environment grapples = BLOCK. Table 2's NAME entries 23-81 are placeholders (REVERSAL / BLOCK; 118+ "KUMIDAGEKI" = Japanese for grapple strike; "no name" lower case = hidden). Constant table-2 slots passed to `FUN_8021d4a4` (code address → t2 move): 0x8022CA48 → 59 (hold shove), 0x80220544 → 44 (wall stick state 0x1B), 0x802376D4 / 0x802319F4 / 0x801FD418 / 0x801FD578 → 58 (wall stick 0x37 etc.), 0x8022DE4C → 48, 0x8022C424 → 34, 0x80231620 → 55 (parry counter damage), 0x8021F578 → 33, 0x8022FB38 → 29, 0x80221988 → 28, 0x80221ED0 → 31 (crowd weapon), 0x80235CB0 / F58 / 0x802361D0 / 0x80236D34 / 0x802370D0 → 56 (missed dive), 0x802070F8 / 0x80236720 / 0x802369C4 → 5 (turnbuckle toss), 0x802322F0 / 0x802325AC / 0x8023283C → 0 / 1 / 4 (weapons), 0x8021E43C → 70 (taunt code). New: `<show keys> yes </show keys>` in movenames.xml makes every pop-up end with its keys, e.g. `BLOCK  [t2-59 I404]` (move number, t2- for table 2, and the attacker's animation), so any pop-up can be renamed; `movenamesxml` now also lists all table-2 moves (names + known uses) and adds the `<show keys>` line to an existing file. dolpatch: the first version is `oldmovename` (replaced automatically when applying `movename`, settings kept). Emulated: keys text, upgrade from v1 with combody kept.
- In Robert's main_all.dat almost all light strikes (chain hits) are "NO NAME"; named are mostly hard strikes, grapples, submissions, Vendetta ports.

## Rollback netcode study (16 October 2026, read from code; Slippi's sources read; nothing built for Dolphin yet)

Robert's question: what would it take to add rollback netcode (like Slippi for Melee) to FFNY in Dolphin? FFNY already runs on normal Dolphin netplay (lockstep) without desyncs, so the game is deterministic for equal inputs.

**How Slippi does it** (project-slippi/dolphin `Source/Core/Core/Slippi/SlippiSavestate.cpp`, `HW/EXI/EXI_DeviceSlippi.cpp`, `SlippiNetplay.h`; project-slippi/slippi-ssbm-asm `Online/Core/*.asm`): not Dolphin savestates (too slow) but **copies of chosen memory ranges** (Melee: data sections, data+bss 0x803B7240-0x804DEC00, 0x8065C000-0x8071B000 and the main heap read from the game's own heap bounds, ≈ 8 MB, minus a list of sound / XFB ranges) taken and restored by Dolphin when the game asks through a custom EXI device (commands 0xB0 online inputs, 0xB1 capture savestate for frame N, 0xB2 load savestate + "preserve blocks" the game wants kept). CPU registers are not saved: capture / load happen at one fixed point of the game's frame loop. The game-side code (Gecko ASM) uses Melee's engine loop, which already runs one logic update per queued pad-alarm input before one render: on a misprediction it loads the state and lets that loop re-run the frames with the corrected inputs (`LoopEngineForRollback`, `ForceEngineOnRollback`), skips new input fetches during re-simulation, logs sounds per frame and kills sounds of frames that were rolled back / prevents duplicates (`Sound/PreventDuplicateSounds` etc.). Max 7 frames (`ROLLBACK_MAX_FRAMES`); time sync by small emulation-speed changes and rare frame skips; ENet UDP; initial RNG seed sent at game start.

**FFNY's frame loop fits the same design (found today):**
- **main()** = `FUN_80003a90`; its loop (0x80004CCC-0x80004DA0): `FUN_800ec074(tick object, 60)` (0x80004CCC), then forever: `while (FUN_800ec144(tick) != 0) { dt = FUN_80021d80(0x8035D618); FUN_800f2094(input system); FUN_800830d4(dt, module manager) = UPDATE; FUN_800cd510 / FUN_800cdd00 (audio messages); FUN_8000e0b8(dt, ...) }`, then `FUN_800838f4` = RENDER once. **So FFNY already runs a fixed 60 Hz logic step with catch-up (several updates, one render), like Melee.**
- **Tick / pad object** (singleton 0x27 from `FUN_80004ec8(0x80319A28, 0x27)`, constructor `FUN_800ec214`): its own thread `FUN_800ec374` wakes per VI, reads the 4 pads (through its vtable +0x24, one 0x94-byte record per port, status 1 none / 2 connected) and pushes a 0x260-byte snapshot (0x10 status + 4 × 0x94) into a ring of 10 at +0 (write index +0x17C4, read index +0x17C0, pending count +0x17C8, mutex +0x1A30); `FUN_800ec144` copies the oldest snapshot to +0x17CC (status) / +0x17DC (pads) for the next update and returns 1, and stops after 5 updates in a row without a render (+0x1A54; must be raised for 7-frame rollbacks; +0x1A4C = half-rate mode). **The snapshot copy in `FUN_800ec144` is the natural input-injection point** (remote / predicted pads in, local pads out).
- **dt is a constant** 1/60 (0x8029AE70, returned by `FUN_80021d80`, which only measures the real frame time into its object). The fight's own step (`DAT_803F0FFC`, used by the animation frame advance `FUN_802015bc`, clock, regen ...) = match object +4 (0x803668C4, 1.0; set only by `FUN_8003adb0` from constants: pause / slow motion) × the Blazin' slow-motion factor. Fight clock `FUN_802037b8` adds step × 1.2 per frame and divides by 60 → the match clock runs 1.2× real time at 60 updates / s.
- **Update / render split:** mode class vtable 0x80315D28 (entries of 8 bytes): Update `FUN_80083d18` (→ `FUN_80022254(dt, game)` → for the fight mode `FUN_80096c04(dt, match 0x803668C0)` → state 8 → `FUN_80098014` → fight loop `FUN_801f9fc4(step)`, fighter update `FUN_801fa18c`, ..., HUD packet `FUN_801fb22c`; plus HUD / camera / particle updates), Render `FUN_80083e80`. Fighters are updated in alternating order every frame (`DAT_803F1014` toggles 0..3 / 3..0) - that is state too.
- **Randomness:** one game RNG, state in bss 0x803F6AC8-0x803F6B50 (32-entry float shuffle table + LCG 0x803F6B48 / 4C; `FUN_80201518`, `FUN_802007dc`, `FUN_8020089c`, `FUN_80200984`), seeded by `FUN_80201498` from fight init (`FUN_801f9980` args). **The time base is read only at match set-up** (`FUN_80095c10` → match +0x302F8 / +0x302F4 seeds; `FUN_80095f8c` / `FUN_80096518` → `FUN_800dba94` (AI seeds); `FUN_8009828c` → `FUN_800b7d38`), and in profiling (`FUN_800c93a8`), speech-system init, EAGL, Apt. **OSGetTime (0x80277A44) is not called by fight code** (only SDK / audio engine / Apt / start-up). So a fight depends only on its starting memory and the pad snapshots - which is why lockstep netplay works. With rollback the two emulators' emulated time stops being identical, so those match-set-up time-base reads must be replaced by a seed agreed over the network (Slippi: CMD_GET_NEW_SEED).
- **Memory:** code + data to 0x80319A00 (data 0x80296D60-0x80319A00, 523 KB), bss 0x80319A00-0x8043F7E0 (1.15 MB: fighters 0x803F1144 + 4 × 0x12F8, match object 0x803668C0 (~0x32B00 bytes), HUD packet 0x8035EA28, RNG ...), sdata 0x8043F7E0-0x804424C0, main stack 0x804424C0-0x804624C0, **patch areas with game state** (cave: runcancel fighter table 0x80002DA0, announcer timers; bigspace: movename / other patch data), heap arena 0x804A2500-0x817FF480 (19.4 MB with bigspace). **Heap** (`FUN_8001f174(name, size, flags)` → `FUN_80249884`; blocks have a 16-byte header: u16 'BM' used / 'BF' free, u16 flags, size, next, prev; heaps 0x8040BD70[flags & 0x3F]; ≤ 0x400 bytes from a small pool 0x802E9B00) - **every allocation has a debug name** (185 names found, e.g. GAME_HUD, PLAYERHUD, Crowd, PARTICLESYSTEM::PARTICLEEMITTER, FXDecalMan, Animation Event System, SPCHSTRM / SPCHBNKMEM / GENRSTRM / ABKRES (speech streaming / banks / audio), Render Subsystem, CRenderSubsystem::Init.m_pEAGLDevice, InputBuffer, SM_Engine), and the heap has **four unused debug hooks**: 0x8040BD60 pre-alloc, 0x8040BD64 post-alloc, 0x8040BD68 pre-free, 0x8040BD6C post-free (called with {name, header, block size, align, offset, flags, pointer, size}; cleared by `FUN_80249cdc`). Must stay out of a rollback state: audio engine memory + stream buffers (DVD / ARAM DMA write them asynchronously; announcer lines are streamed during fights), GX FIFO / XFB / EFB copies, DVD read buffers; static assets (models, textures, animation banks) need no saving.

**First rbprobe results (Robert, 16 October evening):** v1's speed test did nothing in fights: it waited for match object state 8, but the normal fight runs in states 4 / 5 (set in many places; 8 is rare) - **v2 tests the fight loop state 0x803F1000 = 1** (fight running, clock on). The heap table worked: 453 live blocks, 8.95 MB recorded in a fight; big blocks: BaseAnimBank 2.1 MB, SkilAnimBank 2 × (0.66 / 1.0 MB), weapon models and the 284,784-byte main_all.dat copy at **0x7E126000-0x7E5B0000 = FFNY's MMU-mapped virtual memory (ARAM backed)**, so the animation banks are not even in MEM1; crowd model 372 KB (45 blocks), Particles 8 × 44 KB, PLAYERHUD 4 × 4.6 KB, _AsyncLoaderCallback 13 blocks at the top of the arena, assets/misc.viv|pause.gsh, specialcamera 117 KB, fighter skeletons (CSkeleton 6 × 4.5 KB). His two dumps differed in only 1.2 KB of bss and 257 KB of untracked arena, so the game had not run between them (in-game pause or the same frame): the tool now warns about that; dumps must be taken with Dolphin's pause + Frame Advance in a running fight.

**Second rbprobe results (Robert, 16 October, 20:00):**
- **Speed test v2 works:** with rbextra=6 only the playable fight parts run very fast (7 logic frames per render). Without underclock: Dolphin speed 100 %, unthrottled maximum 170-200 % (Babylon, one of the heaviest stages: 130-160 %); it looks "like 30 fps forwards". In the pause menu the speed falls to 85 % (pause menu drawing, unrelated). With the 50 % CPU-clock underclock it looks like 10-15 fps while Dolphin reports 100 % / max 280 %. Reading: the host PC has headroom; the limit is the **emulated** CPU time per 1/60 s - with half the clock, 7 updates + 1 render no longer fit in one VI, so the game draws only every 2nd-4th VI (FFNY's loop then catches up with more updates). Needed next: the game's draw rate (Dolphin's FPS counter, not the speed %) for rbextra 1 / 2 / 3 / 6 at 100 % clock (and maybe a CPU overclock): the highest N that keeps 60 fps is how many frames a rollback can re-simulate without a visible hitch; beyond it a rollback costs one or more dropped draws (the game stays in sync, FFNY catches up by itself).
- **Memory, dumps from a real fight (some P1 / P2 fighting between them):** heap blocks with any change 2.12 MB in total size (changed bytes: (no name) blocks 189 KB, Particles 18 KB, CSkeleton 6 × ~1.2 KB, Particle Timeouts, PLAYERHUD, SparkleAnims, CArena::_LoadXML.m_pObject); the biggest changing blocks are unnamed: 0x80BC8800 (267,472 bytes, 46 KB changed), three ~197 KB blocks with ~35 KB changed each, 91 KB / 29 KB / 26 KB blocks; bss 28.6 KB changed of 1.15 MB; data 0.8 KB; stack 2.8 KB; bigspace 0.4 KB; arena outside the table 514 KB changed (allocated before v2 installed its hooks: probably frame buffers / GX FIFO / render data - not game state). First estimate of one rollback state: data + bss (1.7 MB) + changing heap blocks (~2 MB) ≈ 4 MB per frame, half of what Slippi copies for Melee. Animation banks are in the ARAM-backed virtual memory (0x7E...), static.
- **rbprobe v3:** hooks installed right after main's memory init (0x80003AB4 `bl FUN_8000ed14` → `rb_meminit`), so blocks from boot on are listed (no more untracked arena), and each entry records the allocating game function (first return address outside the allocator); the tool prints "(no name, allocated at XXXXXXXX)". Emulated (table, caller walk on a synthetic stack, tick counting).

**Third rbprobe results (Robert, 16 October, 20:30; v3, no underclock; worst case = fighters far apart, camera zoomed out):**
- **Draw rate (FPS) with N extra logic frames per draw:** Club Murder: N = 1 39-59.9 (mostly 59.9), N = 2 29.9-59.9 (59.9 most of the time with the fighters close together), N = 3 29.9-60, N = 6 19.98-30. Babylon: N = 1 29.9 (short jumps towards 50 with Dolphin's speed dropping), N = 2 29.9, N = 3 25.9-29.9, N = 6 15-23. The draw rate snaps to 60 / 30 / 20 / 15 (a draw waits for the next VI). On Club Murder the rate dips while the next song loads and recovers when it starts (music streaming costs emulated CPU time; Robert would like that fluctuation gone). Pause screen: 50 fps at 80-90 % speed (unrelated).
- **Reading (emulated CPU time per 1/60 s = 16.7 ms):** Club Murder fits draw R + 2-3 logic updates U in one VI, not 4; N = 6 takes ~3 VIs → U ≈ 4-5 ms and R ≈ 6-7 ms of emulated time, i.e. **one logic update costs ~30 % of a frame**. Babylon (heavier drawing) has room for no extra update at all. So with the stock 486 MHz CPU a rollback of 1-2 frames already costs a dropped draw on heavy stages. Two ways out: (1) **Dolphin's emulated CPU clock override (overclock)**: more emulated cycles per VI; normal frames then just idle longer, so the host only pays for actual re-simulation (Robert's PC has 1.5-2× headroom); (2) **cheaper re-simulation**: skip the purely visual subsystems (crowd, particles, camera, HUD, decals, lights) on re-simulated frames and keep their memory out of the state, if they never feed back into the fight - to be measured (a time-base profiling patch could give the cost of each part of the update).
- **Heap map from boot (v3, 573 live blocks, 14.54 MB recorded, untracked arena now only 23.5 KB changed):** unnamed blocks are almost all **files loaded whole** (caller 0x80245CD0 = `FUN_80245c10`; 47 blocks, 2.3 MB, 0.2 KB changed: model / asset files with a few live words) and the MMU virtual-memory loader (0x800F33B4: cmusen.loc, djv2.sod, UserDataMgr, audio (animtags), main_all.dat, banks). **Changing per frame, not game state (leave out of a rollback):** FIFO (GX, 256 KB, all changed), FrameBuffer 560 KB, EAGL::GCBuffer::mbegin 2 × 256 KB (display lists, 6 KB changed), AUDIO::INIT::Aems Stream Buffer 200 KB (161 KB changed), SPCHSTRM 115 KB (56 KB), AUDIO::PathStreamBuffer 160 KB (34 KB), GENRSTRM 2 × 207 KB (32 KB), AudioHeap 256 KB (29 KB), AudioManagedRAM, Fixed MPF Buffer, ABKRES (sound banks / modules), File Sys, ASYNCFILE, bigfile buf, _AsyncLoaderCallback, thread stacks (InputBufferThreadStack, ThreadStack, CRunModuleMan::m_pThreadStack). **Changing, probably game / scene state:** EAGLAnim Memory Pool 256 KB (52 KB changed; animation player pool - state or cache?), Animation Buffer 16 KB, CSkeleton 6 × 4.5 KB, Particles / Particle Timeouts, Crowd structs 73 × 1.1 KB, crowd models (few words), Light Man 241 KB (0.6 KB), PLAYERHUD, SparkleAnims, tCrowdGroup3DInfo, SceneSortArray, InputBuffer / Input (24 / 48 bytes), EVTMAN_LASTGCSI, CParticleSubsystem; bss 37 KB of 1.15 MB, data 0.7 KB, stack 2.3 KB. Callers worth naming later: 0x80113160 (FrameBuffer), 0x801131C0 (FIFO), 0x80113288 / 0x80113360 (GCBuffers), 0x800F6A4C (EAGLAnim pool), 0x8000DE90 (Animation Buffer), 0x800192E0 / 300 / 320 (CSkeleton), 0x801F6000 / 6020 (particles), 0x80093B80 / 0x8008FDE8 (Crowd), 0x80094F30 (crowd model), 0x80010300-0x800112D0 (audio buffers), 0x800267E8 / 0x80026824 (AudioManagedRAM, MPF).
- **Rollback state estimate now:** data + bss 1.7 MB + changing game-side heap blocks (anim pool, skeletons, particles, crowd, HUD, lights, the loaded files with live words ~2.3 MB if copied whole) ≈ 4.5-6 MB per frame.

**Patch `rbprof` (16 October evening, built + emulated):** times every call (164 `bl` sites) made by the main loop, the mode update `FUN_80083d18` / render `FUN_80083e80`, the game-modes update `FUN_80022254`, the match update `FUN_80096c04`, the fight frame `FUN_80098014` and the fighters update `FUN_801fa18c`, in emulated time (time base 40.5 MHz), counted only while the fight clock runs. Each site's `bl` goes to its own trampoline at 0x8049C000 (no stack frame of its own: LR and start time kept in the site's slot, r0 / r11 / r12 / cr0 / XER CA only), slots at 0x80498000 ("PROF", 164, 32; per slot site, LR, start, count, 64-bit total, max, target). Generated by `ffny_tools/rollback/rbprof_gen.py` from the DOL; `python ffny.py rbprof mem1.raw` prints per group: ms per call, ms per logic update and % of 16.7 ms, max, calls per update. The wait at the top of the main loop (0x80004CD4 → `FUN_8024e170`) shows the idle time left per frame. Keystone note: `addi rD, 0, imm` is `li` (rA = 0 means literal 0) - use `addic` to add to r0.

**rbprof results (Robert, 16 October 20:45, no underclock, 111 s of fight, 6697 logic updates):** one logic update = **7.74 ms** of emulated time (46 % of 16.7 ms), the render 8.89 ms (53 %), audio messages 0.05 ms (max 345 ms = a load). Inside the update only ~1 ms was covered: fight frame 0.63 ms (0.96 calls / update; fighters update 0.61 ms: per-fighter 0.4, animation advance 0.16), crowd `FUN_8008dc84` 0.32 ms. The "mode update" groups were empty because **fights do not run the menu mode class**: the fight modules are vtables 0x80315E68 (update `FUN_800862d8`, render `FUN_80086584`) and 0x80315EB8 (update `FUN_80087a00`, render `FUN_80087cc0`), which call match update through `FUN_80086794` / `FUN_80087e6c`, then HUD update `FUN_800c4cc8`, `FUN_800c9380`, `FUN_800d8984`, `FUN_800d8d9c`, `FUN_8000f848`, `FUN_800dd9d0`, `FUN_800ec5b8`, `FUN_800ecf48`, plus `FUN_8002ba78` first. ~6.7 ms per update are still unattributed (to be profiled in the lab below with a sampling profiler or a v2 call tree; `prof2/gen2.py` = trampolines of 8 instructions with a shared tail, LR restored from a constant per slot so re-entry is safe, 450 sites breadth-first from the fight modules; generated, not sent). Correction: the **main loop's back edge goes to 0x80004CD0**, not 0x80004CCC (0x80004CCC `FUN_800ec074` runs once before the loop), so rbprobe's `rb_start` hook ran only once (harmless).

**Lab: FFNY running headless in a Dolphin build here (16-17 October; Robert's modded_root copied with his permission):**
- Dolphin tag 2609 (Robert's 2609-79 = commit e2ad093; State.cpp / savestate version unchanged in between) built with `dolphin-emu-nogui` (Ninja, Qt off), run on Xvfb with Mesa llvmpipe OpenGL, single core, HLE DSP, no audio. The GCRebuilder folder works as a Dolphin **DirectoryBlob**: `sys/boot.bin` = ISO.hdr bytes 0-0x440, `sys/bi2.bin` = 0x440-0x2440, `sys/apploader.img` = AppLoader.ldr, `sys/main.dol` = start.dol, `files/` = the rest (symlinks); boot with `-e .../sys/main.dol`. Speed ~58 VI/s in menus.
- **Harness `FFNYLab`** (Dolphin patch, files in `ffny_tools/rollback/lab/`: `dolphin_ffnylab.patch`, `FFNYLab.cpp/.h`, Python drivers): env `FFNY_LAB=dir`, commands through a FIFO `dir/cmd`, replies in `dir/out`: `run N` (VI fields), `pad P buttons sx sy cx cy l r` / `padoff` (pad override in `Pad::GetStatus`), `shot file.png`, `dump` / `dumpr` / `dumpv` (MEM1, a range, fake VMEM), `save` / `load` (deferred to a VI field when issued inside a hook), `peek` / `poke`, `regs`, `spr N`, `hookloop ADDR` (HLE "Fixed" Start hook at the main-loop top 0x80004CD0; `loops N` counts loop passes; a VI watchdog reports "no main-loop pass for 300 fields"), `script file` (per-loop pad lines), `snap K` / `restore K` (MEM1 + fake VMEM + GPRs / FPRs / CR / FPSCR / XER / LR / CTR / GQRs), `restorer K rangefile` (restore only listed ranges), `track N` (bytes changed between the pre-update hook 0x80004D24 and post-update hook 0x80004D28 vs the rest of the frame), `input rec|play|off|clear` (record / replay the 0x260-byte pad snapshot at tick object +0x17CC keyed by the logic-update counter, injected at 0x80004D24 = the real rollback input point), `storetrace on|all|off|dump` (interpreter only: every CPU store with writer PC, OS current thread (0x800000E4), main-thread-in-logic vs other counts), `hooktrace ADDR` (print LR / r3-r6 / strings at a function), `msave` / `mload` (Dolphin's whole-machine state in RAM, timed). HLE hooks need `JitInterface::ClearSafe()` after `HLE::Patch`. `pkill -f` must not match the shell's own command line (it killed the tool shell twice).
- Menu path scripted from boot: autosave warning A, memory card slot A, A, skip movies A, START ×2 → main menu, RIGHT + A (Battle), A (One on One), A, START on ports 0 and 1, A / A (user IDs), A, A → fighter select (P1 Method Man as Blaze, P2 RIGHT → DMX), A → venue (Foundation), A, skip intro → fight. Savestate `fight0.sav` at fight start (18 MB).
- **Logic-update counter:** 0x80440C24 (+1 in `FUN_8002ba78`, every update). Fighters 0x803F1144, match object 0x803668C0. Tick / pad object = singleton 0x27 at 0x808A81C0 in this session (r28 in the main loop).
- **Store trace of 900 updates of random two-player fighting** (`d/st_all2.txt`): CPU stores touch only ~125k words (490 KB) in total; main-thread stores inside logic updates ≈ 250 KB per 300 updates, mostly EAGLAnim Memory Pool (~70 KB, `FUN_800f866c` / `800f9364` / `800f88e0` / `80100f10` ...), CSkeleton 26 KB + 19 KB in arena outside the table (`FUN_800f3c30`), bss ~10 KB, Particles 10 KB, Crowd 4 KB, HUD / sparkles / lights small; only ~300 words of fake VMEM (0x7E...). Other threads: 0x803E23C8 (audio: `FUN_801e1e04` etc., AudioHeap + bss), 0x804099A0 (file / stream reader: File Sys, Aems Stream Buffer, `FUN_8024ce24` memcpy), the pad thread (InputBuffer, tick object), interrupts (thread 0). **So the real per-frame game state is small (well under 1 MB), smaller than Melee's 8 MB copy.**
- **FFNY's 0x7E000000 "virtual memory" is Dolphin's fake VMEM** (MMU off): a separate 32 MB host buffer, not MEM1 and not the game's page table (the VM library at 0x8028D000, page table 0x804B0000, VM globals 0x80440A98-AA8 stay empty). A rollback state must include the written parts of fake VMEM; MEM1-only copies miss them.
- **Rollback experiments (snap at a loop top, record 30-40 updates with random inputs, run 7-47 updates with other inputs, restore, replay the recorded inputs by counter, compare fighters + match object per update):**
  - Whole MEM1 restore: game deadlocks (OS / thread / hardware state rolled back). Dolphin's own whole-machine state in RAM: 91.7 MB, save 16-68 ms, load from inside a VI event crashed Dolphin → **per-frame Dolphin savestates are far too slow**; a Slippi-style range copy is required.
  - Selective sets (`mkset*.py`): restoring only main-thread logic words crashes in the crowd / animation blend nodes (`FUN_800188b0` virtual call +0x3C → `OSPanic`-style halt `FUN_8025b028`, or a jump into data) because objects are allocated and freed every few frames from the **small-object pool** (≤ 0x400 bytes, pool object at *(0x802E9B00), sizes 0x10 / 0x24 seen, `FUN_8001f498` / free `FUN_8001f120`) whose bookkeeping is shared with other threads. Restoring everything except other-thread words, audio / stream / FIFO / framebuffer / GCBuffer / thread-stack blocks, plus the small-pool words (set9-set13, ~49 MB incl. fake VMEM) gets past the crowd crash, but then **the first update after the restore blocks in `GXDrawDone` (`FUN_8026c68c`, waits for the PE finish flag 0x804408B0) called from the HUD update (`FUN_800c4cc8` → `FUN_800be348` → ... → `FUN_80113ce4`)**: the logic update itself talks to the GPU (font / HUD textures) and the GX library's software state no longer matches the hardware FIFO.
  - Status (16 October): no fully consistent restore yet (superseded 17 October, see below: consistent except sound handles); next: exclude the whole GX library state (`__GXData` and friends) and the OS thread / alarm / mutex structures by symbol rather than by writer address, or keep HUD text generation out of re-simulated updates; then the per-update comparison (note 17 October: the replay was NOT reaching the game - the hook was after the input system; fixed, see below).
- Unknown-instruction panics: `UsePanicHandlers = False` in the lab's Dolphin.ini, or Dolphin nogui blocks on the alert.

**Lab session 17 October (rebuilt in a fresh container; rewind made consistent):**
- **Rebuild recipe** (works from scratch in ~25 min on 2 cores): `git clone --depth 1 --branch 2609 https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin.git`; `git submodule update --init --depth 1` for all except Qt / FFmpeg-bin / mGBA / libadrenotools; then **reset every submodule to its pinned commit** (`git ls-tree HEAD <path>` → `git fetch --depth 1 origin <sha>; git checkout <sha>`; a shallow init checks out branch tips instead); cpp-optparse needs a plain clone + checkout of its pinned sha; bzip2 (gitlab) is unreachable, the system libbz2-dev is used. apt: libxi-dev libxrandr-dev libegl-dev libgl-dev libevdev-dev libudev-dev libasound2-dev libpulse-dev libsystemd-dev libdrm-dev libgbm-dev libxxf86vm-dev. `cmake -G Ninja -DENABLE_QT=OFF -DENABLE_NOGUI=ON -DENABLE_TESTS=OFF -DUSE_MGBA=OFF -DENABLE_VULKAN=OFF -DENABLE_CUBEB=OFF -DUSE_RETRO_ACHIEVEMENTS=OFF ...`, `ninja dolphin-nogui`, `git apply dolphin_ffnylab.patch` (now also contains FFNYLab.cpp/.h). Game folder: `sys/` from &&systemdata (boot.bin = ISO.hdr[0:0x440], bi2.bin = ISO.hdr[0x440:0x2440], apploader.img, main.dol; **no fst.bin**), `files/` = **real directories holding file symlinks** (`cp -rs`; directory symlinks are not followed by Dolphin's DirectoryBlob → the game hangs before the disc header loads). Dolphin.ini: CPUThread False, DSPHLE True, Backend "No Audio Output", SlotA 1, SIDevice0/1 = 6, UsePanicHandlers False. Lab DOL = `start_rbextra0.dol` (rbprobe heap table, rbextra 0).
- **Menu path** is scripted from a title-screen savestate (`tofight.py`): START, (wait) START → main menu, RIGHT A (Battle), A (One on One), A, A, A → Select User ID: START on ports 0 and 1, A / A ("no user ID"), A → fighter select: P1 A (Method Man as Blaze), P2 RIGHT A (DMX), A (outfit 1), A (Foundation), A, wait, A until the fight flag 0x803F1000 = 1. At boot: autosave warning A, slot A, A, autosave NO (DOWN A, DOWN A). One missing START at the title shifts every later press by one menu (it ended in the story-mode name entry once). The memory card file must be deleted before replaying the path, or the prompts differ.
- **Harness fixes / additions:** (1) **the input injection hook was in the wrong place**: 0x80004D24 is *after* the input system `FUN_800f2094` (called at 0x80004D18), so "input play" never reached the game (the replay ran on the live pads; the earlier note "input replay by counter is exact" was wrong). Pre-update hook moved to **0x80004D14** (after `FUN_800ec144` / `FUN_80021d80`, before `FUN_800f2094`) - also the right injection point for real rollback. (2) `rec TAG lo hi ...` / `rec off`: after every logic update the listed ranges are written to `dir/rec/TAG_<counter>.bin` (per-update comparison). (3) `hooktrace` prints the PC, the current OS thread, in-logic flag and a 6-frame back chain. (4) store trace also records LR (memcpy / memset writes are attributed to the caller) and "main thread outside logic" counts.
- **Why rewinds broke (three separate causes, all found):**
  1. **The small-object pool (`*(0x802E9B00)` = 0x8060C0E0, memory 0x8060C3C0-0x806E84E0, 20 size classes ≤ 0x400, mutex at +0x2A4, alloc 0x8028F988, free 0x8028FC10 which forwards non-pool pointers to the main heap) is shared with EA's sound library**: its allocator interface (0x8028F300 alloc(this,size) / 0x8028F324 free) puts sound objects in the pool, allocated on the main thread and freed by the audio thread (0x803E23C8). Sound-library state cannot be rewound, so any pool rewind leaves free lists half old / half new → blend-tree nodes (0x24-byte pool objects, `FUN_800188b0`) reused while live → crash. **Fix: lab patch `rbsndheap`** = 0x8028F300: `mr r3,r4; lis r4,0x802A; addi r4,r4,-0x70F0 ("Audio Subsystem"); lis r5,0x8000; b 0x8001F498` → sound objects go to the main heap (flags bit 31 bypasses the pool; the main heap has its own mutex). After it the pool holds only game objects (all allocated / freed on the main thread inside logic updates); its only other-thread words are its mutex and one OSThread struct (0x806D5A34) that permanently lives in a pool slot. Main heap: no game allocations during a fight.
  2. **OS / GX / SDK words written by the main thread itself** (OSSleepThread in GXDrawDone during the HUD update, thread contexts, alarms, mutexes) were rewound. Fix: exclude words written by system code (SDK/MSL/VM 0x8024E000-0x8028E000, EA snd/spch/AV 0x801CC000-0x801F3000, EA file/heap 0x80243000-0x8024E000; memcpy/memset ranges 0x8024CE24-0x8024D160, 0x80280FBC-0x802811E0, 0x80281600-0x802816CC, 0x8027ECEC-0x8027EDCC resolved to the caller) and static words referenced only by system code (`sysdata.py`, from a scan of lis/addi and r13 (0x804477E0) / r2 (0x804577E0) references).
  3. **Render-side words** (written by the main thread outside logic: EAGL render queues / display-list bookkeeping around 0x802F45C4-0x802F49AC and others) were rewound → the render wrote to the GX FIFO with a stale state and the FIFO-overflow handler suspended the main thread forever (main thread state 1 suspend 1 at 0x801F7C10, r4 = 0xCC008000). Fix: do not rewind render-only words.
  4. **Main-heap bookkeeping** (found by a long stress series: after 5 earlier trials in the same Dolphin session one rewind froze; main thread spinning in the main-heap code at 0x8024A41C, called from the main loop's heap statistics at 0x80004D48, holding the heap mutex with priority inheritance, the audio thread waiting on it). Since `rbsndheap` the sound objects live in the main heap, so a rewind that copies old bytes over arena areas where a sound object was allocated in between overwrites its block header → free list corrupted. Fix: from the heap arena restore **only the contents of live heap blocks (no 16-byte headers, no free space)** plus the small-object pool (0x8060C0E0 to pool end); the game itself does not allocate from the main heap during a fight. Main heap struct = heaps[0] at 0x8060C050 (mutex +0x3C); heaps[2] = 0x7E000010 (the fake-VMEM heap).
- **Restore set** (`mkset.py TRACE MEM OUT noM`): data / bss / sdata / sbss / sdata2, cave, main stack, bigspace, heap block contents + small-object pool, fake VMEM (0x7E000000-0x80000000), minus: heap blocks named AUDIO / Audio / GENRSTRM / SPCHSTRM / SPCHBNK / ABKRES / FIFO / FrameBuffer / GCBuffer / ThreadStack / File Sys / bigfile / Fixed MPF / ASYNCFILE / _AsyncLoaderCallback / Stream / InputBuffer / MCThread / MyThread; static system words; words written by other threads; words written by system code; render-only words. ~49.6 MB of ranges (mostly static loaded files) for the lab's brute-force restore; **only ~200 KB of it is written by the logic** over 1,200 traced updates (EAGLAnim Memory Pool 73 KB, small-object pool 32 KB, CSkeleton 26 KB, weapon models 30 KB, Particles 11 KB, bss 10 KB, Crowd 4 KB, ...) → a real per-frame state can be small.
- **Stress series** (`series.py SET`: 8 trials in one Dolphin session, snapshots at updates 4396-5937, rewinds of 1-7 updates, both random input scripts, 120 replayed updates each): **7 of 8 perfect** (fighters / RNG / logic bss identical for all 120 updates). Trial 6 (snapshot 5336, rewind 7, rand2) froze 2 updates after the rewind, only late in the long session (a fresh Dolphin running the same trial passes, so it depends on thread interleaving).
- **Open problem 5 = sound handles (reference counts)** (theory later found WRONG, see part 2 below; kept for the record): sound objects are reference counted: `0x801F33A4` = Release(obj) (sound lock `0x801F2E30` / unlock `0x801F2E5C`; runs the object's callback list at +0xC, decrements the count at +4, frees through the allocator interface at 0 → `0x8028F324`); the audio thread drops its reference in `0x801F3744` when playback ends; `0x801F327C` (the sound event call) returns a new referenced object (game code at 0x80037B78 creates and immediately releases one: transient, harmless). **Game-side state holds long-lived references**: the game's 3D-voice table in bss (e.g. 0x80437338, written by 0x80036EF4; per-voice positioning in `FUN_80033b18`) and emitter objects in the small-object pool (e.g. object 0x8060D4E0, vtable 0x803154B0, handle at +0x18). A rewind restores "game holds reference" while the counts inside the (not rewound) sound objects keep their later value → after the replay the game releases again → count one too low → object freed twice → main-heap free list becomes a cycle → main thread spins in the heap walk 0x8024A41C (holding the heap mutex), audio thread blocked on it. Needs a sound to end inside the rewound window, hence rare.
  - **Planned fix (rollback-aware sound handles, also needed for real netcode):** (a) **deferred releases**: game-side Release calls (main thread, inside logic) are queued with their frame number and executed only when that frame is older than the rollback window (confirmed); on a rewind to frame S, queued releases issued after S are dropped (the re-simulation issues them again); (b) **speculative acquisitions**: references the game obtained after S (event objects from `0x801F327C` and other acquire / AddRef paths, still to be listed) are released at the rewind, because the restored game state no longer holds them; (c) keep the lab check "no pointer into an 'Audio Subsystem' block in rewound memory except through the tracked handles". Alternative if (b) is too invasive: Slippi-like "sounds are fire-and-forget during re-simulation" plus (a).
  - Also: exclude the rbprobe heap table (0x80480000-0x80497670, lab instrumentation) from the set; it is restored now (harmless to the game).
- **Result so far:** rewinds of 1-7 updates at several fight moments, replay of the recorded inputs, per-update comparison of all of bss (fighters, match object, RNG, pad tables): **fighters, RNG and all logic-owned bss identical to the reference for 120 updates** (see the series below). Remaining differences are expected and harmless: render-written matrices inside the match object (0x803675D0, 0x80372DB0, 0x803955E0..., written by 0x80119B64 / 0x8001A970 / 0x8001B7F4 during the render, recomputed every frame), the main OSThread context, EA file-system word 0x804405B0, audio/stream memory, and the anim-sound event queue at 0x80437558 (`FUN_80034900`, 32 entries of 0x26 bytes copied from the caller's stack, so uninitialised stack bytes differ - also without rollback).

**Lab session 17 October, part 2 (afternoon): rewind made stable, also when rewinding on every frame:**
- **Correction to part 1, "open problem 5":** the sound-handle reference-count theory was wrong (a lab check that keeps any rewound word pointing into a sound object at its current value never fired in hundreds of rewinds). The real causes of the remaining freezes were:
  1. **The main heap is also used by the game logic during a fight** (HUD frees text / popup objects bigger than the pool's 0x400 limit, code 0x800C9EC0 / 0x800CA950 / 0x800CC388; weapons allocate `CWeapon::Make...` blocks), so it must be rewound - but it also held the sound library's objects (even in the unpatched game, sound allocations > 0x400 bytes go there, freed by the audio thread). A free inside the rewound window → after the rewind the game frees again → main-heap free list becomes a cycle → main thread spins in the heap walk at 0x8024A41C. **Fix = lab patch `rbsndheap2`** (replaces `rbsndheap`): at boot, right after the game's memory init (hook at 0x80003AB4, after rbprobe's `rb_meminit`), 512 KB are taken from heap 0 and turned into a **dedicated heap "SndHeap"** with the game's own heap-create function `0x80249D60(index, name, base, size, 0x20, 0x20, 0, 0, [0, 1])`, index from the game's free-index function `0x8024A014` (indices start at 2; **index 1 is used by the game's front-end heap**, a fixed index collided), stored at 0x80498300; the sound library's allocator interface 0x8028F300 → code at 0x80498000: `0x8001F174("Audio Subsystem", size, 0x80000000 | index)`, zero-filled like the original, falls back to heap 0 if SndHeap is full. Frees need no change (pool free forwards non-pool pointers to the heap free, which takes the heap from the block header). Code at 0x80498000-0x80498190 + "SndHeap" at 0x80498200 (bigspace, unused). In a fight all sound objects (≈ 7-20 live, a few hundred bytes) are inside SndHeap. Built by `sndheap.py` / `patchdol.py IN OUT rbsndheap2`.
  2. With that, **the whole heap arena is rewound** (block headers, free lists, free space; minus SndHeap and the FIFO / frame buffer / GCBuffer / audio / stream / file blocks), so heap layout changes inside the window (weapons, HUD) are undone consistently. Heap mutexes (heap struct + 0x3C) and the pool mutex (pool + 0x2A4) are never rewound. EA heap allocator code 0x8024986C-0x8024A600 counts as game code (its bookkeeping is rewound with the heap).
