  3. **DSP-written arrays**: big SDK arrays in bss (e.g. 0x804184C0-0x8041A63C in 0x500-byte pieces, 0x80420020-0x8042CD88; audio voice parameter blocks / DSP structures) are written by the DSP by DMA, so the CPU store trace never sees them, and the code references only their base addresses → they were rewound → after some rewinds the game sent garbage to the controller port ("SI: Unknown command" panic on every poll, emulation nearly stops) or the HUD's `GXDrawDone` (0x8026C68C, via `FUN_80113ce4` from the deferred-free path 0x8001EAEC) waited forever. Fix: **a statically referenced SDK / EA-library object owns the words up to the next referenced address** (cap 64 KB in bss, 4 KB in data), but never inside the match object, fighters or stack (fields of game objects referenced by system code, e.g. match object +0x30 by audio code 0x80039FE8).
  4. **EA file-system state**: the 16 KB buffer 0x803508A0-0x803548A0 that the game's memory init (0x8000EDF8) hands to FILESYS init (0x80244BD4) is file-thread / DMA state → never rewound.
  5. **The game's audio module is game state, not audio-engine state**: the anim-tag event queue at 0x80437558 (`FUN_80034900`: count + up to 32 entries of 0x26 bytes) is filled during the logic update and dispatched later (`FUN_80035394`); besides sounds it drives HUD / crowd effects (e.g. `0x800BCEE0` → `FUN_800a18f4`, HUD popups through `FUN_800c7e48`). Not rewinding it left events from thrown-away frames in the queue (7 entries instead of 2) → different HUD popups. So the audio module (0x80024600-0x8003A000) is rewound like other game code; only the EA sound / speech libraries, their heap and the DSP / stream / file buffers are not.
- **Note: uninitialised stack bytes**: queue entries are copied from a caller's stack struct with uninitialised bytes (bytes 12-23 of an entry often hold stack garbage such as 0x801E7A30 / 0x804622B0). In lockstep netplay both machines have the same garbage; with rollback the stack history differs (thrown-away frames, interrupts at other moments). Rewinding the main stack (0x804424C0-0x804624C0) covers it in the lab; if a divergence ever appears in a field like this, the fix is to zero that stack struct in the caller.
- **Test design fix**: the chain test (`chain.py`) originally reached its start point twice (load + 100 updates, once for the reference and once for the rewind run); inputs then come from the live script, and which update sees a pad change depends on emulation timing (the lab's hooks change it), so the two runs drifted apart without any rewind. Now a Dolphin savestate is taken once at the start point (`chain0.sav`) and both runs start from it; baseline without rewinds: 60 updates, 0 differences.
- **Restore set** now (`mkset.py st4.txt st4.raw setP.txt noM fullheap`, trace `st4` on the patched build): data / bss / sdata / sbss / cave / main stack / bigspace / whole heap arena / fake VMEM, minus system-owned statics (with extents), words written by other threads, by system code (SDK / MSL / VM, EA sound / speech / AV, EA file), render-only words, the system heap blocks, SndHeap, the FILESYS buffer, the rbprobe table, the allocator mutexes; the logic counter 0x80440C24 is always rewound (the lab keys input replay by it). 48.7 MB of ranges in the lab (brute force); the part the logic actually writes is a few hundred KB.
- **Lab savestates**: `title3.sav` (title screen, patched build from boot, blank memory card), `fight3.sav` (Blaze vs DMX on Foundation, fight running, update 4557). Boot path from a fresh card: wait ~1000 fields, A (autosave warning), A (slot A), DOWN A, DOWN A (no autosave, confirm), A, A, START, A, START (movies) → title. From the title: START, wait, START → main menu (one START too many or too few ends in the story-mode user-ID screen).
- **Shared random generators (rollback desync source found by the 1000-frame chain):** two generators are used by everything: generator 1 = `FUN_8001fc88` (6-word state, global at 0x8035D588, wrapper `0x8001FBE8`, 89 call sites: crowd, AI 0x800D7F74 → 0x80290B94, HUD, audio...), generator 2 = `FUN_800b7dac` (32-float shuffle table + LCG 0x5D588B65, object passed in r3; wrappers `0x800B7E90` randint(obj, n), `0x800B7E18` chance(obj, p); global object 0x803BE03C used by crowd 0x8008B494, game logic 0x8020F91C / 0x80211808 / 0x802122EC, audio...). The game's audio module draws from both inside the per-frame 3D-sound update (`FUN_80033b18` → `FUN_80031e10`, looping over the sound library's active voices) and in speech / anim-tag code; the number of draws depends on which voices are still playing, i.e. on audio state that a rollback cannot rewind → after a rewind the fight logic gets other random numbers (first divergence at update 5286 of a 1000-frame chain, fighters from 5472). Lockstep netplay never sees this (both machines have identical audio timing). **Fix = lab patch `rbaudrng`**: the 10 generator-1 calls in the audio module (0x8002C294, 0x8002CB88, 0x800317A0, 0x80031FA8, 0x80033408, 0x80035C60, 0x80039E5C, 0x80039E98, 0x80039ECC, 0x80039F04) → stub 0x804986B0 with a private state at 0x80498690; the 2 generator-2 object loads (0x80031ECC/0x80031ED4, 0x8002F53C/0x8002F544) → private object 0x80498600 (0x88 bytes); private states start as copies of the shared ones (the lab copies them when it applies the patch; a real patch must initialise them at boot).
- **Uninitialised event bytes (lab patch `rbevzero`)**: zeroes r1+8..r1+0x2F right after the prologue of the three audio-module functions that build anim events on their stack (0x80037D0C, 0x8003816C, 0x80039CA4; stubs at 0x80498400+); one more source remains (event type 0x16 with stack garbage in bytes 12-23, from a code path where the struct area is shared with other locals), harmless so far.
- **Results (17 October, evening; build: start_rbextra0.dol + `rbsndheap2` from boot, `rbevzero` + `rbaudrng` poked; restore set `set_final.txt`):**
  - **Rewind on every frame, 1000 frames** (rand1, from update 4959): 1000 rewinds, 3980 wrong-input updates thrown away, 998 replayed updates compared → **fighters, match object (incl. HUD) and RNG identical to the reference every time**, no freeze.
  - **Second run, 1000 frames** (rand2, later in the fight, from update 6061): 986 rewinds, 3887 thrown-away updates, 1001 compared → **identical**.
  - Earlier: 8-trial series (single rewinds of 1-7 updates at different moments, 120 replayed updates each) also all identical.
  - Still different after rewinds, by design (not game state): SDK matrix scratch (0x80421000-0x80422FFF, written by 0x8025A25C-0x8025A650), EA speech-library words, the 3D-sound pan values (0x8043732C..., derived from the sound library's voices), render-only matrices in the match object, the main OSThread context.
  - Lab definition of "working well" reached: rewinds of 1-7 frames anywhere in a fight replay the exact same fight, with no freezes, also when rewinding on every frame for ~1000 frames.
- **Patch `rbstate` (17 October, evening) = the three lab patches made boot-safe for the normal DOL**, in `ffny_dolpatch.py` (generator `ffny_tools/rollback/rollback_patch.py`; needs cave + bigspace, independent of rbprobe / rbprof, both can stay or go). Code / data 0x8049A000-0x8049A3B0 (bigspace +0x37B00, the free gap between rbprof's table 0x80498000-0x80499490 and its trampolines 0x8049C000; the lab's 0x80498000 addresses collided with rbprof). Boot hook moved to **0x80003AD0** (main: `lis r3,0x8032` right after the memory-init call 0x80003AB4 and its failure report; the stub ends with that instruction), so rbprobe's hook at 0x80003AB4 is untouched. The stub also seeds the private generator-2 object (0x8049A228) with the game's own seed function `0x800B7D38(obj, 0x2A6D, 0x3039)` (the shared object 0x803BE03C is zero until a match seeds it at 0x80095C5C from match +0x2F4 / +0x2F8); the private generator-1 state (0x8049A210) starts as a gen-1 state copied from the title screen (DOL data). SndHeap index at 0x8049A1F8 (= 2 at boot; the fake-VMEM heap becomes index 3). Sound allocator 0x8028F300 → 0x8049A000, gen-1 stub 0x8049A200, event-zero stubs 0x8049A2C0+.
  - **Lab check of the real build** (Robert's start.dol of 16 October, rbprof + rbprobe reverted, + rbstate; nothing poked): boots fresh to the title, the scripted menus reach a Blaze vs DMX fight; rewind-every-frame chain 400 updates (rand1): 400 rewinds, 1545 thrown-away updates, 394 compared → **fight state identical**, no freeze (same ~55-60 non-game words differ as before); second chain (rand2, from update 5155): 400 rewinds, 1563 thrown away, 395 compared → **identical**, no freeze. `chain.py` takes the start savestate from `FIGHT=`.
  - Test build for Robert: `ffny_scripts_new/rollback test build/start.dol` (md5 0c23cd307ea57e70d315532ad2534e5a) = `ffny_dolpatch.py start.dol x.dol rbprof rbprobe --revert` then `rbstate`. To check in normal Dolphin: sound in fights / menus / story mode (512 KB less main heap), no freezes.
  - **Robert's test in normal Dolphin (17 October, evening; no underclock): works.** Intro movie / sounds, menu sounds and music, Vendetta Snowman vs DMX on Junkyard with crowd (full round), Story Mode cutscene, message-center voice messages, background sirens / foley, Henry Rollins' gym, a Story Mode fight: all sounds present, no freeze or crash. Side notes: Story Mode fights play their own stage music regardless of the Jukebox ticks (America's Most played with only the ported songs ticked); Babylon still runs under 100 % speed without underclock on his PC (same as before the patch), while PCSX2 runs the PS2 version at full speed on the same PC.
- **Dolphin version speed question (17 October, evening; Robert: 5.0-12147 seems to run FFNY better than the new version, Babylon < 100 % without underclock on R5 3600 / RTX 5060).** 5.0-12147 = commit d6341c4117 (merged 17 June 2020, PR #8883); 2609 is 25,711 commits after 5.0, so ~13,500 commits apart. Dolphin wiki / forum / bug tracker pages are behind a bot check (not readable from the lab). Found so far: (1) FFNY's game INI (GNW / GNWE69): 2020 had `[Video_Hacks] ImmediateXFBEnable = False` (forced off) and a no-op `[Core] Patch Region`; since 2026-04-11 (cb9f8a2b04) `FastDepthCalc = False` (accurate per-pixel depth, GPU cost only; fixes missing menu text) and immediate XFB no longer forced off. (2) The game's 0x7E000000 memory is Dolphin's fake VMEM (MMU off), read / written through the slow path in both versions (unchanged apart from a mask fix, PR #9739, 2021). (3) Page-table fastmem (PR #13768, 2026-02; INI setting `[Core] PageTableFastmem`, PR #14480): no effect on FFNY in the lab (300 fight updates: 59.1 vs 59.3 ms per update host time, Null video); FFNY's VM library (tlbie / mtsr / mtsdr1 at 0x8028D37C-0x8028DE84) only matters with MMU on. (4) Idle-loop detection change (PR #14683, 2026-06, ignore nop in busy-wait loops) and new per-game speed hacks (PR #13771) do not touch FFNY. Next: compare Robert's two Dolphin configs (backend, dual core / sync GPU, CPU clock, shader compilation, EFB / XFB options, resolution, MMU) - a settings difference is the likeliest cause; if the configs match, an A/B build of both versions in the lab.
  - **Robert's settings (17 October, 20:44):** both versions share Documents\Dolphin Emulator\Config: D3D backend, **Dual Core off** (CPUThread = False), Sync GPU off, MMU off, fastmem on, no CPU clock override (Overclock 0.5 stored but disabled), synchronous shader compilation, internal resolution 1x, Skip EFB Access on, EFB / XFB to texture only, immediate XFB on, Fast Depth Calc off, **Enable Debugging UI on** (code / registers / breakpoints panes open). User game INI GNWE69.ini holds a memory breakpoint 0x803F27B0 and a watch, but 2609 loads breakpoints only with the Load button (not at boot), so no memory check is active unless loaded. Robert: 5.0-12147 = 60 FPS on Babylon without underclock, newest version ~48 FPS (no frame skip in the new one). Lab (2609, Null video, 300 fight updates): debugging UI on 58.4 / 57.6 ms vs off 59.3 / 59.3 ms - no measurable effect (in 2609 debug mode only turns off the BLR return optimisation + adds a dispatcher state check). Old version: P2 keyboard mapping does not work (GCPadNew.ini written by the new version). Next: Robert's A/B tests in both versions (as-is, speed limit off, Null backend, Dual Core on) to split CPU emulation from graphics; then a bisect over Dolphin dev builds if the CPU side differs.
  - **Robert's A/B test (17 October, 20:59; newest = Dolphin 2609-79, single core):** Babylon 1v1, speed limit unlimited: with the **Null video backend** the game still runs below 100 % (audio slowed down; Null shows no FPS overlay) → the cost is on the emulation thread, not in D3D drawing (with Dual Core off that thread still does the GPU command / FIFO processing besides the CPU emulation, so CPU emulation is the likeliest part, not yet proven). **Dual Core on in 2609-79 → Babylon at full speed, like 5.0-12147 single core.** Reading: the single-thread work per frame grew between 2020 and 2609; with Dual Core the GPU work moves to its own thread and FFNY fits again. Practical: use Dual Core (also good for rollback: re-simulation runs on the CPU thread). Pinning the exact change needs the old version's Null-backend number and then a bisect over dev builds (optional now).
  - **Dual Core and FFNY netplay (Robert, 17 October 21:02):** since ~2019 FFNY online through Dolphin netplay has never worked with Dual Core: the emulator hangs at start-up just before the players get synchronised (the community's FFNY netplay setup notes say Dual Core off); Robert also had at least one crash with Dual Core on while testing (an error about the CPU / draw cycle being too fast or similar; exact text not recorded). So the rollback build has to work in single-core mode, like the lab (CPUThread = False) - which makes the single-thread slowdown since 5.0-12147 the thing to find (bisect), the netplay Dual Core hang second.
  - **Robert's speed series (17 October 21:27; Babylon 1v1, single core, D3D11, no underclock, speed limit unlimited):** 5.0-8993 (Nov 2018) 95-98 %, 54-58 FPS; **5.0-11900** (Apr 2020) **117 %, 65-72 FPS**; 5.0-12147 (Jun 2020) 55-63 FPS, about 100 %; 5.0-15945 (Jan 2022) 99-102 %, 50-62 FPS; **2407** (Jul 2024) **78-87 %, 47-52 FPS**; **2609-79** **75-83 %, 44-50 FPS**. Main drop between 5.0-15945 and 2407 (Jan 2022 - Jul 2024, ~5,900 commits). **Prime suspect: "Cull Vertices on the CPU"** (PR #11208, merged 2023-01-26, default off, per-game on for a few games): Robert's GFX.ini has `CPUCull = True` globally, so every version since 2023 transforms and culls all FFNY vertices on the emulation thread before sending them to the GPU - in single-core mode that thread also runs the game, and Babylon's crowd geometry is heavy. Versions before 2023 ignore the setting. Test: 2609 with CPU culling off.
  - **Bisect update (21:37):** CPU culling ruled out (2609-79 with "Cull Vertices on the CPU" off: still 77-83 %, 47-50 FPS). **5.0-18799 (2023-03-02): 81-87 %, 49-53 FPS = slow.** Regression therefore between **5.0-15945 (2022-01-27, ~100 %)** and **5.0-18799**. Next: a build near 5.0-17370 (2022-09-18); then 16650 (2022-06-14) or 18080 (2022-12-23). Merges in range worth a look once narrowed: #10741 audio DMA one block at a time (2022-10-24), #10343 cubeb update (2022-11-27), #11326 frame pacing / #11449 throttle reset (Dec 2022 - Jan 2023), #11303 / #11301 CoreTiming class + globals refactors (Nov 2022), #11139 / #11304 Jit64 NaN handling (Nov 2022), #10890 vertex line expansion (2022-10-23).
  - **Bisect update (21:55):** "Enable Debugging UI" off in 2609-79: no change. 5.0-17370 (2022-09-18): 95-107 %, 55-64 FPS (garbled venue-select textures in that build); 5.0-18080 (2022-12-23): 90-100 %, 52-61 FPS; 5.0-18263 (2023-01-17): 83-93 %, 50-55 FPS; 5.0-18441 (2023-01-30): 81-91 %, 48-51 FPS. Series 15945 ~100 → 17370 ~101 → 18080 ~95 → 18263 ~88 → 18441 ~86 → 18799 ~84 → 2407 ~82 → 2609 ~80 %: **a gradual decline with the steepest step between 18080 and 18263** (23 Dec 2022 - 17 Jan 2023, ~70 merges), not one single break. In that step: #11326 frame-time analyzer (12-24), #11348 CoreTiming "throttle before every event using std::chrono" (01-13), #11183 PowerPC data-cache emulation (01-09; only active with Accurate CPU Cache, but adds checks in the slow memory path), and AdmiralCurtiss's "globals" refactors moving emulator state behind Core::System (pixel / vertex / geometry shader managers 12-28/29, ProcessorInterface 01-04, **GPFifo 01-09** - the gather pipe every GX command goes through). The globals series runs from late 2022 into 2023, which would fit a gradual loss on the single emulation thread. Next: ~5.0-18170 (≈ 2023-01-04) to split the step; also worth testing Dual Core + Synchronize GPU Thread (the deterministic dual-core mode meant for netplay) for speed and, later, for the netplay start-up hang.
  - **Netplay test with a friend (17 October ~22:30, Robert):** Dual Core + Synchronize GPU Thread with "Deterministic dual core" = auto or fake-completion → **hang at start-up in both 5.0-12147 and 2609-79**; = none → 12147 runs with Dual Core (netplay chat: desync reported at frame 0 for Robert, the game looked in sync), 2609-79: friend stuck on a black screen. Dual Core off in 12147: runs, slower. "Emulate Disc Speed" off: only shorter loading, same fight speed. In game properties, a dash ("-", blue box) = undetermined = Dolphin's global setting; 2609's dialog has "Emulate Disc Speed" where old builds had "Speed up Disc Transfer Rate". **5.0-18171 (2023-01-05): 74-83 %, 45-51 FPS, FPS dips at regular intervals** - slower than 18080 and 18263, so the build-to-build differences in this window are within the measuring noise (eyeballed OSD, ±5-10 %); bisect stopped here: the loss looks gradual (~20 % from late 2022 to 2026), not one change.
  - **Lab: the netplay start-up hang reproduced (2609, CPUThread = True, SyncGPU = True, GPUDeterminismMode = fake-completion, test_rb.dol, fresh boot):** the game never reaches the title screen (logic counter 0); the main thread sleeps in **GXDrawDone (0x8026C68C)**: it writes BP 0x45 = 0x000002 (set draw done) into the gather pipe, flushes, then waits on a thread queue (r13-0x6F2C) until the PE Finish interrupt callback sets the byte r13-0x6F30; called from 0x80113CE4 (boot path 0x8011FFA0 ← 0x8001EAEC ← ... ← main 0x80003DD4). In Dolphin's deterministic GPU-thread mode the PE Finish is raised only by the CPU-side FIFO preprocessing (`LoadBPRegPreprocess`, from `FifoManager::RunGpuOnCpu` / `ReadDataFromFifoOnCPU`, which runs only while GP read is enabled, read-write distance > 0 and the CP is not at a FIFO breakpoint); the direct path in BPWritten skips SetFinish when the deterministic thread is used. So the draw-done command FFNY sends at boot is apparently never preprocessed (to check next: CP FIFO state at the hang - read enable, breakpoint, linked / unlinked CPU vs GP FIFO). Fixing this in the rollback Dolphin (or with a small game patch) would allow Dual Core with deterministic GPU timing for FFNY = the speed of Dual Core with netplay-safe behaviour.
  - **FIXED (17 October, night): Dual Core + "Deterministic dual core: fake-completion" hang = Dolphin bug.** At the hang the CP FIFO is stopped at a **FIFO breakpoint** (read pointer = breakpoint 0x7F50A0, BP enable + BP interrupt on, bFF_Breakpoint set; FFNY's GXDrawDone command lies in the 0x80 bytes after it). FFNY uses FIFO breakpoints as **GPU fences**: a 128-entry ring at 0x80304640/0x803046AC {fifo position, callback, arg} (code 0x80113A90-0x80113C2C: GXEnableBreakPt 0x8026AA1C / GXDisableBreakPt 0x8026AAA8 / GXSetBreakPtCallback 0x8026A9E0; GXDrawDone 0x8026C68C); the breakpoint interrupt runs the next callback and moves the breakpoint on. **Dolphin:** in deterministic GPU-thread mode `CommandProcessorManager::SetCPStatusFromGPU()` runs on the CPU thread (`FifoManager::RunGpuOnCpu`), but because `IsOnThread()` (= dual core) is true it sets `m_interrupt_waiting` and calls `UpdateInterruptsFromVideoBackend()`, which does nothing when the deterministic thread is used → the interrupt is never delivered and `m_interrupt_waiting` stays set forever (code unchanged since 2014: "desynced GPU thread" mode). **Fix:** in that case call `UpdateInterrupts(userdata)` directly (it clears `m_interrupt_waiting`), like single core. Source patch `dolphin_cp_breakpoint_fix.patch`; Windows: **Dolphin.exe 2609-79, one byte: file offset 0x7BE2B2 0x38 → 0x2D** (SetCPStatusFromGPU at VA 0x1407BEDB0: the `jne` after `cmp byte [fifo+0x200208],0` (= m_use_deterministic_gpu_thread) now goes to `mov rdx,rdi; mov rcx,rbx; call UpdateInterrupts (0x1407BF030)` instead of returning); patcher `ffny_tools/rollback/fcfix/ffny_dolphin_fcfix.py` (pattern search, refuses other versions, --revert). Other binary facts (2609-79): GatherPipeBursted 0x1407BD930, UpdateInterrupts 0x1407BF030 (CP object: m_interrupt_set +0x5A, m_interrupt_waiting +0x5B, m_system +0x60, update-interrupts event +0x48), System dual-core flag byte +8.
  - **Lab validation (2609 + the identical change, Dual Core + Sync GPU + fake-completion):** fresh boot → title → Blaze vs DMX fight (logic counter 4554 at fight start, same as single core); two runs of 400 fight frames with the same inputs (rand1) from the same savestate: fighter + match-object state **identical on all 400 frames**. Robert to test speed on Babylon and netplay with a friend (both players need the patched exe, same settings; "auto" should also work during netplay).
- **Dolphin speed: why old builds ran FFNY faster (18 October 2026, lab measurement; supersedes the eyeballed bisect above).**
  - **Method:** the headless lab with a version-portable benchmark hook (`FFNYBench`, see Files): same boot, same menus, same inputs, Robert's own memory card (GCI folder `69-GNWE-TOM.gci` / `69-GNWE-Options.gci`, copied with his permission; Babylon is locked on a blank card), Babylon 1v1 with the fighters walked apart (camera fully out), single core, Null video, unlimited speed; time = host seconds for 1800 logic frames (b0 -> b3). Repeat runs agree within ~2 %. Old builds: 5.0-11900 = commit f2f8168966 (`git rev-list --count 5.0..` = 11900), built here with GCC 13 (needs `-include` of cstdint/mutex/... and an offsetof fix in DSP/Jit/x64); old nogui ignores `-v`, so the Null backend was forced in the old build's VideoBackendBase (env FFNY_NULL).
  - **Result:** Dolphin 2609 **38.8-39.8 s**, Dolphin 5.0-11900 **28.2 s** (= 2609 needs 1.38x the time; matches Robert's 117 % vs ~80 %). Venue map path to Babylon from Foundation: DOWN, DOWN, RIGHT, DOWN, DOWN.
  - **Profile (perf, Babylon fight):** 2609: emulated-CPU JIT code 35 %, **gather-pipe / MMIO path 23.5 %** (GatherPipeBursted 7 %, GPFifo::Write32 4.6 %, MMU::WriteToHardware 4.2 %, JitInterface::CompileExceptionCheck 2.8 %, WriteU64SwapFromJit 1.8 %, UpdateGatherPipe 1.6 %), FIFO decoding 16 %, XF/BP/texture state 9 %, vertex loading 5 %. 5.0-11900: JIT 41 %, gather pipe 12 %, FIFO 14 %. In seconds: gather-pipe path 9.1 s (2609) vs 3.6 s (11900) = most of the difference; the rest is spread (FIFO decode +2 s, JIT +2 s; Core::System indirections everywhere).
  - **Cause:** FFNY's CPU skinning routines (0x801F7CB8 3-bone / 0x801F7E78 1-bone tails and the caller loops at 0x801F7B24 / 0x801F7BFC; used for fighters and the crowd) send every bone matrix to the GPU as XF register loads: stw/stb command + 6 psq_st (paired floats) to 0(r4), r4 = *(0x80300D40) = 0xCC008000 (static table: pipe pointer, then XF command words 0x10000B00, 0x10000804, 0x10000700). A per-PC histogram of slow FIFO writes in 2609 counted **~11 million slow writes per second**, all psq_st / stw / stb at those PCs. Dolphin's JIT inlines FIFO writes only for stw/sth/stb/stfs/stfd with a constant address (or a "speculative constant" block input); psq_st never, and r4 loaded inside the block is not constant -> each store goes through fastmem fault -> backpatched slow path. **Since JosJuice's commit ecbce0a204 (2021-07-26, "PowerPC: Pass on full 32-bit register contents for 8/16-bit writes") `MMU::WriteToHardware` takes at most 4 bytes, so every 8-byte psq_st to the pipe became two full hardware writes** (translation + GPFifo::Write32 + CheckGatherPipe each); 5.0-11900 did one Write_U64_Swap -> GPFifo::Write64.
  - **Fix 1 (game side, works in every Dolphin): `gxfast` DOL patch** (see Files). Stock Dolphin 2609 JIT: 39.8 s -> **29.3 s** (= 5.0-11900 speed). No slow FIFO writes left in the fight.
  - **Fix 2 (Dolphin side): `ffny_tools/rollback/dolphin_jit_gatherpipe.patch`** (against tag 2609): Jit64 psq_stXX writes straight into the gather pipe when the address is a (speculative) constant or, for stores already seen writing to the pipe (`js.fifoWriteAddresses`), after a runtime `cmp addr, 0xCC008000`; same runtime check for stw/sth/stb. Unpatched DOL: 38.8 s -> **30.3 s**. Needs a Windows build of Dolphin (none possible from the lab: no MSVC / Windows SDK reachable; gh / GitHub not linked).
  - Still open for more speed: FIFO decoding + XF/BP processing on the emulation thread (~25 % in single core; Dual Core moves it to the GPU thread), JIT code itself (~35 %), and the remaining 2609-vs-11900 overheads.
- **Why a Babylon fight is so much heavier than the intro camera pan (18 October, lab; gxfast DOL, Dolphin 2609 single core, Null video).** Robert noticed full speed during the round-intro pan and the slowdown starting when the fight starts. Measured per logic frame: intro pan ~6-8 ms host time, ~0.5 MB GPU FIFO data, 388 display-list calls, ~70k position-matrix words, **3.4 M emulated CPU cycles**; fight ~16 ms, **1.73 MB FIFO data**, ~1300 display-list calls, ~260k position-matrix words (≈ 20,000 bone-matrix uploads per frame), **7.1 M emulated cycles** (of the 8.1 M a 486 MHz GameCube has per 1/60 s). Camera distance does not change it (fighters together or far apart: same numbers). Renders per logic frame: 1 in both; EFB copies: 1 to texture + 1 to XFB in both (no extra shadow pass).
  - **Cause: the 3D crowd.** Babylon has 30 crowd members (crowd type `csmall` in venue.xml; each member is built from separate skinned parts: upper body, lower body, hands, accessory, glasses, shoes, cincrowd.xml). Per member the game runs a bounding-sphere frustum test `FUN_800948bc` (member flag +0x50 bit 0 = enabled) from `FUN_80094300` (called per member in the loop at 0x80090088, member list at crowd object +0x130); during the pan only ~5 members per frame pass it, in the fight ~26 (the fight camera looks at the ring the crowd surrounds). Each drawn member costs ~50 display lists, each with its own bone palette computed on the CPU by the skinning routines (skin callee 0x801F7CB8: 2,800 calls per frame in the pan, 9,700 in the fight). Emulated-cycle growth intro -> fight: skinning loops 0.48 M -> 1.66 M, 0x800F5DE0 (animation) 0.11 M -> 0.40 M, 0x80124D54 / 0x80118238 / GX library (0x8026F4D8) / draw variants 0x80132078, 0x80131820 each +0.15-0.25 M.
  - **Test with crowd drawing switched off** (`FUN_800948bc` -> `li r3,0; blr`, lab only): fight 15.9 -> **9.7 ms per frame**, 544 display lists, 114k matrix words (the rest = the two fighters, stage, HUD). So the crowd is ~40 % of a Babylon fight frame.
  - **Where the crowd's cost goes (host profile, gxfast, single core; ms per frame with / without crowd): emulated CPU (JIT) 5.95 / 3.72, gather pipe 1.92 / 0.84, FIFO decoding 5.26 / 2.38, XF/BP/texture state 1.75 / 1.18, vertex loading 0.87 / 0.50** -> about two thirds of the crowd's cost is Dolphin processing the GPU data (matrix uploads), one third the game's own code. Matrix statistics per fight frame: 22,500 position-matrix uploads, only **8,500 different** (62 % are repeats of a matrix already sent that frame, e.g. the same bone blend for several packets / body parts); only 17 % of matrix words land in a slot that already holds the same value (so skipping "already loaded" uploads would save little). Per bone the skinning loop at 0x801F7BFC sends 3 XF loads: position 3x4 (12 words, slot r10*12), normal 3x3 (9 words, 0x400+), and a second 3x4 with zero translation at slot r10*12+0x78 (texgen matrix, likely for env / specular mapping); loop 0x801F7B24 sends only the first two. A per-frame cache of blended matrices would cut the skinning arithmetic (~1.3 M of 7.1 M emulated cycles) by ~60 % but not the upload volume (~0.5-0.7 ms per frame host time).
  - Triple buffering (Robert's question): FFNY already runs a fixed 60 Hz logic step with catch-up (up to 5 updates per drawn frame), so the game speed never depends on the draw rate; the slowdown Robert sees is Dolphin's emulation speed below 100 % (host PC too slow for the emulated work), which no buffering inside the game can fix. In a Babylon fight the game uses ~7.1 M of the 8.1 M GameCube cycles per 1/60 s, so it still draws at 60 when Dolphin keeps up.
  - Possible next steps (Robert to choose): fewer / farther-culled crowd members on heavy stages (visual change), caching the crowd's skinned matrices instead of recomputing them per display list (no visual change, more work), Dual Core (moves the FIFO decoding, ~40 %, to the GPU thread).
- **Robert's Dolphin fork + Windows builds (18 October):** fork `github.com/robertbelko/dolphin` (Claude GitHub app installed for that repository only), branch **`ffny-speed`** on top of upstream master 38d619b (17 October 2026). GitHub Actions workflow `.github/workflows/windows-build.yml` (windows-latest, MSVC via ilammy/msvc-dev-cmd, `cmake --preset ninja-release-x64`, artifact "Dolphin-ffny-<sha>"; compiler errors are re-emitted as `::error::` annotations because job logs / artifacts live on blob storage the lab cannot reach; annotations are readable at `api.github.com/repos/robertbelko/dolphin/check-runs/<job id>/annotations`). Commits: (1) JIT gather-pipe fast paths (psq_st / stw / sth / stb) + CP breakpoint fix (Dual Core + fake-completion hang) + workflow; (2) **FIFO batching**: GPU-side loops (single-core `RunGpuOnCpu`, dual-core GPU thread) read and decode up to 1 KB per iteration (`FifoBatchBytes`: never across the FIFO end or the CP breakpoint, which FFNY uses as GPU fences) instead of one 32-byte block; `UpdateGatherPipe` hands all complete bursts to one `GatherPipeBursted(bursts)` call and the JIT checks the gather pipe every 256 bytes (`GATHER_PIPE_CHECK_BYTES`) instead of 32; (3) the deterministic dual-core CPU-side read / preprocess batched too; master renamed `js.fifoWriteAddresses` to a `LazyMemoryRegionBitSet` (`TestBit(pc / 4)`). Lab results (gxfast DOL, Babylon fight, Null video, ms per frame): single core 15.0 -> 11.5; Dual Core + Sync GPU 27.6 -> 24.1 (2-core lab, not representative); **Dual Core + Sync GPU + fake-completion (netplay mode) 23.0 -> 12.8**. Fighter select pixel-identical, fight pictures normal, no hangs in any mode. Generators: `ffny_tools/rollback/fifobatch.py`, `fifobatch2.py` (apply to a Dolphin tree). Robert's Dolphin settings now: D3D11, Dual Core + Sync GPU + deterministic auto; with gxfast Babylon is "mostly 100 %, often a bit under".
- **Robert's test of the ffny-speed Windows build (18 October, 13:00; build 1a7a45e, run 39118866226; gxfast DOL):** Babylon 1v1, D3D11, single core, no underclock: **speed 100 %, max 118-121 %, almost always 60 FPS**, never under 100 % while fighting; Vulkan single core: about the same. **Dual Core + Sync GPU + deterministic auto: max 150-170 %, 59-60 FPS** full screen. Babylon with 4 fighters (Robert + 3 CPU): single core often drops to 30 FPS at full speed; with Dual Core mostly 60 FPS, rare ~0.25 s drops. Visuals look right. The 30-FPS drops at full speed mean the *emulated* GameCube CPU runs out of time in that frame (the game, not Dolphin, is over its budget; a 1v1 Babylon fight already uses ~7.1 M of 8.1 M cycles per 1/60 s) -> for rollback re-simulation an emulated CPU overclock (Dolphin setting) will be needed; host headroom (max 150-170 %) is what pays for it.
- **Netplay self-test with the ffny-speed build (Robert, 18 October 13:20):** two instances of the build on his PC, Direct Connection to 127.0.0.1:2626, both Dual Core + Sync GPU + deterministic auto, Emulate Disc Speed off: **boots without the old start-up hang, a fight ran with no desync message, 60 FPS, speed 150-170 %**. Traversal from the same PC to itself did not connect (expected: routers often lack NAT loopback). Still open: a real two-PC test over traversal with his friend (same build on both).
- **First netplay with Robert's friend (18 October, afternoon; ffny-speed build 1a7a45e):** with Dual Core + Sync GPU + determinism **Auto** the friend got "FIFO is overflowed by GatherPipe! CPU thread is too fast!" (CommandProcessor.cpp GatherPipeBursted assert) on Babylon within two minutes, and after "ignore" "GFX FIFO: Unknown Opcode (0x09...)" (Robert had seen the same assert before). With determinism **Fake-Completion** online 1v1 and 1v1v1v1 for 3-4 minutes: smooth, no desync, max 150-170 %, no crash. (Offline, "Auto" means no deterministic GPU thread.) The suspected colour-correction difference between builds turned out not to be reproducible (Robert's screenshots of 2609-79 and ffny-speed look the same).
- **Lab analysis of the FIFO overflow (18 October, evening):** FFNY's main FIFO is 256 KB with the high watermark at size - 16 KB (GXInitFifoBase 0x8026A5B8: `addi r4,r5,-0x4000` at 0x8026A5DC; low watermark size / 2). Over the mark, the CP overflow interrupt makes the GX handler (0x8026A484) suspend the drawing thread until the low-watermark interrupt (overflow count r13-0x6F40 = 0x804408A0). Lab counters (`MAXDIST` / `ovf` in the bench marks; lab-only env `FFNY_GPUSLOW=ns per KB`, `FFNY_GPUSTALL=N:ms`, `FFNY_GPCHECK=bytes`, `FFNY_NOBATCH`): Dual Core + Sync GPU, Babylon with random inputs: 15,000 overflow suspensions handled correctly; the CPU writes past the mark before it is suspended by up to 3 KB with the fork's batching and up to 5.7 KB with upstream-like settings (batching off, JIT FIFO check every 32 bytes), **up to 8 KB when the GPU thread stalls** for 40 ms now and then. So the late interrupt is Dolphin's dual-core behaviour (not the batching), and a host with bigger GPU-thread hiccups goes past the 16 KB margin -> overflow -> garbage commands. Not reproduced in the lab itself (2-core container, Null video). Fake-Completion avoids it (deterministic GPU thread on the CPU side; interrupts delivered directly by the fork's fix).
  - **Fix = DOL patch `fifomargin`** (in `ffny_dolpatch.py`): `addis r4,r5,-1` at 0x8026A5DC -> high watermark size - 64 KB (0x30000; low stays 0x20000). Lab: stall test max fill 0x30820 (62 KB margin left), speed unchanged, single core unchanged (Babylon 11.5 ms / frame). Changes game timing slightly but identically on every machine (netplay-safe). Test build `fifomargin test build/start.dol` = `gxfast test build` + `fifomargin` + `rbtag` (md5 1374fb873acc8837daf1919f5dda207f) for Robert's friend to try Dual Core + Sync GPU + Auto again. A Dolphin-side guard (CPU waits for the GPU thread near overflow) is in the lab tree (`FFNY_NOGUARD` to disable) but not in the fork: it cannot help when the GPU thread is itself waiting for an interrupt acknowledgement, which is the likely case.
- **Compact rollback state, step 1 (18 October 13:30, lab, Babylon fight savestate `babylon_fight.sav` with Robert's GCI save):** main heap = heap 0 "RAM" (struct 0x8060C050: +0x08 base 0x8060C040, +0x0C end 0x817FDFD0); blocks form a list from 0x8060C0D0, header 16 bytes {u16 'BM' used / 'BF' free, u16 flags, u32 data size, u32 next, u32 prev}. In the fight: 485 blocks, 477 used = 11.4 MB, 7 free = 7.4 MB. Used blocks >= 64 KB: 32 blocks, 8.6 MB (small-object pool 0x8060C3B0 901 KB, frame buffers, FIFO 256 KB, GCBuffers, audio / stream buffers, loaded model / asset files, EAGLAnim pool 256 KB, Light Man 241 KB); blocks < 64 KB: 2.8 MB. **Planned snapshot** (instead of the lab's 48.7 MB brute-force set): data / bss / sdata / stack (~1.9 MB, minus system-owned statics) + every heap block header (~8 KB) + contents of used blocks < 64 KB (minus the system / audio / stream blocks) + the big game-state blocks (small-object pool, EAGLAnim pool, Light Man, skeleton / particle blocks) + the few logic-written words inside big static file blocks and fake VMEM (to be taken from a store trace) -> roughly 5-6 MB per frame (Slippi copies ~8 MB for Melee), ~0.5-1 ms per save. The heap list is walked at every save, so blocks allocated / freed during the fight are handled; restoring the saved headers (used and free) restores the layout. Next: store trace of a Babylon fight -> list of written words in big blocks / VMEM; implement the save / restore in the lab harness; rewind-every-frame chain test against the reference.
- **Compact rollback state, step 2 (18 October, afternoon, lab):** store trace of 400 Babylon fight updates (interpreter, logic-only): 111k words written; in fake VMEM only 156 words, all inside the animation banks (BaseAnimBank / SkilAnimBank), written by `0x8010DBF0` (track reset: obj+0x0C = value, obj+0x10 = 0) and `0x8010D82C` (sth obj+0x10): per-track key-cursor caches (search hints). Named heap map of a Babylon fight with the rbprobe DOL (gxfast + rbstate + rbprobe, booted fresh, `tobabylon.py`): 571 blocks / 15.3 MB incl. VMEM; system blocks (FrameBuffer, FIFO, SndHeap, GCBuffers, GENRSTRM, AudioManagedRAM, AudioHeap, AUDIO::*, SPCHSTRM, ABKRES, _AsyncLoaderCallback, bigfile buf, File Sys, Fixed MPF, thread stacks, InputBuffer) = 4.45 MB. Heap block flags (u16 at header +2) seen: 0x0000 / 0x0100 / 0x0400 / 0x0500 (used), 0x4000 / 0x4500 (free), 0x8100 ('BS' sentinel) -> the low byte is free for a "not rollback state" tag set by a post-alloc hook (heap debug hook 0x8040BD64 gets the name).
  - **`set_compact.txt` = 8.8 MB** (728 ranges): the old set's static part below the heap arena (data / bss / sdata / stack / cave / bigspace minus system-owned words) + heap 0 struct 0x8060C040-0x8060C0D0 + every block header + contents of every used block except the system names; **no fake VMEM at all**. Rewind-every-frame chain (`chain2.py`, own judge: fighters, RNG, gen1 / gen2 random states, match object, all bss): 198 rewinds (824 wrong-input updates thrown away) over 200 updates -> **fighters, fight RNG and both random generators identical every update**; the only match-object differences are the render-written matrices at 0x80367440-0x80367640, 0x80372C00-0x80372E00, 0x803955C0-0x80395680 (recomputed in every render, known since 17 October); baseline without rewinds: no match-object difference. So the anim-bank key cursors do not need to be in the state (they are only search hints).
- **Compact rollback state, step 3 (18 October, evening, lab): freezes in 1000-frame chains found and fixed -> `set_compact4.txt`.** With `set_compact` / `set_compact2` (minus words never written by the logic) / `set_compact2v` (+ all of fake VMEM) the rewind-every-frame chain froze (seed 9 at step 495 / update 6152; seed 11 at step 105 / update 5748). New lab command `cp` (Dolphin's CP FIFO registers + PI FIFO pointers) and a CPU-state readout at the freeze showed: the **audio thread (OSThread 0x803E23C8, prio 1) took a DSI exception** -> `__OSUnhandledException` (0x80274440 -> PPCHalt 0x8025B028, so the "main thread in 0x8025B02C" seen before was really the halt loop); its context: srr0 = 0, called through the sound-command jump table 0x802FE7B4 at 0x801DD05C (`0x801DCFF8`, entered via table entry 0x802FE7E4) with a garbage command type (0x80 = the first byte of a pointer) while walking an object in the audio block 0x809A0F20. The main thread was only collateral (suspended by the GX overflow handler with the FIFO drained; GX shadow state itself was consistent: `__GXData` 0x80431998 (cpEnable +8 = 0x19, cpStatus +0xC), FIFO object 0x80431918, `__GXCurrentThread` r13-0x6F50 = 0x80440890, overflow-in-progress r13-0x6F48 = 0x80440898, overflow count r13-0x6F40 (505 at the freeze), breakpoint address r13-0x6F3C; r13 = 0x804477E0; these statics are not in the compact set anyway). A full-RAM restore (`restore`) freezes on the first rewind and `set_final.txt` (made for the 17 October lab DOL) at step 23 with this DOL, so neither is a usable reference.
  - **Cause:** the compact set restored memory that other threads own, all inside "game" heap blocks: (1) the loaded EA sound event map file **`MOIR`** (heap block 0x80A420C0, 0x1A9C bytes): the sound library keeps per-sound instance list heads in it (audio thread `0x801F38A8` links nodes into `*(r30)` at 0x801F38F0, more at 0x801F3958) -> rewinding it brought back list heads pointing at freed nodes; (2) **OSThread structs living in the small-object pool** (threads 0x806D5A34 and 0x806D8AE0; written by the OS at 0x80277828...); (3) the **pool mutex** (pool struct 0x8060C0E0 = *(0x802E9B00), mutex at +0x2A4 = 0x8060C384) and the heap mutexes (heap struct + 0x3C: heap 0 0x8060C050, heap 1 0x806E8510), which the old `mkset.py` excluded but the compact set did not. Found by intersecting the set with the all-threads store trace `d/st_rbp_all.txt` (writes by threads other than main 0x80432D10).
  - **`set_compact4.txt` = `set_compact2` minus** the MOIR block, every OSThread struct (0x318 bytes, from the OS thread list at 0x800000DC / +0x2FC) and every thread stack except the main one, the pool mutex and the heap mutexes: **8.77 MB**. Result: **seed 11: 1000 rewinds (3941 wrong-input updates thrown away) over 1000 updates, no freeze; fighters, fight RNG, gen1 / gen2 identical every update; match-object differences only the known render matrices** (997 updates compared). **Seed 9 (the old step-495 freeze): also 1000 rewinds (3896 thrown-away updates), no freeze, 1000 updates compared, same result (only the known render matrices differ).** `set_compact5.txt` additionally drops the 18 `ABKC` sound banks and the `EMIT` block (8.48 MB, not yet run).
  - **For the real build (DOL-side tagging):** the "not rollback state" tag must also cover blocks the sound library loads and mutates (MOIR event maps; probably ABKC banks / EMIT emitter data too: they are sound-engine data, the logic never writes them), OSThread structs and stacks allocated from the pool, and every allocator mutex; i.e. tag by allocation site / file magic, not only by heap-block name.
- **Compact rollback state, step 4 (18 October, evening, lab): snapshot built at save time + DOL-side block tags (`rbtag`).**
  - **Lab commands `csave K STATIC.txt [tag] [OUT.txt]` / `cload K`** (FFNYLab.cpp, `CBuildRanges`) = prototype of the real per-frame save: the static ranges come from a file (the part of `set_compact4.txt` below 0x804A2500: data / bss / sdata / stack / cave / bigspace minus system statics, 1.68 MB); the heap part is decided **at every save** by walking heap 0 (struct *(0x8060C050+8) .. +0x90, every 16-byte block header, the contents of every used block except: tagged / system-named blocks and blocks starting with `MOIR`, `ABKC` or `EMIT`); then the exclusions are cut out: every allocator mutex (heap table 0x8040BD70, heap struct + 0x3C, 0x18 bytes), the pool mutex (*(0x802E9B00) + 0x2A4), every OSThread struct (list 0x800000DC, next +0x2FC, 0x318 bytes) and every thread stack except the main one (+0x308 .. +0x304). The data is copied into one buffer (ranges + bytes + the registers the lab restores). Without `tag` the system blocks are found by the rbprobe name table (lab only); with `tag` by the `rbtag` flag bit.
  - **Cost (lab container, Babylon fight): 8.51 MB in ~738 ranges; building the range list 0.5 ms, copying 1.5 ms, restoring 1.6 ms.** (Melee / Slippi is in the same range. Possible later savings: skip the 1.9 MB of static data the logic never writes, or copy only pages written since the last save.)
  - **Chain results with `csave` / `cload`:** rbp DOL, names, seed 9: 1000 rewinds (3896 thrown-away updates), no freeze, fighters / RNG / gen1 / gen2 identical every update, only the known render matrices differ. rbtag DOL (`rbp` + `rbtag`), tags, seed 9: 991 rewinds / 1002 updates compared, same result.
  - **DOL patch `rbtag`** (in `ffny_dolpatch.py`, needs `rbstate`; source `ffny_tools/rollback/rbtag/rbtag.c`, built with clang for PowerPC 750): rbstate's boot call 0x80003AD0 -> `rbtag_boot` 0x8049A410 installs a post-allocation hook into the heap allocator's debug hook word 0x8040BD64 (called at 0x80249C34 with an info struct: +0 name, +0x18 pointer, +0x1C size; it chains to the previous hook, so rbprobe still works) and then runs rbstate's boot code 0x8049A100 (so SndHeap is tagged too). The hook sets **bit 0x0080 of the block header flags** for system names. Heap block flags read from the allocator: **bits 0-5 = heap index** (used by free: table 0x8040BD70 + index * 4; VMEM heap blocks have index 3), 0xC000 = state (0x4000 free), 0x0100 / 0x0400 used, 0x0040 / 0x0080 unused (the "low byte is free" note of step 2 was wrong: bits 0-5 are the heap index). Lab: the rbtag DOL boots, plays and reaches a Babylon fight; tags agree with rbprobe's names for 533 of 538 used blocks, the 5 others are sound-library blocks whose name strings were temporary (rbprobe stores garbage for them; the hook saw the real name at allocation time), among them the MOIR block. A release-style DOL (`gxfast test build` + rbtag, no rbprobe; md5 af4510d38422d0708eb3b7c0c2bdbcb8) tags exactly the same 74 blocks (4.48 MB) as the research DOL. **Rewind-every-frame chain on that release-style DOL with tags only (no name table), seed 11: 989 rewinds (3898 thrown-away updates), 1001 updates compared, no freeze, fighters / RNG / gen1 / gen2 identical, only the known render matrices differ; range build 0.16 ms without the name table.** Test build for Robert: `rbtag test build/start.dol` (= that DOL) - should play, sound and look exactly like `gxfast test build`.
  - **Next:** the Dolphin side: put `CBuildRanges` + save / load into a real rollback netplay mode in the fork (Slippi-style: per-frame save, input prediction, rollback trigger, re-simulation with the emulated CPU overclocked), then the sound question during re-simulation.
- **Rollback netcode, step 5 (18 October, night): the rollback engine (Dolphin) + netplay option, lab-tested.**
  - **Design (how it fits the game):** the main loop (0x80004CC0-0x80004D3C) runs one logic update per pad snapshot that the input thread produced: tick() `0x800EC144` copies the next snapshot from a 10-entry ring in the input-thread object (r28; 0x260 bytes each; count +0x17C8, read index +0x17C0, mutex +0x1A30, frame-skip counters +0x1A4C-+0x1A54) to object+0x17CC and returns 1; then `0x800F2094` (input) and `0x800830D4` (mode / fight update) run, then the per-update work at 0x80004CDC (0x800CD510, 0x800CDD00, 0x8000E0B8), then tick() again; tick() = 0 ends the pass and the frame is rendered. **Snapshot layout:** 0x10-byte header (per-port words) + 4 pad records of 0x94 bytes at +0x10 + port * 0x94; one byte per held button at 8-byte stride, measured relative to the record start: Z (0x10) +0x1F, A (0x100) +0x27, B (0x200) +0x2F, X (0x400) +0x37, Y (0x800) +0x3F, START (0x1000) +0x47, D-pad up / down / left / right +0x67 / +0x6F / +0x77 / +0x7F, main stick X / Y +0x80 / +0x81 (L / R digital: no change seen); **no edge flags** (edges are derived by the rewound game logic). So a frame's input = the records.
  - **Engine (`Core/FFNYRollback.cpp`, fork branch `ffny-rollback`):** HLE replace hooks **PRE 0x80004D14** (`mr r3,r26`, right before the input / update calls) and **TICK 0x80004CFC** (`bl 0x800EC144`), installed once the game code and the rbtag v3 marker ("RBT2" at 0x8049A408, hook word 0x8040BD64 inside 0x8049A410-0x8049AE00) are in memory (checked every VI field). Active while 0x803F1000 = 1 (fight running); frame number = logic counter 0x80440C24. At PRE(F): capture the local records (they become frame F + delay), send them (window of the last 8 frames), pump received records; if a received record differs from what frame S < F used: **load the state of S (same PC: PRE), re-simulate S..F-1** (PRE injects the corrected inputs and saves again; TICK makes tick() return 1 without consuming a real snapshot), all inside the current main-loop pass, then the frame is rendered once; otherwise inject (received or predicted = newest known record) and save. Wait when more than 7 frames ahead of the other side; at fight end wait until every input is confirmed. Per-frame checksum (fighters, fight RNG, gen1, gen2, counter) of every fully confirmed frame is exchanged -> DESYNC report. **Game timing object 0x8035D618** (written by 0x80021D80 before each update: +8 VI retrace count, +0xC measured time step; read by mode / fight code) is forced to base + 2 * (F - F0) and 1/60 during fights (real-time values would differ in re-simulations and between machines). Remaining real-time reads found (not needed so far): 21 callers of VIGetRetraceCount 0x8024E22C (e.g. 0x800F21E4 in the input code), 4 of 0x8024E234, mftb in game code (0x80095F68.., 0x800982C8 in the fight frame, 0x8011CE6C..); the GPU fence counter 0x802F4CB0 (+1 per render event, +5 per GXDrawDone in 0x80113CE4; objects keep stamps at +0xDC) is render-driven but only decides GPU syncs.
  - **State fixes found by the new single-machine stress mode** (`FFNY_RB=3:3 FFNY_RB_STRESS=7`: rewind 1-7 frames on every frame, compare every re-simulated frame with its first run, plus a heap list check): (1) **free blocks keep their free-list links in the first 8 content bytes** (+0x10 next free, +0x14 prev free; free list sorted by address, sentinel in the heap struct +0x10; insert 0x8024A468, remove 0x8024A4EC; free-block size field includes the header, used-block size does not) -> every block's first 0x18 bytes are saved now (the compact set had only the 16-byte header -> after a rewind the allocator followed stale links and built an inconsistent list -> use-after-free crash in the HUD code 0x800CB94C -> 0x8001B08C -> virtual call 0x80018538 through a null vtable); (2) **system allocations / frees in the main heap during fights** (file loader "bigfile" blocks) -> rbtag v3 SysHeap + deferred frees; (3) **file-system queue** -> rbtag v3 queue guard (see the rbtag v3 note in `ffny_dolpatch.py`; the loads are the round intro / extro cut-scene files `assets/v2ta.viv|BT1_01.viv` etc. of `CLogic::Init:CIntroExtroSubsystem`, requested during the fight via 0x80098D04 -> 0x800B1914 -> 0x800B19C0 -> 0x8007A6BC). With all three: stress run over a whole Babylon fight (6,300 frames, a rewind of 1-7 frames on every frame): **no freeze, every re-simulated frame identical**.
  - **Two-Dolphin lab tests** (`lab/rbnet/run2.sh`: both boot fresh with the same scripted menu inputs, custom RTC, Null video, real-time speed; in the fight A plays pad 0 and B pad 1 with random scripted inputs, linked by UDP on localhost with optional artificial lag / jitter): no lag, delay 1: **6,000 fight frames (~100 s), A 878 rewinds (max 7 frames, 4,466 re-simulated frames), 0 desyncs over ~1,050 compared checksums**; save 1.5-2.5 ms per frame, load ~1.2 ms per rewind (2-core lab container, two Dolphins). **With 40 ms one-way lag + up to 20 ms jitter, delay 2: 5,400+ fight frames, A 765 rewinds (max 7), B 67 (max 3), 0 desyncs over ~950 compared checksums, no freeze.** (Before the state fixes, 2 of 4 runs froze.)
  - **Netplay integration (fork, branch `ffny-rollback`, commit 8e0b2c5):** Network menu option **"FFNY Rollback (fights)"** (host; config NetPlay/FFNYRollback, delay NetPlay/FFNYRollbackDelay = 2), sent in the start-game settings; message `MessageID::FFNYRollback` (0x68) carries the engine's messages and is relayed by the host to the other players; the local / active ports come from the netplay pad mapping. While a fight runs, `GetNetPads` returns the local pad without exchanging or waiting (the rollback layer overwrites the game's records); menus stay lockstep netplay.
  - **Resync after a fight (fork commits 2f23f3f / 4eef521):** when a rollback fight is over (fight flag != 1 and every input confirmed) the engine calls netplay's fight-end callback on the CPU thread: the client sets "resync pending", calls `CPU.Break()` (the CPU stops at the next block boundary) and sends `FFNYFightEnd` (0x69, frame). When the server has it from every player it saves the host's emulator state on the (stepping) CPU thread (`State::FFNYSaveCompressed`: the normal savestate DoState into a buffer, LZ4-compressed; `Core::RunOnCPUThread` only queues the job, so the caller waits on an event), sends it chunked (`FFNYState` 0x6A, title "FFNY resync") to every player except the host, each loads it (`State::FFNYLoadCompressed`) and answers `FFNYStateLoaded` (0x6B); then the server sends `FFNYResume` (0x6C): every client clears its pad buffers, resets its time-base frame counter and continues (`CPU.Continue()`); the server clears its time-base table. Time-base desync reports are not sent during rollback fights and the resync. OSD messages: fight start (input delay), fight end (rollbacks, max depth, desyncs, "synchronising..."), desync (red), 10-s input timeout (red). Not testable in the headless lab (no netplay there): first real test = Robert + friend.
  - **Not done yet:** (a) ~~after a fight the two Dolphins are no longer identical~~ (resync implemented, untested) below the game state (sound, threads, timing), so lockstep netplay in the menus after the first rollback fight can desync -> planned: the host sends its savestate at the fight end (only the differing 64 KB blocks); (b) sounds during re-simulation play again (duplicates); (c) the emulated CPU needs headroom for re-simulation (Dolphin CPU clock override, e.g. 200 %, set by the host); (d) on-screen status (rollbacks, desyncs).
- **Rollback netcode, step 6 (18-19 October): real mispredictions + 60 ms lag fixed (rbtag v5, fork commit ee6e812).**
  - **Symptom (step 5 runs t7 / t8, 60 ms lag, aggressive inputs):** after ~1,000 real rewinds the audio thread crashed (jump through a garbage callback pointer at 0x801F2644, an object inside the "AUDIO::INIT::Aems Stream Buffer" block now holding sample floats) or the main thread spun forever in the EA stream service 0x801DEE38 (called every loop pass via 0x8024E1F0 -> 0x801EF95C -> 0x801EA990, from main-loop 0x80004CD8 and from file waits) walking a looping stream list.
  - **New lab mode for this: loopback** (`lab/rbnet/runL.sh TAG K`: one Dolphin, `FFNY_RB=1:3 FFNY_RB_LOOP=K`: pad 1 is scripted locally but its records are delivered as "remote" input K frames late, so every input change is a real misprediction and rewind; deterministic, so a crash repeats at the same frame). Lab extras: `FFNY_RB_CRASHDUMP=path` (RAM dump at the first interpreter "unknown instruction"), `FFNY_RB_TRACE=addr,addr` (FFNYLab trace hooks; `FFNY_TRACE_FILTER=1` = only frees of non-main-heap blocks during fights).
  - **Cause 1 (the big one): the rollback state rewound the first 8 data bytes of every used heap block.** BuildRanges saved header + 8 bytes of every block (meant for the free-list links of free blocks). In a used system block those bytes are system data: the EA stream objects (0x60 bytes each, list head 0x803DE644, free list 0x803DE648, allocated on the audio thread by 0x801DE434 from the state machine 0x801DD508) sit at the start of the Aems stream buffer, so their next / prev links were rewound -> list cycle. **Fix (Dolphin): used blocks -> 16-byte header only; free blocks ('BF') -> header + 8.**
  - **Cause 2: double frees in SndHeap.** The game's sound code (`0x801D3888` path, from 0x801D34B8 / 0x801D53D0) frees 0x20-byte SndHeap blocks inside rewound frames; the re-simulation freed the same block again (seen 5 times in one run). **Fix (rbtag v5): main-thread frees of blocks in heaps whose memory is not rewound (SysHeap, or a heap whose own block in heap 0 is tagged 0x80 = SndHeap) are logged with the frame and done once that frame is confirmed; dropped after a rewind to that frame or earlier.**
  - **Cause 3: sound-library reference counts.** acquire 0x801F327C(handle, values, &out) -> *out = object (+4 refcount), release 0x801F33A4(obj); the game calls both from rewound code. **rbtag v5: main-thread acquires are logged, releases deferred, both tagged with the frame of the newest saved state;** confirmed releases are done; after a rewind to S, logged acquires of frames >= S are released (the restored game no longer holds them) and deferred releases of frames >= S are dropped (the re-simulation redoes them). Settling runs only at the entry of acquire / release (settling from inside free crashed: free is called in the middle of the library's list work). Tagging by "frame of the newest save" instead of "inside the logic update" matters: everything the main thread does after a save (render phase too) is undone by a rewind to that frame.
  - **rbtag v5 layout:** code 0x8049A410-0x8049B21D (must stay below 0x8049B400); deferred-free queue 0x8049B400; words 0x8049B500 active, 0x8049B504 frame of newest save, 0x8049B508 rewind target S+1, 0x8049B510 / 514 / 518 log counts, 0x8049B51C settling flag; newest confirmed frame 0x8049A40C; logs (pairs object / frame, 96 each) acquire 0x8049B600, release 0x8049B900, free 0x8049BC00. Marker "RBT5" at 0x8049A408. Dolphin excludes 0x8049B400-0x8049BF00 from the state and writes the words (fight start: active, conf = F-1; every save: frame; rewind: S+1 and conf = S-1; fight end / 10-s timeout: active = 0, so the next acquire / release settles everything). `ffny_dolpatch.py rbtag` upgrades v1 and v3 DOLs to v5 (UPGRADE_FROM can list several old versions now). Sources: `ffny_tools/rollback/rbtag/` (rbtag.c, tramp.s, link.ld, build.sh, mkrbtag.py).
  - **Results:** loopback K = 3, delay 1: whole scripted fight, 12,600 frames, 2,878 real rewinds, no crash / freeze (before: crash at frame ~12,336 every time, or the stream-list hang). **Two Dolphins, 60 ms one-way lag + 30 ms jitter, delay 2: the whole fight to the KO (9,880 frames), A 1,799 rewinds (max 7 frames, 7,657 re-simulated), 0 desyncs over 972 compared checksums, both reached fight end at frame 16,568.** Save ~2.5 ms / frame, load ~1.2 ms / rewind (lab).
  - **First Windows build with all of it: fork commit 39b7cbf (branch `ffny-rollback`), Actions run 39167092896, artifact `Dolphin-ffny-39b7cbf...`** (MSVC fixes on the way: missing Core.h include, size_t cast, parameter `out` shadowed State.cpp's LZO global `out` (C4459); the workflow now reports MSVC warnings outside Externals too). Test DOL: `rollback test build/start.dol` = fifomargin test build + rbtag v5 (md5 376c1502825e3084878c08c7988e6c9f; lab loopback run identical to the lab DOL). Not tested anywhere yet: real netplay, the post-fight resync, the pause menu in a rollback fight.
- **Robert's first test of the rollback build (19 October; fork 39b7cbf + `rollback test build/start.dol`; two netplay instances on his own PC, each player vs bots):** both instances share one Dolphin user folder, so they share one controller config (P1 cannot be a different pad per instance) -> 1v1 between the two instances not tested yet (fix for local tests: second instance from a copy of the Dolphin folder with `portable.txt`, or started with `-u <other user folder>`). **No CPU overclock: the game crashes at the moment the fighter becomes controllable (= rollback start)** - should only slow down, not crash; open bug. **200 % overclock: "rollback started" OSD, plays fine, no desync message in chat or OSD**; after the round the **post-fight resync ran and worked; a rematch started rollback again without errors** (first real test of the resync). With the host's "Limit Chunked Upload Speed" at ~100 kbps the resync progress bar was very slow; with the limit off it took ~0.5-1 s. Reason: the resync state goes through Dolphin's chunked-data thread, which applies that limit; the limit only throttles chunked data (save / code sync at game start, now the FFNY resync) - pad / rollback input messages (0x68) go on the normal channel and are never throttled, and during a fight no chunked data is sent. Robert's experience: the limit gives him a lower, steadier ping. Ideas: resync ignores the limit (CPU is stopped anyway, nothing to protect) or has its own limit setting; send only the 64 KB blocks that differ (client sends block hashes first).
- **Robert's second offline test (19 October, afternoon; same build / DOL):** CPU overclock **off** on both instances: rollback started without a crash, twice (the earlier crash at rollback start without overclock did not repeat; its message was not kept). Visuals / sound right (Blazin' colour grading etc.), **except: in the Blazin' finisher screen freeze the green / white flash overlay (normally a white overlay that brightens gradually) flickers / shows wrongly; in stock 2609-79 it fades correctly.** Not yet known which DOL Robert used in 2609-79. Suspects: fork FIFO batching (1 KB per GPU loop iteration, gather-pipe check every 256 B: GPU runs further behind the CPU between fences, so an effect that changes memory the GPU reads after issuing the draw, or reads an EFB copy, could show the wrong frame's data), the rollback engine (active only in netplay fights), or the DOL patches (gxfast / fifomargin / rbtag). A/B plan: (1) stock 2609-79 + `rollback test build/start.dol` (DOL patches); (2) fork build offline, no netplay, same DOL (rollback engine off -> FIFO / JIT changes); (3) fork in netplay. Fork facts: `FifoManager::FifoBatchBytes` (Fifo.cpp, MAX_BATCH 1024), `GPFifo::GATHER_PIPE_CHECK_BYTES` = 256 (GPFifo.h).
- **"FFNY runs at 30 fps" (Robert's friends; 19 October, from code already read):** no 30-fps cap found: logic = fixed 60 updates / s (dt constant 1/60, tick object started with 60), render = once per main-loop pass after all due updates. If update + render do not fit in 1/60 s the loop does 2 updates and 1 render -> 30 drawn fps at correct game speed (Dolphin at 100 % clock: 1v1 Babylon fight ~7.1 M of 8.1 M emulated cycles per 1/60 s; 4 fighters dropped to 30 FPS; Dolphin's cycle model is optimistic, so real hardware likely drops more often). Animation data is keyed at 30 fps (scene frame-rate byte 0x1E, animtags frames at 30 fps) and decoded at fractional frames with linear interpolation between keys -> at 60 fps every second pose is interpolated. Real-hardware draw rate not measured.
- **Flash flicker narrowed down (Robert, 19 October, 16:03):** `rollback test build/start.dol` offline: **fork build = flicker**, **stock 2609-79 = correct fade** -> the cause is in the fork's emulator changes, not the DOL and not the rollback engine (offline = no rollback). Candidates: FIFO batching (1 KB), gather-pipe check every 256 B, JIT gather-pipe fast paths (psq_st / stw / sth / stb), deterministic CPU-side batching (only with Dual Core + Sync GPU). Next: a fork build with a separate on / off setting for each change, Robert finds which one flickers; keep the rest. Upstream reads FIFO data per 32-byte block = the hardware's gather-pipe burst size; finer GPU / CPU interleaving is the conservative, accurate choice for many games, the cost only matters for games that stream as much as FFNY (20k matrix uploads per frame).
- **PAL / underclock notes (Robert's friend, 19 October):** friend says PAL FFNY (PS2 too) uses a variable time step, so PCSX2 can draw e.g. 90 Hz with the game at normal speed; not checked (PS2 EU ISO on Robert's PC: `E:\Asztal\games_tools\Def Jam Mods\ffny-rebalanced-ps2_eu.iso`; no PAL GameCube DOL found yet). USA GameCube: fixed 1/60 step, so no higher draw rate without interpolated rendering. Friend used per-stage CPU **underclock** in Dolphin for "perfect 60": explanation: underclock lowers the host PC's work (fewer emulated cycles per second) so Dolphin keeps 100 % speed on a weak PC, but gives the game fewer cycles per frame -> too low = game-side drops to 30 drawn fps (lab: 50 % clock = 10-15 fps look); the sweet spot differs per stage (Babylon ~88 % of the budget at 100 % clock).
- **Flicker = Dolphin's deterministic GPU-thread mode (Robert, 19 October, 16:12):** fork build, Dual Core, offline: **determinism fake-completion = flicker, auto = no flicker.** Offline "auto" means no deterministic GPU thread at all (`FifoManager::UpdateWantDeterminism`: auto -> on only when determinism is wanted = netplay / movie; fake-completion -> always on; only with Dual Core), so the flicker belongs to the deterministic GPU-thread path: either upstream's own behaviour in that mode (never reachable for FFNY before the CP breakpoint fix: stock 2609-79 hangs) or the fork's batched CPU-side read / preprocess (change 3). **In netplay with Dual Core, auto = fake-completion (both use the deterministic thread), so the flicker will show there too; single core does not use it.** Next: toggle build (batching off for the deterministic path) to tell the two apart; a savestate just before a Blazin' finisher would let the lab reproduce it.
- **PS2 PAL vs USA timing (19 October, read from SLES_525.07 / SLUS_210.04 in `E:\Asztal\games_tools\Def Jam FFNY Isos\ffny-ps2_*_root`; EU addresses, US mostly a few KB lower):** same engine as the GameCube: main loop 0x101D50 (in main 0x100A2C): tick object (input-buffer object, `InputBufferThreadStack`) started with **60 in both regions** (`0x1E5810(obj, 60)`), `while (tick 0x1E58C0) { dt = 0x11D6F0 (measures real time into 0x43DAF8 +0xC but **returns the constant 1/60**); input 0x1EBF68(dt); update 0x176480(dt) }` then render; tick caps at 5 updates per pass (+0x17E8 < 5). Fight frame 0x18BC98 -> 0x2DCBD8, fight step = match object 0x4BC410 +4 (= 1.0, like GC 0x803668C4). **The difference is what clocks the input thread (= the logic rate):** **USA: VBlank interrupt** (`AddIntcHandler(2, 0x3663E0)` in the start method at US 0x366340) -> 1 logic update per vblank -> a higher PCSX2 refresh speeds the game up. **PAL: the EA hardware-timer list** (PAL-only code: EU 0x366628 registers 0x1E5E90 in the timer client list 0x3A0C78 via 0x332168 (PAL-only); accumulator +0x1808 += 60 / timer Hz (+0x180C = 0.6 at the 100 Hz timer) per tick, signal when >= 1.0 at 0x3666B8) -> **60 logic updates per second of real time, independent of vblank**. Render pacing: `SetFrameRate` 0x21C800 (vblanks per flip = 60 / fps, render object +0x8A0, counted by the vblank handler 0x21C768): USA 30 (cinemas, 0x171B08) / 60 (0x171BA8), PAL 25 / 50 (60/25 = 2, 60/50 = 1 vblank per flip). EA system timer rate 120 Hz USA / 100 Hz PAL (0x331388; T0 on bus clock, handler 0x331650, tick count 0x3A0C9C, rate 0x3A0C98; GetTicks 0x332D10, GetTickRate 0x332D38). Other PAL changes: screen height 448 -> 512, French movies / language assets. **So the friend's 90 Hz PCSX2 observation fits the PAL build: drawing follows the vblank (1 per flip), logic stays 60 Hz real time** (no render-side interpolation looked for: probably 60 distinct game states per second shown on 90 refreshes). GameCube: which clock drives its input thread (`FUN_800ec374`) not checked yet.
- **First real two-PC rollback test (Robert + friend, 19 October evening, ~120-150 ms ping, 150-200 % CPU clock):** normal fighting with rollbacks worked (stutter on ping jumps, no desync). Two repeatable failures: (1) **crash a little after fight start**, "Invalid read from 0x0000000c, PC = 0x80121df0", mostly on the friend's side only (Robert then sees the 10-s input timeout); (2) **DESYNC reported on consecutive frames right when a fighter activates Blazin'** (several stages; one Blazin' passed without). Netplay buffer: only used in menus (lockstep); fights use FFNYRollbackDelay (default 2, Dolphin.ini [NetPlay], host's value, clamp 0-6; no menu option), max rollback 7.
  - **Crash, read from code:** 0x80121df0 is inside **0x80121B4C = the EAGL dynamic loader of an ELF `.o` resource** (allocates a 0x430 object, then byte-swaps the ELF header **in place** (e_type .. e_shstrndx at +0x10-+0x32), the section table etc. and applies relocations in place); wrappers 0x80122B40 / 0x80122BB8; users: PosePaletteBank `%s.o` (0x80017E6C / 0x80017F8C), CAnimationBank::_InitData (0x80018094), CMorph (0x800199FC), CINEMA::CCinemaCrowd::Init (`assets/cincrowd.viv`, crowdmodels / crowdshapes, 0x800DEAF4 / 0x800DEFC8), SphereMapManager (0x8010E6FC), menu-picture models (0x8019F6FC). The crash reads +0xC of a section pointer that came out 0 because a header value is out of range = **the loader ran a second time on data it had already swapped / relocated**. Likely mechanism: a scene / crowd / pose file finishes loading during the fight (file data in a block that is NOT rewound: SysHeap / file-system blocks), the game logic runs the loader in frame X, a rollback rewinds to before X, and the re-simulation runs the loader again on the already-converted data. Fix options: (a) make the loader idempotent (DOL patch: mark the ELF after conversion, e.g. an e_ident padding byte, and skip swap + relocation when marked), (b) put completed-load buffers into the rollback state.
  - **Crash mechanism confirmed from the code (22 October):** the loader takes a descriptor r3 (+0x10 = file data, +0x14 = size, +0x18 = optional separate section-table pointer) and byte-swaps the ELF header **unconditionally** at 0x80121BB8-0x80121D60 (no "already done" check; e_ident is not changed). Run twice, the header is swapped back, e_shoff (+0x20) comes out huge, `cmplw e_shoff, size` fails, +0x18 is 0 -> section-table pointer (sp+0x9C) = 0 -> `lwz r6, 0xC(r31)` at 0x80121DF0 with r31 = 0 = exactly "Invalid read from 0x0000000c". Allocations inside: 0x430 object via the function pointer at 0x802F9938 (*(0x80300000-0x66C8)), two more blocks into object +0x420 / +0x424 (0x801223FC / 0x8012241C). Planned fix (Dolphin side, in the rollback engine): HLE hook on the loader entry during rollback fights; copy the file data (size +0x14) before conversion, tagged with the frame of the newest saved state (like rbtag); on a rewind to S restore the original bytes of every copy tagged >= S (the re-simulation then converts fresh data); drop copies once their frame is confirmed. Needs the lab to check which heap the buffer and the allocations live in.
  - **Lab result 22 October (stress mode, rollback of 1-5 frames every frame, Babylon):** ~50 frames into the fight (frames 6718-6720, fight start 6668) the MAIN thread runs the loader on ~30 small ELF files (0x330-0x10F0 bytes each, descriptors 0x8063cbe0.. step 0x40, LR 0x80122B90 = wrapper 0x80122B40), all inside ONE heap-0 block (0x80768510, tag word 0x424D0080 = rbtag-tagged, NOT rewound); the first ones during re-simulated frames. **After a rewind the game did NOT call the loader again for them** (the bookkeeping that decides "file ready -> convert" is not rewound either) -> no crash there, and the first fix version (undo every conversion on a rewind) was WRONG: it restored unconverted bytes that were never converted again. **Fix v2 (in the fork, `HookLoader`):** keep a copy of the original bytes at every conversion of fresh data (ELF with little-endian e_ehsize `34 00` at 0x28); when the loader is called on data that is already converted (`00 34`) and a copy of the same address + size exists, restore the copy first, so the second conversion works like the first; copies dropped when their frame is confirmed and at fight end. Lab-only env `FFNY_RB_NOLOADFIX=1` disables it. Boot-time calls: also from the loading thread 0x806D8AE0 (one into fake VMEM 0x7E127000).
  - **Blazin' desync reproduced in the lab on ONE machine (22 October):** script `blazin_nopoke.txt` (babylon_long's fight inputs + a C-stick flick every 25 pad steps alternating P1 / P2; Robert: Blazin' mode is triggered like a taunt = flick of the right stick / C-stick), stress mode `FFNY_RB=3:3 FFNY_RB_STRESS=5` (both pads local, forced rollback 1-5 frames every frame). Fighter 0's +0x93E bit 0 set at frame ~8955 (Blazin' mode); from frame ~9245 `STRESS MISMATCH` (re-simulated frame != first visit), identically in two runs. More fight-time loader calls: frames 7126 / 7366 / 8226 (files 0x61B0 + 0x1100 / 0x3A50 + 0xEA0 bytes) - in rewound heap blocks (tag 0x424D0400), re-called after every rewind, harmless. **Lab pitfalls:** stress needs both pads local (`FFNY_RB=1:3` without a transport stops after 10 s: "no inputs from the other side"); RAM pokes from the pad script (FFNYBench `poke`) land in re-simulated frames and cause fake mismatches / an OSPanic (halt loop 0x8025B028) -> use `FFNY_RB_MOM=N` (Dolphin sets both momentum bars to 98.5 every N fight frames inside Inject, deterministic). New lab env: `FFNY_RB_STRESS_FROM=frame`, `STRESS DIFF` lines (fighter / offset / first / now at the first mismatches). FFNYBench ops added: `padc P BTN SX SY CX CY` (C-stick), `poke`, `pokef`, WATCH of fighter +0x93C.
  - **Blazin' desync CAUSE found (22 October):** first stress diffs were only in shared random generator 2 (0x803BE03C, words +0x70 / +0x80). Lab trace (`FFNY_RB_RNGLOG=from:to`, HLE hook on the generator core 0x800B7DAC, phase U = logic update / T = after the tick hook / R = render, plus 3-level back trace): every draw matched between first visits and re-simulations EXCEPT one caller that draws only on first visits, once per rendered frame: **0x800EDA24** (randint(gen2, 255) at 0x800EDB4C when its object's +0x4F4 and +0xA0 are set), called at 0x80086690 from the mode's render method **0x800838F4** (main loop render part: 0x80004D88 bl 0x8001ED94, 0x80004D98 bl 0x800838F4). Render runs once per DISPLAYED frame, never for re-simulated frames, so the number of gen-2 draws per logic frame differs between the machines -> the fight's next random decision differs -> desync (the effect is on in Blazin' mode). **Fix (fork, Dolphin side):** hook 0x80004D88 marks the render part ('R', until the next PRE / tick); hooks on the generator cores (gen 1 0x8001FC88 state in r3 = 0x8035D588, gen 2 0x800B7DAC object in r3 = 0x803BE03C) swap r3 to the private states rbaudrng already has in the DOL (gen 1 0x8049A210, gen 2 0x8049A228; checked: stub 0x8049A200 = `lis r3,0x804A; subi r3,r3,0x5DF0`) for draws in the render part during rollback fights. Lab env `FFNY_RB_NORNGFIX=1` disables it.

Seen at frames 7178 / 10549 in stress runs. Note: in non-MMU mode Dolphin does NOT raise a game exception on an invalid read (it shows its alert and continues), so these are other exceptions; cause still open.

(1) **Desync:** the hash message 'H' now also carries 8 part hashes (fighter 1-4 = 0x803F1144 + i * 0x12F8, fight RNG 0x803F6AC8, generator 1 0x8035D588, generator 2 0x803BE03C, frame counter 0x80440C24); on a mismatch: "FFNY DESYNC at frame F: <parts> differ. Mine now: P1 state / blazin byte +0x93E / momentum, P2 ..., rollbacks (max, last from->to), loads / fixed, render draws".   - **Second Blazin'-mode desync source: the VI retrace counter (22 October, lab):** per-frame draw recording at the first stress mismatch showed one generator-1 draw (call chain 0x800E0A10 <- 0x800863FC <- 0x800832C8, the mode's logic update) landing on a different frame in the re-simulation (8861) than on the first visit (8863). It is the countdown at object +0x58 in **0x800E0994** (object 0x806955E0, in a rewound heap block: block 0x8060C3B0, 0xDC120 bytes) that draws a new countdown randint(gen1) & 15 at -1; the function first reads **0x8024E22C = getter of the VI retrace counter 0x8043FC20** (r13 = 0x804477E0, -0x7BC0), which the SDK's VI thread **0x8024D6F0** increments through **0x8024CD94** on every retrace message (with 0x8043FC24, read by the render pacing at 0x8024E0D4-0x8024E1F0). 21 game call sites read the getter (e.g. 0x80021DE0 = the per-update timing object, already faked by Inject; 0x800E09C8; 0x80083990/0x800839A4; 0x800F21E4; 0x8011C538..). Re-simulated frames run inside one retrace, and the two machines see different counts per logic frame -> logic diverges. (0x8024E234 returns -0x7BC4 = a constant set at init, harmless.) **Fix (fork):** `HookViCount` replaces the getter: during a rollback fight's logic update ('U' phase) it returns `time_base + 2 * (frame - fight start)` (= what Inject writes to the timing object +8), otherwise the real counter; 0x8043FC20-0x8043FC27 excluded from the rollback state (real-time system state). Lab env `FFNY_RB_NOVIFIX=1` disables it. Lab tool: `FFNY_RB_PROBE=hexaddr` logs r3 + its heap block (tag, size, first word) at that address once per r3.

  - **22 October, night (fork `ffny-rollback` f166802, Actions run 39843710181, artifact Dolphin-ffny-f166802...; lab branch `lab-22oct`):**
    - **Netcode:** inputs on `FFNY_INPUT_CHANNEL` (MessageID::FFNYInput 0x6D, ENet unsequenced, relayed by the host on the same channel; all other messages stay reliable). Message 'J': port, first frame, count, 4 acks (newest confirmed frame of each port), sender frame, sender lead, then per frame a flag byte (0 = same record as before, 1 = 148 bytes follow); starts at the oldest frame the other side has not acked, max 1100 bytes. OnRemoteInput ignores frames <= confirmed or >= confirmed + RING. Resend every 8 ms while waiting, reliable resend at fight end. `Common::ENet::DisableUnreliableThrottle` (enet_peer_throttle_configure(interval, SCALE, 0)) on both sides - the bundled ENet otherwise drops unreliable packets while the RTT is above average. Old messages were ~1.2 KB per frame per player (~570 kbit/s), all reliable in-order. Both players need the same build (channel count).
    - **Time sync (GGPO style):** lead estimate = f - remote frame (theirs reported in 'J'); diff of both estimates (16-frame history) >= 2 -> one frame later via `CoreTiming::AddThrottleDelay(16683 us)`, at most every 20 frames; after a wait the throttle reference is shifted by the waited time (before: Dolphin sprinted to catch up and ran into the limit again). Lab two-Dolphin (`lab/run2.sh`: custom RTC 946684800, SIDevice0/1 = 6, menu script blazin_nopoke, fight scripts fightA / fightB per pad; UDP lag 60, jitter 10, `FFNY_RB_LOSS=5 FFNY_RB_BURST=3`): without sync one side 398 waits / 5.2 s and 0 rollbacks (other side all), with sync 763 / 751 rollbacks, 60 / 63 waits (0.9 / 1.0 s). Lab: `FFNY_RB_NOSYNC` disables.

Lab watch: counter 0,0,0 on the first visit vs 1,2,3 in the re-simulation, object identical. Ranges merged to 0x80395660-0x803D0960. Other gaps of the static ranges may hide similar logic-read words (they were cut as render-written).

  - **Blazin' desync, likely the same family:** loads during a fight complete at a time that depends on emulated time; with rollback (re-simulation, waits) the emulated time per logic frame differs between the two machines, so a load that the logic waits for (Blazin' camera / scene / crowd resources?) completes at different logic frames -> divergence. Fix idea: pause the logic (render only, no frame advance) on both machines while the file-system queue (0x804018E0) is busy, so every logic frame starts with no load in flight; plus (a)/(b) above. Needs the lab (game ISO + Linux build of the fork with FFNYLab) to reproduce: crash = stress / loopback mode around the first scene load; Blazin' = scripted inputs that fill momentum.
