  - **Robert's local test before it (two Dolphins on one PC, the current build):** no desync in the OSD or chat, several Blazin' activations, no crash.
  - **Network simulation (Robert's request, for testing unstable pings without his friend):** netplay window > Network (host only) > **"FFNY: Simulate Ping / Jitter..."** (non-modal; Apply works during a fight): ping (added round trip, half per direction), jitter (+- per message, so the round trip varies within ping +- jitter), packet loss %, ping jumps (+X ms for Y ms about every Z s, randomised 50-150 %), plus the rollback input delay (NetPlay/FFNYRollbackDelay, from the next game start). Implemented in `NetPlayServer` (SimQueue / SimFlush / SimServiceTimeoutMs / SimForgetPlayer): every message between the host's server and each other player is held back in both directions (incoming before OnData, outgoing in Send); the host's own client (pid 1) is never delayed; reliable messages keep their order per player and direction (a late one holds back the later ones, like a real resend; a "lost" reliable message arrives one round trip + jitter later); unsequenced rollback inputs (FFNY_INPUT_CHANNEL) are timed independently, may overtake each other and are dropped at the loss rate. Applies to all netplay traffic, so Dolphin's ping list shows the simulated ping. Always off when the netplay window opens; values kept in Dolphin.ini (NetPlay/FFNYSimPing, FFNYSimJitter, FFNYSimLoss, FFNYSimSpike, FFNYSimSpikeLength, FFNYSimSpikeEvery). Applying posts a chat line from the host. For a local test: host = instance 1, instance 2 joins 127.0.0.1; only the host sets the simulation.

Ping 120 / jitter 50: one match, 0 desyncs, no crash; 1,134 rollbacks (max 7, 2,630 re-simulated), 4 waits (0.1 s); logs max 7 of 96, heap 2 lowest 508 KB. Speed counter of the instance Robert plays dips briefly below 100 % (= time sync / waits while the other instance re-simulates; both instances share one PC's CPU). **Voice lines can differ between the two machines without a desync** (e.g. Snowman's Blazin' success lines): the audio module's random draws go to the private generator (rbaudrng), whose draw count depends on the voices playing (machine-dependent), and since 528ba1b / 32c1613 render and effect-manager draws use it too -> cosmetic only, the hashed state stays identical. Possible fix: a separate deterministic private generator for the speech-choice call sites only (which of the 12 audio-module sites, not yet known; needs the lab).

P1 state 103 blazin 01 momentum 99.0 | P2 state 104 ... | rollbacks 948 (max 5, last 15212->15210) | loads 1085 fixed 60 | heap free lists ok | rbtag logs max 4/4/2, heap2 510 KB heap3 756 KB". Read from code: 0x80018408(bank, name) = animation lookup by name: `lwz r3,4(r31)` (bank +4 = name table) -> 0x800F7294 binary search (count at table +4, entries +0x14) -> crash because bank +4 = 0. 0x8001837C.. (the function before) is the bank unload (frees, then zeroes +4 / +8). Callers of the lookup: 0x8000C548.., 0x8001B044.., 0x8007C838.. (cinema scene code), 0x800DC8EC, 0x800DE12C / 0x800DE180 (cinema crowd), 0x800EBDA8; some check for 0, some do not. Reading: a bank empty in this timeline, most likely a file finished loading / was converted in a frame that a rollback threw away (the load bookkeeping is not rewound, the bank object is) - the KO / extro scene files are loaded during the fight. **Fork 60462a2 (Windows build: Actions run 39969058253, artifact Dolphin-ffny-60462a2...):** Replace hook 0x8001841C (`FFNYRollbackBankGuard`): empty bank -> "not found" (r3 = 0 via the epilogue 0x8001843C) + one chat report per fight with bank, name pointer, call chain, last rollback, last finished file request and last conversion frame; fight-over line adds file-system busy frames (0x804018D4 current / 0x804018E0 queue), finished requests, rollbacks across a finished request / across a conversion of fresh data (main thread), empty-bank lookups caught; both crash reports add the call chain. Possible real fix if the statistics confirm it: no rollback across load completions (run lockstep while the file system is busy, plus a few frames after) - cost depends on how often the FS is busy in fights (music streaming may use it), hence measured first. **Menu stutter with 2 % loss:** expected - menus are lockstep netplay over reliable messages, and the simulation delays a "lost" reliable message by one round trip, holding back the ones after it (~1 per second at 2 %).

The bank was filled by a conversion at frame 5229 that a rollback threw away; the game never converts the same file again. **Fork 92e2089: lockstep while loading** - while the file system is busy (0x804018D4 / 0x804018E0, sampled before each update and at each VI field), after a conversion of fresh data by the main thread, during the first 20 frames of a fight, and for 20 frames (LOAD_TAIL) after any of these, frame f runs only when MinConfirmed() >= f (a pending rollback also waits for that, so its re-simulation uses only confirmed inputs). Reading: the lockstep was triggered by every loader call on fresh data, including the many small ELF files in rewound heap blocks that each re-simulation converts again (harmless) -> nearly every rollback restarted 20 frames of lockstep. **Fork 9e753df:** only conversions of data outside the rollback state (block not in heap 0, or rbtag-tagged 0x80, e.g. SysHeap) count for the lockstep, the statistics and the warning. Robert's idea "load every file on the loading screen instead of in the match": not possible from outside (the game decides what it loads and when, and memory is tight); the narrowed lockstep should cost only the ~35 busy frames + tails.
  - **Friend's Dolphin crashed after fights, during / after the post-fight resync (twice, build 92e2089):** "GFX FIFO: Unknown Opcode (0xda @ 0x1e254920005, preprocess=true)" and "(0x3e @ 0x179491f0000, preprocess=true)"; the resync was also very slow. preprocess=true = the CPU-side command preprocessing of Dolphin's deterministic dual-core GPU thread (Dual Core + netplay / fake-completion), i.e. independent of the video backend. Reading so far (not reproduced): after loading the host's state the client's video buffer / preprocess position and the emulated FIFO no longer line up (FifoManager::DoState sets seen = pp_read = read; the GPU thread may run the loaded [read, write) range before the CPU has preprocessed it; the comment in VideoState.cpp assumes "the GPU should be synced around state save/load"). Workaround suggested: host turns Dual Core off (cpu_thread is taken from the host in netplay; GPUDeterminismMode is NOT synced, so a mismatch between the players is possible). Slow resync: host's "Limit Chunked Upload Speed" and the network simulation (it also delays the resync data) to be checked. Next: reproduce save on one Dolphin / load on another with Dual Core + fake-completion in the lab.
  - **Resync crash also with Dual Core off (18:20):** "GFX FIFO: Unknown Opcode (0x75 @ ..., preprocess=false)" on the friend's side right as the host's state loads; Robert remembers the resync working for the friend with the first build (39b7cbf); between two Dolphins on Robert's PC it works. Nothing in the FIFO / state / netplay code changed since 39b7cbf that explains it (checked: diff 39b7cbf..HEAD). Leading theory: a half-loaded state. State::DoState loads the video part first (VideoCommon_DoState: BP / CP / XF / texmem / TMEM / **Fifo** / CommandProcessor ... / FramebufferManager / TextureCache ...); the EFB contents and the texture cache's EFB copies depend on backend / internal resolution / "Save Texture Cache to State" and are huge at high resolutions (= the slow resync); if the client cannot read them the PointerWrap leaves read mode, the CPU / RAM part is never applied, and the client runs its own RAM with the host's FIFO state. **Fork b95c0dd (Actions run 39986517143):** `g_state_skip_texture_cache` (VideoState.h) set around the FFNY resync save -> no EFB / EFB-copy data in the state (client clears its EFB, decodes textures from RAM); CRC32 of the compressed state in the FFNYState message (after the frame), client loads only on a match and reports to both players: size, CRC match, load ok / FAILED, and its backend, internal resolution, Dual Core, GPUDeterminismMode, CPU clock; host shows its own size + CRC. (9e753df, built just before, is included.)

Needs Dolphin's lockstep pad / Wii remote / GBA data exchange (client -> server -> clients, pad buffers that assume in-order exactly-once delivery) redone with frame numbers, redundancy and acknowledgements - a bigger change to core netplay; not done yet, Robert to decide.

  - **Diagnostics for the two open problems (always on in rollback fights, reported once per fight in the chat):** (1) every frame before the update and right after every rewind, the free list of every heap (table 0x8040BD70; walk from [h+0x20] via +0x10 to the 'BS' sentinel h+0x10, checking range, 'BF' magic and the back link +0x14; heaps whose allocator mutex has an owner, word h+0x44, are skipped as possibly mid-update) -> "FFNY WARNING: broken heap free list found before the update / right after the rewind A->B ..." with heap, node, frame, last rollback; (2) a full rbtag log (acquire / release / free 96, deferred main-heap frees 63) -> warning (from then on rbtag does such calls at once, so a later rewind can repeat them: double release / double free); (3) both crash reports now add the heap free-list state and the rbtag log fill. Compiled here (g++ syntax check of all changed files, Qt dialog against Dolphin's Qt 6.8.3 headers); not run (no lab this session).
  - **Null-context sound crash, traced further (read from code):** the stop function 0x801D7DD0 selects one of the 4 stream channels (tables 0x803DA148, stride 0x928; select function 0x801D8624 sets the current context 0x802FE714 and 0x802FE724) whose handle matches the bits of the game's handle; if none matches, the context stays 0 and 0x801D7EE0 writes to 0xB8. The caller 0x8001106C (from the stop-all 0x80011040) stops the handle in **0x80440AD0 = the game's music stream handle**, set at 0x80010F40 by the music start 0x80010EA0 (stream start 0x801D79D4, file `music/%s/main.mus`, string 0x8029DDA0; channel word 0x80440B20), called from the audio module at 0x80031244 / 0x8003191C. 0x80440AD0 is bss = rewound game state, the stream tables are not: a music start / stop inside a frame that is later rewound leaves the game's handle and the library out of step (and a music start in a thrown-away frame keeps playing). Fix options: (a) exclude 0x80440AD0 / 0x80440B1C / 0x80440B20 from the rollback state (handle always matches the library; risk: a re-simulated start starts a second stream); (b) rbtag-style handling of music start / stop (HLE hooks on 0x80010EA0 / 0x8001106C: no second start for the same frame in a re-simulation, stops of unconfirmed frames deferred). Needs the lab to see when FFNY starts / stops music during a fight.
  - **Allocator crash, current reading:** the garbage next pointer seen (0x6E9C7074) looks like two neighbouring 16-bit PCM samples (0x6E9C, 0x7074), i.e. audio data written into memory the heap considers free. Candidates: (a) an audio writer (stream decoder / DSP / sound library) still using a block that a rewind made free again (allocated by the main thread after the save point); (b) rbtag log overflow (release / free done at once, repeated after a rewind: double free in SndHeap); (c) render-phase heap allocations (main thread, after the save) undone by a rewind while render-side words (excluded from the state) still point at them. The new warnings tell (b) directly and say which heap and whether the list broke in a restore or in normal running.

The log of that fight has the fight-over line but no "my state" line (= the host waited for the other machine).
  - **Fight loop read from the DOL (`FUN_801f9fc4`):** fight state 0x803F0FF8: **+8 = 0x803F1000** state machine 0 (init 0x8020AD34, then +0x12C = (x & ~1) | 2) -> 1 (running: clock 0x802037B8 or the match-end wrapper 0x80001E40 -> `FUN_80203c04` nonzero -> 2) -> 2 (-> 3, +0x18 = +0x14); the state machine is skipped while **+0x12C (0x803F1124) & 0x60 and not 0x80** (0x801FA0D8; `FUN_801fa18c`, the fighter update, skips the same way). +0x12C is cleared with 0xFFFE017F at each loop start (bits 0x80 and 0x200-0x10000 are per-frame), set to 1 by the fight init 0x801F99E0 (after a 0x14C memset). **Bit 0x8 = fight decided**, set by: match end 0x80203E38 (only when 0x803F0DA0 == 4, which is checked first), 0x80238720 (path that marks fighter +0x932 |= 0x20, sets +0x130 |= 0x40, calls the match end; only when 0x803F0DA0 == 0x11), 0x80206BF4 (fighter +0x932 & 0x400), 0x80228FAC, 0x801FBD54, 0x801FA0B8 (end of the +0x12C bit-4 slow-motion phase, timer +0x13C). Match end `FUN_80203c04`: per fighter (+0x28 != -1, +0x932 bit 0x200 = out) counts newly lost (bit 0x8, cleared) / remaining (+0x937 bit 4 = team); decided -> +0x14 / +0x18, `FUN_802040d0`, `FUN_8020485c`, +0x12C |= 8, returns 1; not decided -> elimination recorded via 0x80002780 (patch cave). `FUN_80204000` = decides the end (bit 0 of 0x803F0D9A = always; bit 1 = teams). Disassembled here with a small own Python PPC decoder (scratch, pypi / capstone not reachable from this session).

"No inputs from the other side for 10 s" no longer switches rollback off for the session (both then ran on unsynchronised): it ends the fight and waits for the resync.   - **"Possible desync detected: robert_belko might have desynced at frame 180" (once 540) after every resync** = Dolphin's time-base check (every 60 frames after the resume; 0 / 60 / 120 matched -> the machines really ran apart). Cause in Dolphin: `CEXIChannel::DoState` re-inserts a GCI-folder memory card (AddDevice None + delayed ChangeDevice) when the loaded state's card header data differs and no movie runs; the header data is made at boot from the **wall-clock time** (EXI.cpp `format_time`), so it always differs between two machines -> after every resync only the client's game saw the card pulled out and put back. Also `CEXIMemoryCard::DoState` saves the card's contents / device state only while a movie runs. **f38a005:** `State::g_ffny_resync_save` (set by FFNYSaveCompressed) saves the card like a movie state; with `g_state_foreign_load` the client does not re-insert the card, `GCMemcardDirectory` keeps its own folder and maps the loaded saves' file names into it. Not yet tested.
  - **Slowdown with input delay 2 = lockstep while loading, not the laptop:** fight-over lines: lockstep for file loads 1,776 frames (15.4 s waiting) of a 7,448-frame fight and 3,211 frames (27.2 s) of 11,808; with delay 4: 732 frames, 0.5 s. File system busy in only 55-105 frames, but each busy stretch (and each conversion of data outside the state) adds LOAD_TAIL = 20 frames, and with ~3-5 frames of one-way delay (sim) each lockstep frame waits unless the input delay covers it. The laptop side still had 52-146 rollbacks (max 4-5) at delays 4 / 2. f38a005 adds to the fight-over line: load episodes, lockstep frames only because of the tail, conversions of data outside the rollback state and the largest gap (frames) between the last busy frame and such a conversion -> data for shortening the tail (or making it apply only to loads that are followed by conversions).

Robert's host Dolphin.ini (saved 21:25): CPUThread False, GPUDeterminismMode auto, Vulkan, internal res 1x, EFBAccessEnable False, overclock 200 %, SlotA = GCI folder; the laptop reported D3D 1x, dual core on, auto. **Correction (Robert, 21:51): his game's own config (Game Config of modded.iso / GNWE69) overrides Dolphin.ini: Dual Core on, Synchronize GPU thread on, Deterministic dual core = fake-completion; the laptop had auto** - in netplay with Dual Core, auto also uses the deterministic GPU thread, so the two were effectively the same; Strict Settings Sync was on. The server reads the settings with the game INI layer loaded, so a2d47bb sends fake-completion. Stock Dolphin syncs CPUThread from the host but **not GPUDeterminismMode** -> **a2d47bb: NetSettings.gpu_determinism_mode from the host** (start-game packet after ffny_load_lockstep; netplay config layer). Suggested to Robert: "Strict Settings Sync" in the netplay window (syncs internal resolution etc.), and one Dragon House session with "FFNY Rollback (fights)" off to see whether plain lockstep shows the warning too.
  - **Robert's questions answered:** Dolphin netplay already runs over ENet = UDP with its own reliability; lockstep while loading already uses the rollback input channel (unsequenced, redundant). The cost is latency, not the transport: a lockstep frame needs the other side's input of that frame, which arrives one-way latency minus input delay later. Long-term idea for full-time rollback across loads: make file loads deterministic in logic frames (HLE the game's file-system request processing so a read completes inside the same logic update, from Dolphin's disc image), so a re-simulation repeats the load exactly; the loader-copy fix already handles re-conversions. Needs the lab.
- **24 October, 22:00: Dolphin's lockstep netplay read for desync sources (Robert's request; fork commit 58ff4c0, Actions run 40127972379):**
  - **Pad exchange is sound:** each machine pushes its local pad states into its own buffer and sends exactly the same states (reliable ENet channel, in order); every player pops the same sequence per pad at the same emulated polls, also when the buffer size changes (the change only inserts / drops states that are also sent). All GCPadStatus fields the game sees are sent (only the Triforce `switches` byte is not). Controller combos (X+Y+START origin, B+X+START reset) use emulated time. Changing ENet reliability would not change determinism.
  - **Gaps found:** (1) GPUDeterminismMode not synced -> fixed in a2d47bb; (2) **GCI-folder memory card header from each machine's boot wall-clock time** (EXI.cpp `format_time`; used when the folder has no `MC_SYSTEM_AREA`, as in netplay's synced folders: only the game's .gci files are sent) -> every machine's game reads a different card serial / format time into RAM; **58ff4c0: with netplay running, format_time = host's initial RTC value - GC epoch**; (3) **OnFrame game patches** from each player's own game INI are loaded locally and NOT synced (only AR / Gecko codes are, with "Sync AR/Gecko Codes") - not changed; (4) the video backend is not synced (only matters with EFB access from the CPU, bounding box or EFB copies to RAM; Robert: EFB access skipped, EFB copies to texture only).
  - **Robert's GameSettings/GNWE69.ini** (overrides Dolphin.ini): FastDiscSpeed True, CPUThread True, GPUDeterminismMode fake-completion, SyncGPU True, plus a leftover **memory breakpoint `803f27b0` and a watch `803f27b4`** (from earlier debugging) - suggested removing it (memory checks switch the JIT to slow memory accesses; a break would pause only that machine).

Scripts in `/home/claude/lab` (rb.sh, run2.sh, pair.sh; bench = lab22 + lab23 files incl. FFNYBench.inc). rb.sh has no custom RTC: single-machine runs differ between runs (game seeds from the clock).
  - **FFNY's file system, read from the DOL:** EA FILESYS struct 0x804018C0 (+0x8 priority limit, +0xC queued count, +0x10 "in callback" count, +0x14 request being processed, +0x18 handle table, +0x20 queue head (nodes linked by +0x2C, sorted by +0x10), +0x24, mutex +0x28 = 0x804018E8). Request node: +0 user arg, +2 op (2/8 open, 3 close, 4 read, 5 write, 6 size, 7/9/0xA, 0xB), +4 cancel, +8 result (1 ok, -1 cancelled, -2 failed), +0x10 priority, +0x14 arg 2, +0x18 offset, +0x1C length, +0x20 dest, +0x24 file handle (+0 type: 1 = member of an archive -> archive handle *(h+0x108)+0xC and offset + h+0x10C; 3 = other device, 0x80253F80.. paths; DVDFileInfo at handle + 0x108: +0x30 disc offset, +0x34 length), +0x28 callback (r3 = +0, r4 = result, r5 = +0x14), +0x2C next. Start / kick `0x80244408(node)` (rbtag's queue guard jumps in at its first instruction): enqueue by priority, start the head if idle (+0x14); op 4 read -> `0x802491B8(handle, dest, offset, length)` stores the parameters at 0x8040197C.. and OSSendMessage(0x8040BCB8, 0, block); op 5 write is synchronous and calls done directly (0x80244820: li r3,1; bl done). **File-system thread** `0x802490BC` (OSCreateThread at 0x8024894C: thread object 0x804099A0, priority 2 = above the main thread, queue 0x8040BCB8 with 32 slots): OSReceiveMessage, then type 3 -> 0x80253F90 / 0x80253F80, else `0x80248EC8(DVDFileInfo, dest, offset, length)` (aligned DVD reads with bounce buffer, state word 0x80401940, 5 = done) and waits with OSReceiveMessage (0x8024914C / 0x80249170) until state 5, then `0x80244880(1)` = done: node +8 result, +0x14 cleared, +0x10 count up, callback, count down, kick(0) for the next request. OS: OSReceiveMessage 0x80274E5C sleeps at 0x80274EB0 (bl OSSleepThread 0x802776E8); OSSendMessage 0x80274D94; external-interrupt dispatcher calls the handler at 0x80274C94 and reschedules at 0x80274C9C. (A small own Python PPC decoder in the session scratchpad did the disassembly; it prints `blr` for `blrl` - indirect calls.)
  - **Attempts:** (1) synchronous reads by Dolphin inside the request start (HLE replace of 0x802491B8 + continue at 0x80244820) -> done / callbacks ran in the main thread: OS run queue broken early in the boot (crash in the scheduler at 0x80276D60 from OSUnlockMutex). (2) DVD reads with 0 latency -> crash (null jump from the interrupt dispatcher). (3) Moving pending DVD completions to "now" when the file-system thread is about to wait, from boot on -> OS run queue broken after ~4,300 reads (also with the move done at its OSSleepThread call) -> some boot code expects reads to take time.
  - **Result (709bb7a): instant loads only while a rollback fight runs.** HLE Start hook at 0x80274EB0: when the sleeping thread is the file-system thread (0x800000E4 == 0x804099A0) on its queue (r31 == 0x8040BCB8), `DVDThread::FinishPendingReadsNow()` (pending read ids tracked, savestated) moves the completion events of all pending reads to now -> the DI interrupt is taken right after the switch to the next thread, the file-system thread (priority 2) runs again first and finishes the load (and every further step of a multi-part read) before the main thread executes anything. Conversions of loaded data no longer trigger lockstep and no longer warn; lockstep remains for reads in flight from before the fight start and the first 20 frames. **Lab:** single Dolphin, stress 5 (rollback 1-5 frames every frame), no lockstep: fight to the KO, 2,683 instant reads, 28,677 re-simulated frames compared, 0 mismatches, 0 empty-bank lookups; two Dolphins over UDP, 50 ms lag +-15 jitter, delay 2, no lockstep: fight to the KO at frame 13,730 on both, ~820 rollbacks per side (max 4), 0-2 waits, 0 lockstep frames, ~1,850 instant reads, 0 desyncs, no crash. Not tested in the lab: environmental KO (window), pause / quit, Blazin' scripts with this change, the post-fight resync.
  - **rbtag follows Dolphin's fight flag (709bb7a):** rbtag v5 reads 0x803F1000 == 1 at 0x8049A670 / 0x8049A694 (SysHeap routing of system-named allocations) and 0x8049A8B0 / 0x8049A8B8 (deferred main-heap frees, done once the flag is not 1). 0x803F1000 stays 1 after a quit and after environmental KOs -> frees stayed deferred in the menus; **likely cause of the 24 October quit crash** (report: deferred frees 24 of 63, heap code 0x80249818). Dolphin patches both reads at install (lis/lwz -> 0x8049B5F4, lis/ori -> 0x8049B5F4) and writes **W_FIGHTW 0x8049B5F4** = 1 at the fight start, 0 at the fight end (the queued frees are then done at the next free). Not yet tested in-game.

  - **Blazin' flash (25 October, evening; fork commit 66587aa, Actions run 40203471656):** Robert's clips (offline, auto vs fake-completion, his 709bb7a build): not a flicker - with fake-completion the white flash fades to the green grading faster and ends darker / lower contrast (average end colour R 17 G 25 B 11 vs R 28 G 37 B 23); the HUD is not affected. **Lab with pictures:** the software renderer now runs without a window when FFNY_LAB is set (lab commit; SWGfx with no window = 640 x 480 backbuffer) and the harness command `xfb` gives the VI XFB address, so frames are read from emulated RAM (GFX.ini `XFBToTextureEnable = False`; YUYV 640 x 448, stride 1280) and converted with OpenCV. Robert's savestate (`blazin_performing_paused.sav`, single core, DSP HLE, ports 1 + 2 = standard controllers) loads only after one of the lab's own states (the DSP HLE ucode state; harness `loaddbg` reports the last savestate marker), needs SIDevice1 = 6 and one START. Dual core with GPUDeterminismMode none hung after loading a state in the lab (not investigated); single core and fake-completion work. **Result:** single core and fake-completion run the CPU identically (same PC samples, same logic counter), but the flash frames differ from the first frame after the white (single core ends at about R 58 G 92 B 67, fake-completion R 26 G 63 B 43) -> the GPU side reads different data. Pacing the CPU-side FIFO reader by the single-core GPU cost changed nothing (single core with SyncGpuOverclock 10 also unchanged); **waiting for the GPU thread right after a preprocessed GXDrawDone (`SyncGPU` in `ReadDataFromFifoOnCPU`) made fake-completion match single core** (last fade frames identical). Cause: with the deterministic GPU thread the draw-done is "completed" when the CPU thread preprocesses it, so the game starts the next frame while the GPU thread is still behind and then reads textures / vertex data the CPU has already rewritten. Fix 66587aa: always sync at draw-done in deterministic mode (host-side wait only: emulated timing and determinism unchanged; the same wait the normal dual core has). Robert to test: the flash vs offline auto, and speed at input delay 2 in netplay. Lab comparison picture `blazin_flash_lab.jpg`.

Log: 0 desyncs, all resyncs CRC-matched; **the laptop reported "lowest free KB heap3 0" in one fight (host 756 KB in the same fight)** - first time heap3 ran empty; nothing broke; suspect if the laptop ever crashes in a long fight. Chat lines unchanged; an aborted transfer now also posts a chat line. Not yet known whether OSD messages show while the emulation is paused for the resync.
  - **Heap 3 leak (25 October, evening; branch `ffny-sysfree` dc21cb7, Actions run 40214534909; diagnostics on ffny-rollback c7f7f1a, run 40208295775):** heap 2 = SndHeap (806E8500-807684D0), **heap 3 = rbtag's SysHeap (80768520-808684F0, 1 MB), both NOT rewound.** At the fight start (and KO scenes etc.) the main thread loads 22 small ELF modules (~126 KB) into SysHeap; a rewind across that frame makes the re-simulation load them again while the first copies stay allocated (lab stress: 1023 -> 516 KB after 3 rewinds; laptop: "across a file conversion" 3 -> 594 KB vs host 880, 4 -> 0 KB). The resync at the fight end wipes it on the client. Fix: Dolphin HLE-hooks rbtag's post-allocation hook (the address in 0x8040BD64) and logs SysHeap blocks allocated by the main thread (or the FS thread) with rbtag's W_FRAME; after a rewind to S the blocks of frames >= S that are still allocated wait in a queue and are put into rbtag's free log (FRL 0x8049BC00, count 0x8049B518) with frame 0 while it is below 48 entries, so the game frees them at its next settle (the first version filled the log to 96 -> rbtag warning). Lab: heap 3 back to exactly 897 KB a few frames after the rewinds. Diagnostics (c7f7f1a): heap samples every update, skipped while another thread holds the heap; fight-over line gives frame / re-simulated / predicted of heap 3's minimum; chat note below 64 KB. **Open: the lab's stress mode (rewind 1-5 frames every frame, ~7,500 rollbacks) crashes late in the fight in the sound / heap code with heap 2 (SndHeap) corruption (calls 8049A85C < 80249C9C < 8001F238 < 8049A03C < 801E0F00, or via the pool free 8028FC5C < 8049AB08) - also without the SysHeap fix (frames 9968 / 13995 / 13798 / 14198); never seen in real play. Lagged two-Dolphin lab fights (100 ms + 20 jitter, ~1,230 rollbacks) end fine; the lab client (no resync in the lab) crashed in the menus after the fight twice (heap code 0x80249818 front-end chain, and a sound thread) - in netplay the host's state replaces it.** Robert to test the sysfree build with a friend.
- **26 October, 00:37: sound calls held until their frame is confirmed (branch `ffny-sndhold`, fork commit 3017204, not on ffny-rollback):** main-thread calls of **0x801D3074** (varargs function, r4 = argument count, installed as a function pointer at r13-31904 by 0x801D3994; calls with r4 > 6 pass straight through) and **0x801D36B8** during fight updates (phase U / T, main thread 0x80432D10) are queued per frame (r3-r10 saved), the hook returns r3 = 1 at once, and the calls are made at a later PRE hook once the frame is <= min(MinConfirmed, f - 1) (registers saved / restored around the calls); a rewind to S drops the queued calls of frames >= S; at the fight end the queue is sent first. FFNY_RB_NOSNDHOLD=1 switches it off. Fight-over line: "sound events held / sent / dropped". Lab: stress mode (8,683 rollbacks) finishes the fight (before: heap damage at frame 15,407).

Host: 0 rollbacks, held = sent, dropped 0 in every fight. The game state is rewound, the sound engine is not -> extras piled up on the host (rising volume, missed music switches, the Gauntlet crackling, the crash); the client is reset by the resync after each fight. 3017204 lab results: lagged fight, each side held 278 calls, the engine got 152, both sides the same 152, 0 desyncs; stress mode (8,683 rollbacks per fight) without the hold crashed at frame 15,407 like every stress run, with it both runs finished. Still open there (thought lab-only): the second lab Dolphin, which never gets the after-fight resync, crashes ~2,000 frames after the fight while loading a file. Lab tools: branch `lab-25oct` on the fork. **More from that session (second paste by Robert):** in the friend session (sysfree build) Robert's host PC rolled back 600-1,700 times per fight (in earlier local tests mostly the laptop / client rolled back, and it is reset by the resync after every fight; the host never is), and **heap 2 (SndHeap) lowest free went 507 -> 506 -> 504 -> 500 -> 497 -> 495 -> 493 -> 492 KB over consecutive fights = a slow sound-memory leak on the host**; heard: slowly rising volume, music cues (Blazin' music, chorus, back to the fight instrumental) sometimes not switching, then crackling / too-fast sound at Gauntlet, then the crash at 0x801E7FC8 (sound thread, code 0x801E7xxx, reading through a broken pointer after a fight); the friend got the same error because he had just received the host's state at the resync. The SysHeap fix worked there (no heap 3 drop). Lab tools added on `lab-25oct`: FFNY_RB_DUMPAT (MEM1 dumps at fight frames, for comparing the sound library's memory after a fight with rollbacks against one without), FFNY_RB_COUNT (per-function call counters with callers, separately for first runs and re-simulations) and a call tracer; these found the repeated / wrong-guess events that led to 3017204. **26 October morning test of 3017204: heap 2 lowest stayed 509-510 KB in all 9 fights (no leak).**
  - **Reading of 0x801D36B8 (26 October; first read as "clear channel" - WRONG, corrected the same morning):** 20 callers, return value never used; each caller first writes a game-side mode word (0x804372A4 / A8 / AC) and then calls it with a channel number (1-6). It takes a busy flag (r13-31892); 0x801D372C(ch) takes the channel's next queued request (0x801D33BC), asks an optional filter callback (0x801D35E0: *0x803D99AC, default yes), and if accepted stops the current item (0x801D53A0 -> 0x801D3854 frees its blocks) and **starts the request (0x801D5B2C -> 0x801D58F0, a big parser / player)**, else discards it (0x801D347C). So it is **"service channel ch = play what was posted to it"**, as the 3017204 session named it; the posts (0x801D3074, called through the pointer at r13-31904) fill the queues. With both held (3017204) post and service keep their order. **The music faults with 3017204 are therefore not explained yet.** Candidates: calls that pass unheld (poster calls with r4 > 6, calls outside phase U / T or from other threads) overtaking held ones; game code that reads the sound library's state (e.g. the silence check 0x80033520(-1) before speech) seeing it a few frames behind; the filter callback reading game state at the later time. Next step: lab trace of an unpause and of the music after a Blazin' with 3017204, comparing what the engine receives with an offline run. A/B test suggested to Robert: same session with FFNY_RB_NOSNDHOLD=1, and only one Dolphin's audio on when testing on one PC (both instances play sound).

So some music faults are the game's own (likely disc streaming / timing: the interactive music's events are one-shot, a missed one is not sent again), not rollback. Baseline needed: offline (no netplay) fights, then netplay with FFNY_RB_NOSNDHOLD=1, then with the hold. With the hold off the fight-over line reads "sound events held 0, sent 0, dropped 0".

Missing music only on his PS2 (backup disc) and **online: yesterday with his friend (build before the hold, i.e. the sysfree / c7f7f1a line) and today with the hold build (two Dolphins, simulated lag)**. So the online faults come with rollback in both builds: before the hold, re-simulations re-made sound calls and thrown-away predictions made calls the real timeline never had (3017204's message already lists "missed music switches"); with the hold, the clear call ran late. (Checked anyway: without netplay none of the rollback hooks are installed.)
  - **Fork commit 47cddca (branch `ffny-sndhold2`, Actions run 40285483402 CANCELLED - do not use):** held only the poster and let 0x801D36B8 run at once, based on the wrong "clear" reading. Since 0x801D36B8 plays what was posted, it would run before the held post arrives and find the queue empty -> music changes delayed until the next service call or lost. Kept on the fork only for the record (it also adds the "FFNY: sound hold ..." chat line and the FFNY_RB_SNDHOLDALL switch). Robert stays on 3017204 (ffny-sndhold).
  - **Music path read from the DOL (26 October; Robert asked whether the conditions for music to play are too strict):** every interactive-music (MPF, tune.mpf) event goes through **`0x80031B54(ev)`** (24 callers, events 0-25; fight audio module: 0x80038014 ev 0, 0x80038154 ev 1, 0x8003826C ev 9 (fight end), 0x80038F00 ev 2 (Blazin' mode), 0x80039030 ev 3, 0x800390B0 ev 4, 0x80039280 ev 5 (chorus after a Blazin'), 0x80039A2C / 0x80039A7C ev 20 / 21, 0x80039BF8 ev 15, 0x80039C84 ev 16; music module: 0x80030A28 ev 5, 0x80031584 ev 23, 0x800315DC ev 9, 0x800319C0 ev 12, 0x80031A84 ev 17, 0x80031B24-0x80031B40 ev 24 / 14 / 16 / 25, 0x80031BF0 ev 10). **It sends the event only if** *(0x80440B40) == 0 (audio-off flag, cleared by the audio init 0x800267B0), *(0x80440BA0) != 0, (*(0x80440CAC) != 2 or ev == 17) and *(0x80440B38) == 2 (**correction, same day: this is the audio system's state - 2 is set once by the audio init 0x800267B0 (at 0x80027918), 0 by the audio shutdown 0x80028358 / 0x80026738 - not "the song is playing"**; *(0x80440BA0) = number of playlist entries, *(0x80440CAC) = music mode); otherwise the event is dropped with no retry. Then `0x80011108(ev)` -> **`0x801D70EC(*(0x80440AD0) = MPF player, (s16)ev)`** (EA's MPF post-event; fails with -18 when the library is not ready or 0x801D8624(-1) finds no stream channel). The real loss point is the library: **0x801D70EC returns -18 while no song player exists (handle *(0x80440AD0) = -1) and the game never retries** (see musicretry below) - a song that starts late (slow disc on the PS2) would lose more cues -> fits Robert's PS2 symptoms, not proven. **Under rollback: 0x80011108 / 0x801D70EC are NOT held by 3017204** (it holds only 0x801D3074 + 0x801D36B8, the request / speech system below), so music events are still re-sent by every re-simulation and sent from wrong predictions; with the MPF "save slot" events (2 / 6 save the position, 3 / 7 / 8 go back to it) a repeated or wrong event can leave the music in the wrong part (no fight loop after a Blazin', chorus cut short). To be confirmed in the lab (trace of 0x80031B54 / 0x801D70EC in loopback mode); fix candidate: hold 0x80011108 in the same queue (return value: callers to be checked).
  - **The request system that 3017204 holds (read 26 October):** event key = `0x801D29F0(ch, b, id)` (pure: id << 16 | b << 8 | ch); post `0x801D3074(key, n, args...)` allocates a 4 + 4n block (SndHeap) and calls **`0x801D2F08`**, which rejects the request (block freed, no retry) when: busy (r13-31896), unknown key, **random 0-99 (0x801D5EE4) > the event's chance byte (descriptor +9)**, or **no slot** (`0x801D2DBC`: 16 slots at 0x803D99F4 + 0x40 + 16 n: a free one, else one older than its timeout (descriptor +2, time 0x801D6838) is discarded, else one of the same channel with priority <= the new one is replaced); accepted -> stored with priority / time, and 0x801D2854(desc) != 0 -> serviced at once (0x801D3620). The service 0x801D36B8(ch) plays the queued request (0x801D372C -> filter callback 0x801D35E0 -> stop current 0x801D53A0, start 0x801D5B2C -> 0x801D58F0). Used by speech / announcer lines (wrappers 0x80024600-0x80026718 load the poster pointer from 0x8043FB40).
  - **Lab confirmation + fix (26 October, lab rebuilt from `lab-25oct` -> branch `lab-26oct` 5ed7b5f; files in `ffny_tools/rollback/lab26/`; ISO = modded-rollback_test (22 October, CRC 5a9482d3), Robert's GCI saves):** script `blazin_pause.txt` (Babylon, momentum poked, Blazin's, pause / unpause twice). Trace of 0x80031B54 / 0x801D70EC. **Reference (no rollbacks):** 29 music cues, e.g. 7702 ev 2, 8137 ev 16, 8605 ev 4, 9014 ev 3, 9085 ev 15, 9195 ev 5, ...; pause = ev 24 + 14, unpause (outside the rollback fight) = ev 16 + 25. **Loopback rollbacks (FFNY_RB=1:3 LOOP=5) with 3017204's hold:** the music engine got ev 2 twice at 7702 and three times at 13228, ev 4 / 3 / 5 / 16 twice, ev 15 from a predicted frame (9086 r) and again from the real one (9089 f) -> **music cues are not covered by 3017204** (10 of 28 sends were re-simulations; 15 of 34 without any hold). **With 0x80011108 held (same queue):** 0 sends during re-simulations, 15 sends, same sequence as the reference shifted by ~4 frames. **Second gap found:** a fight ended by force (no inputs 10 s / host's FFNYForceEnd) dropped the held calls -> the pause cues 24 / 14 were lost in the lab; now `SndFlushThenEnd` makes them first (lab: 24 / 14 sent at the forced end). **Stress (STRESS=5) with the music hold: whole fight to frame 21,137, no crash, each cue once.** Stress compare shows one word, **0x80D13B28**, differing between first runs and re-simulations on almost every frame (values ~0xB600-0xBBD4) - the same without the music hold, so it predates it; not identified (heap block; probably read from the sound engine), never caused a desync in real play. Lab notes: loopback mode needs FFNY_RB_NOLOCKSTEP=1 (the start / load lockstep waits for pad 1's input forever, then "no inputs for 10 s" ends the fight); the lab Dolphin build here had no apt (no ALSA / pulse / udev), configured with everything optional off (see lab26/README.txt).
  - **Fork commit 153a233 (branch `ffny-sndhold3` = 3017204 + this, Actions run 40299554982, built OK, artifact `Dolphin-ffny-153a233d1addd8d40dd61dd73fcd587434ab96c1`; not yet tested by Robert):** 0x80011108 held with the speech poster / service (FFNY_RB_NOMUSICHOLD=1 = music not held, FFNY_RB_NOSNDHOLD=1 = nothing held); held calls sent before forced fight ends; chat line "FFNY: sound hold on (speech + music)" when the hooks are installed; fight-over line ends with "(hold ...)". Compiled here: g++ -fsyntax-only (FFNYRollback.cpp, HLE.cpp); the same code ran in the lab build.
  - **Robert's question (strict conditions):** yes - 0x80031B54 drops a cue when the song is not in state 2 (no retry), and the speech request system rejects requests on its chance / slot / priority rules. A possible later DOL patch: remember a cue dropped because the song was not yet playing and send it once state 2 is reached (would also help the PS2 with slow discs; needs care for cues that are meant to be ignored, e.g. with music off).
  - **Robert's test of 153a233 (26 October, ~12:30; two Dolphins on his main PC, sim 100 / 20 / +40 ms for 500 ms every 10 s):** chat "FFNY: sound hold on (speech + music)" on both; 12 fights: 0 desyncs, every resync CRC-matched, **host and client sent exactly the same number of held calls in every fight** (46/46, 28/28, ... 42/42; client dropped 2-17 per fight, host 0), heap 2 509-511 KB, heap 3 fine; no volume rise noticed. Heard: pause / unpause now fine; once at the first Blazin' the Blazin' stem seemed to restart from its start after a stutter / load / under-100 % moment (that fight: file system busy 202 frames, lockstep for file loads 226 frames, 99 waits = 1.3 s; could not mute one window to tell which Dolphin it was); the chorus after a Blazin' still seems cut short by the fight instrumental compared with his memory (to compare offline). Open.
  - **Patch `musicretry` (26 October; source `ffny_tools/musicretry/`, entry in ffny_dolpatch.py; needs cave + bigspace; not with rbprof):** `0x80011120 bl 0x801D70EC` -> `bl 0x8049F000` (mr_send): post; if rejected (< 0) keep the cue (up to 8, in order; a repeat of the newest kept cue is merged); while cues are kept, new cues are kept behind them and the list is flushed. `0x80004D9C bl 0x8001EDD4` (end of the main loop's render part, once per displayed frame, never re-simulated) -> `bl 0x8049F1C0` (mr_frame): age the kept cues, drop those older than `musicretrytime` (float seconds at 0x8049F26C, default 10), flush with the current handle *(0x80440AD0), then the original call. Data 0x8049B520 (in rbtag's area 0x8049B400-0x8049BF00, excluded from the rollback state; free words 0x8049B520-0x8049B5EF): +0 count, +4 kept, +8 delivered later, +0xC expired, +0x10 last post result, +0x14 repeats merged, +0x20 8 x (cue, age float). Under rollback the held 0x80011108 runs mr_send at the PRE hook and mr_frame runs in the render part: both outside the rewound logic, data not rewound -> no effect on determinism. **Lab, no rollbacks, unpatched (new lab hook FFNYSndTrMusRes at 0x80011124 logs every result):** title 2955 cue 17 -> -18 (handle -1), 2961 cue 22 ok; venue select -> fight loading 6087 cues 16 / 25 / 17 -> -18 (handle -1), 6087 cue 12 ok, 6088 cue 0 ok; all fight cues ok. **Patched:** 5 kept, 5 delivered later (title cue 17 at 2961 with cue 22; 16 / 25 / 17 just before 12 in the same frame), 0 expired; the delivered title cue changed the menu flow (game then sent 23, 17, 17, 22 before the main menu; main menu reached ~190 frames later in the scripted run) - **i.e. in the lab the unpatched title screen never got its cue 17**; in Robert's Dolphin with real audio the timing may differ. Risk: cues meant for a song that is gone (16 / 25 / 17 when leaving the menus) now reach the next song right before its own start cues 12 / 0. Rollback lab (older build without the merge): fine, 2 cues 25 expired after 10 s with -18 in the loopback run's forced fight end. Delivered as `musicretry test build/start.dol` (= rollback test build start.dol + musicretry).
  - **Robert's offline test of musicretry (26 October, 13:51): WORSE - do not use.** Main menu: the jukebox song plays ~1 s, fades out, another song starts; fight loading screen: a different part of the loading theme plays briefly before it fades into the loading stems (the song did not start at the loading part); rematch YES: the intro song fades into silence; no Blazin' stem when Blazin' mode starts, but the chorus plays after a successful Blazin' move. **Reading: the rejected cues are meant to be dropped** - they are sent while one song is stopped and the next not yet opened (handle -1), and delivering them to the next song puts it in the wrong part / starts the next jukebox song; and a cue that keeps being rejected blocks every later cue behind it for up to 10 s (the "keep behind" rule) -> Blazin' cue lost / late. The music player is not persistent: each song is its own MPF player / stream instance (handle in 0x80440AD0) opened when the song starts and closed when it stops; between songs the handle is -1. Conclusion: the game's dropping is by design; the PS2 symptoms are more likely the disc streaming itself. Robert also asked whether rollback tracks "which part of the song is playing": no - that is the MPF engine's own state (sound library, never rewound); the game-side words (0x804372A4.., 0x80440B38..) are rewound game state. With 153a233 the engine gets each cue of the real timeline once (~4 frames late), so rollback no longer moves the song part; remaining chorus-cut reports online are to be checked with one Dolphin muted (two instances on one PC play both songs at once; Dolphin hotkey "Toggle Mute" or Config > Audio volume in one instance).
  - **Resync speed / rollback in menus (Robert, 26 October 14:00-14:10):** the post-fight resync takes ~5-6 s online with his friend, < 1 s with two Dolphins on his PC with simulated 100 ms ping -> bandwidth-bound (state ~15-17 MB LZ4). Ideas: (1) **delta resync** - the client sends a fingerprint per block of its own state, the host sends only differing blocks (the rewound game state is identical after a fight, only non-rewound parts differ; to be measured), plus stronger compression; (2) **rollback in the menus** (Robert's wish; removes the resync). **Lab feasibility test (lab-26oct + `FFNY_RB_ALWAYS=F`: rollback on everywhere from logic frame F; `FFNY_RB_STRESS=3`, from the title at 2500):** crash in a re-simulation at frame 2898 (title screen) inside the sound library: invalid reads / writes at 0x800D0ADC.., calls 0x801D8D94 < 0x801D8D08 < 0x8001117C (wrapper 0x80011164 -> 0x801D8C14) < 0x80031C90 < 0x8003134x (music module); afterwards thousands of "words differ" and the script timed out on the title. **Cause: outside fights the music module calls a whole family of sound-library wrappers in 0x800100E0-0x80011700** (~35 functions: audio init, bank loads 0x800106A0 (7 callers), stream start / stop 0x80010EA0 / 0x80010F5C / 0x8001106C / 0x800110AC (-> stop 0x801D7DD0), MPF event 0x80011108, volumes / fades 0x8001113C / 0x80011164 / 0x8001118C / 0x800111C0, MPF / stream controls 0x80010C54-0x80010DBC, 0x80011208-0x800116CC), none rollback-aware. Some return handles the game keeps in rewound memory (song player handle 0x80440AD0, bank ids), so they cannot simply be held with a fake result: menu rollback needs a rollback-aware layer for them (a song start / bank load re-made by a re-simulation must get the same handle without a second library call; one from a thrown-away frame must be undone), plus the menu-side loads (stage / fighter files, ELF modules), the Flash (Apt) front end, memory-card writes and the rematch / pause UI. Estimated as a multi-step project (several test rounds). Asked Robert which to do first.
  - **Robert chose menu rollback straight away (26 October, 14:20). Lab progress (branch `lab-26oct`):** (1) with rollback simply on in the menus (no rewinds) the audio thread crashed at 0x801E03FC (calls 801E4928 < 801E774C < 801E412C < thread entry 0x80277050) when the venue select went into the loading screen: **instant loads finished the music stream's disc reads too** - every disc read goes through EA's file-system thread 0x804099A0, requests come from the main thread 0x80432D10, the **EA audio thread 0x803E23C8** (streams; created at 0x801E4810) and a heap thread 0x806D8AE0 (menus); fix: `HookFsKick` (Start hook 0x80244408, r3 = request node) records the requesting thread (follow-up requests made by the file-system thread inherit the origin of the request it is serving, node *(0x804018D4)), `HookFsWait` finishes reads at once only for main-thread requests (fight: 1,576 main, 1,661 audio-thread waits; previously all were accelerated). (2) **The menus read the clock (mftb) directly** (Apt Flash player 0x801A3F24 / 0x801A3F80 -> 0x804404BC at 0x801A4010; timer +0x10 per frame at 0x8015BB4C -> 0x8044040C; ~20 inline mftb in the DOL, OSGetTime 0x80277A44): re-simulated frames read a later time. Fix: Dolphin gives the main thread a frame-based time base while rollback runs in the menus: `FFNYRollback::JitTimeBase()` (anchor = real time base at rollback start, identical on all machines in lockstep netplay; + 675,000 ticks per logic frame + 1 per read inside a frame, reset at every update start), called from Jit64 `mfspr` TL/TU (after the inline value, ABI call, result to PPCSTATE TL) and the interpreter when `g_virtual_tb` (decided at process start: switching it at run time with InvalidateICache crashed). Stress "words differ": thousands -> 7 lines by frame 2837. (3) **0x80011164 (music volume, 0x801D8C14(-1, vol), called by 0x80031C58) held** like the music cues: a re-simulated call crashed in the library's stream callbacks (0x800D0ADC..) at frame 2898. **Open next: under stress the title screen does not react to START** (fine without rewinds): probably the menus use button edges computed in tick() (0x800EC144, not re-run in a re-simulation; only the update from PRE is), while fights read the raw snapshot the rollback code injects. Also 0x804404DC: first runs store the measured dt (0x3C8887B4), re-simulations the fixed 1/60 (0x3C888889). Sound-library wrappers called per frame in the menus by the main thread: 0x80010DBC (3,473 calls), 0x800111C0 (2,948), 0x80010BD4 (257), 0x800107C8 (25); rare: 0x80010D04, 0x80010E5C, 0x80010EA0, 0x8001103C, 0x8001106C, 0x800110AC, 0x80011164, 0x80011390, 0x80011580, 0x80011634.
  - **Menu rollback, state at 16:15 (26 October; lab branch `lab-26oct` 89f00cc pushed; lab files in `ffny_tools/rollback/lab26/`):** (a) the "stuck title" under stress was the lab script: 0x803EA56C (the script's "screen counter") is written mostly by render / OS code (0x801F1C54, 0x80276xxx), not game state -> new script `blazin_pause_ctr.txt` waits on the logic counter 0x80440C24 (new bench op `mashge`, step log FFNY_BENCH_STEPLOG; note: `untilge` values are HEX). (c) **Stress run (STRESS=3, ALWAYS=2500) with v3 + the ctr script: title -> main menu -> fighter select -> venue select -> loading -> fight, only 2 "words differ" lines in the menus (frame 2503: 87 words, 2905: 2 words; e.g. 0x80653CC4-0x80653CF0, 0x8060E604 / 0x8060E624, 0x80440D14 / 0x80440D18, 0x8043FAD8) and 0 in the fight** (the fight's stress mismatches at 9055 are the script's momentum pokes, a lab artefact). **Next problem: crash at frame 3285 (main menu) in the heap code 0x80249818 (invalid writes to 0, 1, 2, ... = the 24 October quit crash signature), calls 80249470 < 801A315C < 8007418C < 80076240 < 8008470C < 800832C8 (front end)** - probably rbtag's fight-mode heap handling (SysHeap routing / deferred main-heap frees while W_FIGHTW = 1) meeting the front end's frees, or a front-end heap block freed in a thrown-away frame. Dolphin's crash handler skipped it and the run went on. Still to do after that: the remaining "words differ", the per-frame sound wrappers (0x80010DBC / 0x800111C0 / 0x80010BD4 / 0x800107C8: which return values the game keeps), song start / stop and bank loads under rewinds, memory-card writes, the rematch / pause UI, and then two Dolphins over UDP with lag through the menus. **To continue in a new session:** the lab needs the ISO (`Def Jam Mods/ffny_rebalanced-gamecube/modded-rollback_test.7z.001-009`, unpack with lab22/un7z.py), the GCI saves (`Documents/Dolphin Emulator/GC/USA/Card A`), `lab26/FFNYBench.inc` at /home/claude/lab/bench/ (GCPad.cpp includes it by that path) and branch `lab-26oct`, built as in lab26/README.txt.

- **What a real rollback build needs from this** (next steps): (1) DONE as `rbstate` (see above): the three DOL patches made boot-safe and put into `ffny_dolpatch.py` (`rbsndheap2` must call the game's memory init 0x8000ED14 when rbprobe is not in the DOL; `rbaudrng`'s private generator states must be initialised at boot or at match start - in the lab they are copies of the shared states); (2) Robert tests that build in normal Dolphin first (sound, menus, story mode / shops with the 512 KB sound heap taken from heap 0); (3) a compact per-frame snapshot instead of the lab's 48.7 MB brute-force copy (only a few hundred KB is written by the logic; Slippi-style range list); (4) the Dolphin side (input exchange, prediction, rollback trigger) from Slippi's sources; (5) sound handling during re-simulation (duplicate sounds).

**Patch `rbprobe` (research only, built + emulated today):** heap map + re-simulation cost test, see the patch table. Tool `python ffny.py rbprobe mem1.raw [mem1_later.raw]` lists every live block with its name and size (and changed bytes between two dumps, per name and for bss / data / cave / bigspace / untracked arena).

**Plan (phases):**
0. Measure (Robert, with `rbprobe`): (a) two MEM1 dumps a few frames apart in a 1v1 fight → which named heap blocks change per frame = size of a rollback state (Slippi copies ≈ 8 MB per frame, so up to ~10 MB is fine); (b) `rbextra=N` for N = 1 .. 7: does Dolphin keep 100 % speed and the game 60 fps while the fight runs (N + 1)× fast? That is the cost of re-simulating N frames per displayed frame (FFNY is heavier than Melee; Robert uses a CPU-clock override on netplay). If the emulated CPU runs out of time, Dolphin's CPU overclock during fights is an option.
1. Determinism of a partial state: a Dolphin build (or the rbprobe patch extended with an in-RAM copy of a small state) that every frame captures the chosen ranges, runs ahead, restores, re-runs the same inputs and compares fighter structs / RNG / match object (Slippi-style "rollback every frame" stress test). Adjust the range list until it never diverges.
2. Dolphin side: start from Slippi's Dolphin (GPL): keep `SlippiSavestate` (range copy), `SlippiNetplay` (ENet, input exchange, prediction, time sync), the EXI command device; drop Melee matchmaking / replays; direct connect by IP.
3. Game side (DOL patch in C with zig cc, like `movename`): at 0x80004CFC (`FUN_800ec144`): per logic frame send the local pad snapshot, take remote / predicted pads into the snapshot, ask Dolphin to capture the state; on a misprediction ask for a load and run the update loop again for each frame with the corrected inputs (render stays after the loop, so re-simulated frames are never drawn); raise the 5-update cap; agreed seed instead of the time base at match set-up; sounds: v1 = no new sounds while re-simulating already-shown frames, v2 = Slippi-style per-frame sound log (kill sounds of undone frames, avoid duplicates; FFNY sound paths: animtags dispatcher `FUN_80035394`, event `0x801F327C`, speech `FUN_801d29f0`, audio messages `FUN_800cd254`).
4. Menus: either (a) Dolphin's lockstep netplay for menus and rollback only in fights (switching modes inside Dolphin's netplay; risk: after rollback the two machines are no longer identical outside the game state - timers, audio - so lockstep after a fight may desync), or (b) Slippi's way: menus offline on each side and only the match settings (fighters / outfits / stage / rules) exchanged, then both boot the fight with the same seed (more game-side work: start a fight from the patch).
Other risks: Blazin' cinematics and the HUD-hidden state (part of the update, so fine if state is complete), crowd / announcer streaming, memory-card / story mode not needed, 3-4 player modes (more inputs, same design).

## Front-end menus (Apt) and the SELECT USER ID softlock (25 October 2026)

- **The front end is EA Apt** (a Flash-like player): every screen is `screens/screens.viv` -> `screens/<path>.big` (RefPack BIGF) with `<name>.apt` (movie: characters, frames, ActionScript bytecode; big-endian), `<name>.const` (constant table: "Apt constant file", +0x14 offset of the movie in the .apt, +0x18 entry count, +0x1C entries of 8 bytes: type 1 string (offset into .const), 4 register, 5 bool, 6 float, 7 int), `.o`, `.gsh`. Screen flow and transitions: `screens/feflow.xml` (screen name, `<transition><to>`, `<between>trans/empty</between>`, type). Tools (25 October, in `menu softlock fix/apt_tools`): `aptdis.py` (disassembler), `aptasm.py` (moves an action to the end of the .apt with inserted code; fixes branch offsets and function sizes), `aptpatch.py`, `refpack_c.py` (RefPack compressor, ~1.5 % bigger than EA's), `mkbig.py`.
  - .apt layout: movie at the .const's offset: type 9, signature, frame count, frames (8 bytes each: item count, pointer to item-pointer list), 0, character count, character-pointer table, 640, 480, ms per frame, ... Frame items: 1 action (+4 code), 2 frame label (+4 name, flags, frame), 3 PlaceObject (0x40 bytes: flags, depth, character, 6 floats matrix, colour, ?, ratio, **+0x34 name**, +0x38 clip depth, **+0x3C clip events**: count, pointer to 12-byte records flags / key / code), 5 background colour, 8 init action. Sprite (type 5) = frame count + frames like the movie. All pointers are file offsets, so new code / strings can be appended at the end and pointed to.
  - Bytecode = SWF action codes plus EA ones. Found from the code: 0x59 push 0, 0x5A push 1, 0x5B call function (pop result), 0x5C call function, 0x5D call method (pop), 0x5E call method, 0x70 push this, 0x72 "var = 0", 0x73 push true, 0x74 false, 0x75 null, 0x76 undefined, 0x88 constant pool (count, pointer to u32 indices into .const), 0x96 push data (count, pointer), 0x99 jump / 0x9D if true / 0xB8 if false (s32 offset from the end), 0x9B define function (name, argument count, argument-name pointers, body size, 8 bytes; body follows inline), 0xA1 push string (pointer), 0xA2 push pool[u8], 0xA3 pool[u16], 0xA4-0xA7 get / set variable / member by string pointer, 0xAE push variable pool[u8], 0xAF get member pool[u8], 0xB0-0xB3 call function / method by pool[u8] (pop / keep), 0xB4 float, 0xB5 s8, 0xB6 s16, 0xB7 s32, 0xB9 register. **Operands that are 32-bit pointers / offsets are aligned to 4 (zero padding after the opcode); 0xB4 / 0xB7 are not.**
- **Loader `screens/feloader.big` (= `_root`):** `transitionStart(next)` sets `g_nReadyCode` = 0, **`ActiveController(0)`**, loads the next screen into the spare clip and the `<between>` movie into clip2. `trans/empty` and the new screen each add 1 to `g_nReadyCode` when loaded (screens do it at the end of frame 0 and stop); the loader's `wait` clip waits for >= 2, then the transition runs and `transitionDone()` swaps the clips, gives focus to the new screen (or puts it under an open popup in `aFocusStack`), **`ActiveController(1)`** and plays the new screen. Key events (clip event 0x40) reach `capturedKeyEvent` only while `_root.g_bActiveController`. Popups: `openPopup` pushes the focus, `closePopup` pops it. **`beginPopupSequence()` saves `prevControllerState` = (g_bActiveController ? 1 : 0), enables the controllers and shows a dark shadow + fades the header; `endPopupSequence()` (from `MCOPComplete` / `MCOPCancel`, sent by the game when a memory-card step ends) calls `ActiveController(prevControllerState)`.** `MCOPCancel` also calls the focused screen's `keyBack`, `MCOPComplete` its `g_fMemCardCallback`.
- **Native side:** AS `FuncCall("Obj.Func(args)")` = getURL -> dispatcher **0x80074658** (looks up the name, calls the handler, then `_level0._root.ReturnValue(result or "NO_RETURN")` through **0x80075690**); game -> AS calls through **0x8015CCA0** (r3 = path such as `_level0._root.BEopenPopup`). Handler tables = name, 0x0000FFFF, function: Audio 0x8029F0CC, Loc, Battle 0x8029F1B0, Control 0x8029F2E8 (GetScreenInfo 0x8005A294, GetPrevScreenInfo 0x8005A2F8), Game 0x8029F370 (registration 0x8003B84C; e.g. SetMatchRules 0x8003DE78, ResetMatchData 0x80040E90, FEScreenLoaded 0x8003D824 = no-op), Controller 0x802A4010 (GetControllersEnabled 0x8005AF74 returns nothing). Tracing 0x80074658 + 0x8015CCA0 in the lab (`hooktrace`) prints every menu call by name - the best way to follow the menus.
- **The softlock (retail bug, Robert 25 October: "transition from SELECT USER ID back to CHOOSE MATCH TYPE never finishes, nothing responds until restart"):** `options/battleID` (Battle mode SELECT USER ID) plays frame 1 after `transitionDone` and in **frame 2** (when not coming back from a fighter select) runs `beginPopupSequence(); g_fMemCardCallback = "chooseMCDeviceCallback"; FuncCall("MemCard.ChooseMCDevice()")` (memory-card slot prompt `~MEMCARD_SLT_NGC`). Its `keyBack` with no player in (`aFocus[c] == 0`) calls `transitionStart("battle/cmtype.swf")` at once. **If B is handled in the 1-2 frames between `transitionDone` (controllers on) and frame 2**, the back transition switches the controllers off, then frame 2 still starts the prompt and saves **prevControllerState = 0**. The transition finishes (controllers on), the prompt is answered, the memory-card step ends -> `endPopupSequence` -> `ActiveController(0)` and nothing enables them again -> CHOOSE MATCH TYPE ignores every button. Lab (harness, from a CHOOSE MATCH TYPE savestate, A then B at frame K): K = 40 / 41 lock (A answers the prompt, then no menu call happens for any button), 36-39 and 42-44 fine (B is ignored during the transition, or cancels the prompt: `MCOPCancel` -> `battleID.keyBack` -> back, controllers restored to 1). `options/userID` (main menu USER ID) has the same pattern in frame 1 (popup sequence on entry) but did not lock in a scan of 5 frames.
- **Fix (no DOL change; two screen files, `menu softlock fix/`):** (A) `battleID.apt`: frame 2's action moved to the end of the file with `if (!_root.g_bActiveController) goto stop` in front (skips the prompt and the press-start setup when the screen is already being left). (B) `feloader.apt`: in `transitionDone`, right after `ActiveController(1)`: `if (popupSeqInProgress == true) prevControllerState = 1` (any popup sequence that began during a transition restores "on"). Lab: original locks at K 40 / 41; A only, B only and A + B: no lock at K 34-44 (B only: the prompt appears during the back transition, answering it re-enables the controllers); normal entry still shows the prompt; the bench menu path boot -> fight runs identically with A + B (same frame counts). In the lab ISO the two files were put into the unused PS2 / Xbox poster space of screens.viv; for Robert: `python ffny_tools\ffny_viv.py put screens.viv screens_new.viv screens/options/battleID.big=battleID.big screens/feloader.big=feloader.big` (checked here: entries replaced, data identical). Built from the rollback test ISO's screens.viv. **Not yet tested by Robert.**
